# Astra code review — 2026-10-31

**Completed static review of the inventoried non-planner scope.** Five Astra/high agents inspect source; two additional Astra/high agents reconcile curated concerns. Baseline: `4fafde4d4df17a388f551ccef6efd923b32afe46`. **Planner is excluded at the user's request:** its implementation plan is stalled by Claude Code quotas. Core execution-plan schemas, DAGs, amendments, pressure, verification, existing dashboard and shared runtime remain included.

This report contains **227 numbered findings**, including supplements merged into their original IDs. Every finding retains its evidence, repair guidance, affected consumers, safeguards and future validation. Severity is the reviewer's static assessment: CRITICAL means a traced reachable behavioral defect, MAJOR substantive architecture/maintenance debt, MINOR bounded cleanup. No builds, tests, browser sessions or fault-injection reproductions were run. No production fixes are included. Recheck cited lines against the baseline before implementation.

**Coverage evidence:** 3762 of 3762 paths recorded: 1633 full-source, 1298 test, 690 fixture, 141 explicitly excluded; 0 inventory gaps. These categories are separate; outlines and directory mapping do not count as source review. Inventories, range ledgers, original reviewer reports and concern dispositions are retained in [the supporting audit files](astra-review-work/). Generated/binary/dependency artifacts and planner-only paths have individual exclusion reasons. Under the quota closeout, 55 domain-reference skill documents were excluded from full-body review; 15 received targeted adapter-section inspection. Existing untracked user scratch files are outside the tracked-code inventory.

**Concerns reconciliation is partial.** The runtime-concerns pass accounts for 256 claim groups, with 19 distinct additional findings after deduplication and optional-policy separation. The data/verification pass records 589 rows: 100 source-traced, 171 traced but unresolved, **58 unreviewed and 2 partially traced**, 203 planner-excluded and 55 metadata rows; it contributes 14 distinct findings. Accepted limits, optional proposals and live-platform questions are not treated as established bugs. The remaining 60 claims were deliberately left explicit at quota closeout. See [CON-A reconciliation](astra-review-work/concerns-a-reconciliation-1.tsv), [CON-B closure](astra-review-work/concerns-b-closure.md), and [targeted independent QA](astra-review-work/concerns-a-validation.md).

**Architecture assessment:** the recurring problem is ownership of policy and evidence across boundaries. Existing shared facilities already cover many proposed repairs: safe file readers, atomic publication, typed execution metadata, exact session deltas and verification sinks. Preserve their distinct confinement, compatibility and recovery guarantees; consolidating code must not erase policy differences. Fix proven behavior first, then move shared ownership, then remove redundant wrappers and misleading comments. The report proposes focused changes, not a wholesale rewrite.

| Shared work package | Findings to coordinate | Implementation constraint |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Safe file acquisition and bounded reads | VER-09, CTX-12, CTX-27, INT-E26, RT-12, RT-25 | Reuse verified readers; preserve no-follow, regular-file, byte-cap and error semantics. |
| Atomic publication and owned state transitions | RT-02, RT-03, RT-13, RT-16, CTX-03, CTX-16, VER-08, VER-17 | Separate concurrency ownership from crash consistency; retain exact attempt identities. |
| Verification policy and evidence | VER-01, VER-02, VER-03, VER-13, VER-20, VER-21, INT-E25 | Keep exact selection, complete output, immutable reviewed identity and declared budgets. |
| Shared read models and typed actions | INT-06, INT-08, INT-E06, DASH-05, CTX-23, CTX-24 | Move domain meaning out of presentation strings and preserve wire compatibility. |
| Parsing and command interpretation | VER-04, VER-18, INT-04, INT-E21, RT-11, RT-23, RT-24 | Share the existing parser appropriate to the contract; preserve intentional grammar differences. |
| Lifecycle and process ownership | RT-01, RT-03, RT-27, INT-E05, INT-E07, CON-B-01 | Bound shutdown and retain verified process/attempt identity before destructive actions. |

## Runtime, ownership and persistence

<a id="rt-01"></a>**RT-01 — Daemon shutdown can wait forever on a client that continues to ping**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** architecture and lifecycle ownership. **Established defect.** <br><br> **Locations and symbols:** <br><br> • `loom/src/daemon/server/lifecycle.rs:186–222`, `DaemonServer::run_server`: exits the accept loop after shutdown, then immediately drops the client pool. <br> • `loom/src/daemon/server/pool.rs:47–52`, `WorkerPool::drop`: closes the submission channel and unconditionally joins every worker with no shutdown or deadline contract. <br> • `loom/src/daemon/server/client.rs:196–203,236–257`, `receive_request` / `handle_client_connection`: creates a fresh five-second read deadline for every request and loops without checking `shutdown_flag`. <br> • `loom/src/daemon/server/client_dispatch.rs:18–35`, `ClientDispatch::serve`: Ping and subscriptions return `true`; Stop acknowledges, sets shutdown, and only stops its own connection. <br> • `loom/src/commands/stop.rs:72–75,128–141`: `loom stop` reports success after acknowledgement; destructive-state callers subsequently wait at most thirty seconds for the singleton to disappear. <br><br> **Trigger and evidence:** Client A holds an authenticated connection and sends Ping more frequently than every five seconds. Client B issues authorized Stop. B receives `Ok`; the accept loop ends; dropping the pool joins A's handler. A continues receiving Pong because neither its outer loop nor Ping dispatch consults shutdown. Each request resets the read deadline, so the join has no upper bound. This does not require a stalled filesystem or an invalid request. Queued connections are also drained after shutdown because the pool closes only the sender. <br><br> **Consequence:** The daemon keeps its singleton lock and control files indefinitely after acknowledging shutdown. `ensure_daemon_stopped` refuses cleanup after its separate thirty-second deadline. The five-second thread joins later in `run_server` never execute, so they do not bound this path. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Give the existing connection loop a cooperative shutdown condition before accepting another request, and prevent queued connections from beginning ordinary service after shutdown. Retain the per-request absolute read deadline to bound the one read already underway. Make pool shutdown explicit enough that its lifecycle contract is visible at `run_server`; account for queued work and any stage-control handler still executing. Do not merely detach client workers and release the singleton while they can still mutate stage files. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** `DaemonServer::stop`, all Ping/subscription connections, `ensure_daemon_stopped` used before state cleanup, daemon pool tests, and client dispatch tests. Existing authorization and byte-budget admission must remain intact. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** Start a real handler on a socket pair, repeatedly Ping on connection A, authorize Stop through connection B, and assert A stops receiving successful service and pool shutdown completes within the documented bounded window. Include a queued connection and an idle partial request. Keep `pool.rs:84–110` queue-saturation behavior for ordinary operation; that existing test releases its blocked job before dropping the pool and therefore does not cover shutdown cancellation.<br><br>
<a id="rt-02"></a>**RT-02 — Manual reset erases ownership established after its liveness check**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** architecture and DRY. **Established interleaving defect; repeated retirement implementations are its architectural context.** <br><br> **Locations and symbols:** <br><br> • `loom/src/commands/stage/state/loop_recovery/mod.rs:71–106`, `reset_with`: loads the stage, discovers/checks or retires agents, and then applies reset to whatever record is current without matching the inspected owner or attempt. <br> • `loom/src/commands/stage/state.rs:358–383`, `apply_reset`: unconditionally clears status, attempt timing, retry counters, and `stage.session`. <br> • `loom/src/commands/stage/state.rs:239–250`, `reset_and_cancel`: subsequently cancels a host job by stage ID, again without tying cancellation to the attempt inspected by reset. <br> • `loom/src/orchestrator/core/stage_executor.rs:168–209`, `start_stage`, and `core/stage_spawn.rs:85–114,148–176`, `mark_agent_executing`: scheduler can independently write a new session assignment and start its process. <br> • Existing daemon retirement counterpart: `loom/src/orchestrator/core/event_handler/stage_takedown.rs:153–200,281–367`. <br><br> **Trigger and evidence:** A queued stage has no live agent. Reset reads it and its live-agent scan returns empty. Before reset takes the stage update lock, the scheduler writes a session record, marks the stage Executing, assigns that session, and launches it. Reset then acquires the lock and runs `apply_reset(current)` without comparing `current.session`, status, or attempt identity with the inspected snapshot. It reports success and clears ownership of an agent it never checked or killed. The same gap exists after a `--kill-session` retirement finishes and before reset commits. A fresh host job can similarly become the target of the later stage-ID-only cancellation. <br><br> **Consequence:** The stage becomes WaitingForDeps with no assigned session while an agent is alive. Subsequent adoption may repair some cases, but the reset has already violated its advertised refusal/retirement contract and discarded the new attempt's metadata. Do not overstate this as guaranteed duplicate spawning: the scheduler does have adoption guards. The confirmed fault is erasing the new owner's state and potentially cancelling its new job. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Add a reset reservation/claim under the stage lock before doing slow liveness and teardown work. It must prevent scheduler admission of a new attempt, record the inspected session/attempt, and ensure final reset plus job cancellation apply only to that claim. On refusal, preserve current ownership and a usable recovery state. A short-term locked compare-and-refuse at final mutation should reject a changed owner/attempt; complete serialization also needs the scheduler to respect the reservation, especially the scan-to-spawn window. Do not hold the shared stages-directory flock during a six-second process wait. <br><br> **Concrete boundary and migration:** Extract shared discovery/retirement mechanics from the reset and daemon paths into a runtime session-retirement helper taking explicit target sessions and policy. Keep the policies distinct: reset excludes adjudicators but may stop a merge resolver; ordinary daemon stage retirement excludes both adjudicators and resolvers. First add the reset claim and race tests, then reuse only identity checking, bounded confirmation, and exact terminal recording. Preserve orphan PID evidence, unknown-identity refusal, and the dispute-before-stage lock order. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** CLI reset, `ResetRuntime` fakes, scheduler `start_stage` / `mark_agent_executing`, host-job cancellation keyed to the retired attempt, and daemon retirement callers if helper extraction follows. `commands/stage/state_tests.rs:143–229,335–350` covers survivors, unknown identity, delayed exit and successful reset; it does not introduce a new owner between inspection and reset write. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** Inject a scheduler assignment after the reset scan and before final mutation; assert reset refuses or exclusively owns the transition, leaving the new session, attempt timing, and job untouched. Repeat while `--kill-session` is waiting on an old process. Preserve existing adjudicator exclusion, resolver handling, orphan recovery, identity-missing refusal, and slow-exit tests.<br><br>
<a id="rt-03"></a>**RT-03 — Resolver discovery declares the launch window dead because its session is saved after spawn**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** architecture and DRY. **Established interleaving and persistence-failure defects.** <br><br> **Locations and symbols:** <br><br> • `loom/src/commands/stage/merge_resolver.rs:70–83,134–152`, `spawn_merge_resolver` / `spawn_resolver_session`: checks for an existing resolver, writes its signal, spawns, then saves its session; no per-stage spawn claim spans these operations. <br> • `loom/src/orchestrator/core/merge_handler/resolver_spawn.rs:280–300`, `spawn_merge_resolution_session`: same signal → launch → save order; session-save failure is only a warning. <br> • `loom/src/orchestrator/core/merge_handler/resolver_stop.rs:64–120`, `launch_resolver` / `settle_resolver_spawn`: handles failed backend launch and attempt accounting, but does not create a session record before launch. <br> • `loom/src/orchestrator/signals/merge.rs:61–99`, `find_live_merge_session_for_stage`: loads the record, reduces it to optional PID, treats missing record/PID as dead, and removes the signal. <br> • `loom/src/orchestrator/terminal/tmux/backend.rs:66–94`: launches the process and waits for PID before returning the running session; `native/launch.rs:327–372` prepares identity and wrapper but does not persist a session. <br> • Contrast the already implemented stage-worker write-ahead contract in `core/session_lifecycle.rs:22–64` and `core/stage_spawn.rs:85–114`. <br><br> **Trigger and evidence:** With no daemon, CLI caller A writes a merge signal and enters backend launch, before the session record exists. Caller B retries the same stage and calls resolver discovery. B reads A's valid signal, finds no session record, deletes A's signal, and proceeds to launch another resolver. The Git merge lock does not cover the later spawn path. A related sequence occurs when launch succeeds but session saving fails: a subsequent discovery again declares the live resolver stale. On daemon restart the in-memory handle is also lost. The scanner's raw `is_process_alive(pid)` additionally bypasses the backend's verified process identity, though the missing-record launch window alone proves this finding. <br><br> **Consequence:** Two writers can run in the same stage worktree, or a live resolver loses the signal it needs and becomes invisible to normal signal/session discovery. Existing PID-orphan recovery is a later fallback, not a reservation protecting this admission path. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Put resolver admission and durable launch state behind one shared runtime operation used by CLI and daemon. Acquire a per-stage resolver reservation, write a correctly attributed `Spawning` record with backend and tracking key before signal/process launch, then publish the running PID using a fresh-record update. Discovery must distinguish an active launch reservation from verified death; simply adding a PID-less record is insufficient because the current scanner also removes that signal. Finalize or retire the reservation on each failure, preserving records/evidence whenever a process may survive. <br><br> **Concrete migration:** First introduce the shared reservation/write-ahead primitive and a discovery outcome that represents launching or uncertain liveness. Port the daemon and CLI paths together. Reuse existing backend dispatch and `ReservedAttempt` budget semantics instead of duplicating capsule setup or identity teardown. Preserve daemon-only automatic retry caps and explicit operator retry behavior; shared launch mechanics do not imply identical scheduling policy. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** CLI resolver spawn from progressive completion/retry, daemon resolver spawn and restart recovery, merge signal discovery, session registry/attach/status, resolver failure/attempt tests, and `signals/merge_liveness_tests.rs:126–134`. The latter currently asserts that every readable signal with no record is stale and must be narrowed to evidence of a finished/abandoned launch. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** Pause the first launch after signal publication and attempt a second launch from the other surface; exactly one backend spawn must occur, and the first signal must remain. Inject session publication failure after process creation and verify discovery refuses another writer until identity-based teardown or recovery proves it safe. Simulate restart with a launch reservation. Test true stale-signal cleanup and corrupt-record refusal separately. <br><br> <a id="con-a-06"></a>**Reconciled evidence from CON-A-06: CON-A-06 — duplicate evidence folded into RT-03: recycled resolver PID.** <br><br> Lenses: architecture, correctness. A saved resolver's PID reused by another live process makes its signal look current and holds the stage indefinitely. Evidence: `loom/src/orchestrator/signals/merge.rs:83-87` reduces the record to `session.pid` and calls `is_process_alive`, discarding session identity. Canonical owner: RT-03. Reuse the session/process identity owner with start time and explicit uncertain/dead semantics; preserve fail-closed handling of unreadable records. Consumers: resolver discovery, CLI spawn and daemon merge loop. Keep a separate acceptance case for a live unrelated process with the old recorded PID/start-time mismatch, alongside RT-03's reservation-before-record case. Do not count this as another finding.<br><br>
<a id="rt-04"></a>**RT-04 — Three merge application paths independently translate the same result into lifecycle state**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** architecture, DRY, redundant code. **Established policy divergence; consolidation recommendation.** <br><br> **Locations and symbols:** <br><br> • Daemon: `loom/src/orchestrator/core/merge_handler/landing.rs:81–118`, `land_stage_merge`; `auto_merge_outcome.rs:82–144`, `apply_auto_merge_outcome` / `settle_auto_merge_error`. <br> • Manual retry: `loom/src/commands/stage/merge/landing.rs:56–102`, `attempt_retried_merge`, and `next_step.rs:99–126`, `report_merge_error`. <br> • Progressive completion: `loom/src/commands/stage/progressive_complete.rs:83–107`, `attempt_progressive_merge`, and `progressive_complete/outcome.rs:21–31,57–80,85–150`. <br> • Shared lower-level engine already exists: `git::merge::merge_stage`; shared cleanup exists in `orchestrator::merge_lifecycle`. These are reuse boundaries to preserve, not replace. <br><br> **Evidence:** The daemon distinguishes typed `CommitTreeError` signing failures and moves the stage to NeedsHumanReview (`landing.rs:110–114`, `auto_merge_outcome.rs:139–142`). Progressive completion routes every merge error through `block_failed_merge`, records an infrastructure failure, and marks MergeBlocked. Manual retry handles an `Err` by printing `report_merge_error` and returning `Ok(())` (`merge/landing.rs:98–100`), without persisting that error. Thus the same Git failure has three different state/exit outcomes selected by the entry point. Conflict, typed block, review hold, ancestry proof, completion marking, cleanup and reporting are likewise spread among these owners. <br><br> **Consequence:** A fix for signing or finalization policy must be rediscovered and copied across three paths. A manual command can exit successfully after reporting an actual merge error; progressive completion can put an operator-only signing problem back into automatic blocked-merge handling. The differing surface contracts are visible in source, so the maintenance concern does not depend on a speculative future feature. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Extract a typed merge application policy into a runtime/stage-service module below CLI adapters. Have it classify the engine outcome/error and apply owned stage deltas with the expected completion proof/attempt, returning a typed disposition. CLI and daemon retain presentation, scheduling, and their deliberately different reviewed/enforced authorization inputs. Decide and document the CLI exit contract; propagate actual execution failures unless an explicit non-error disposition is the chosen public contract. <br><br> **Migration sequence:** (1) table-test shared classification of success, already-up-to-date, conflict, typed block, held gate, signing error, and other error; (2) move the fresh-record mutation/proof checks into the shared service; (3) migrate manual retry and progressive completion, then daemon adapters; (4) delete duplicate policy branches after equivalent acceptance. Avoid creating another broad facade that leaves the decisions in every caller. <br><br> **Behavioral constraints:** Keep the existing target hold, merge lock, ownership checks, control-path checks, original completion-commit ancestry, reviewed landing's restricted bypass, stage-currentness checks, stash/checkout preservation, and deferred cleanup. Never collapse a typed block or unknown proof into generic success, and never infer authority from the current HEAD. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** All three entry paths above, human-review force completion using progressive `Landing::Reviewed`, automatic retries/resolver handback, CLI output/exit tests, and existing `landing_signing_tests.rs`, `landing_tests.rs`, progressive outcome and retry tests. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** Feed the same signing failure and every result variant through all three adapters and assert consistent durable disposition where authority inputs match. Check manual retry's process exit, and a concurrent completion-proof change between merge and persistence. Verify existing Git checkout/stash and target-guard tests are unchanged by the extraction. Related to RT-03 only at resolver dispatch; land the launch fix separately.<br><br>
<a id="rt-05"></a>**RT-05 — Persistence comments promise atomicity and a single write path that the code does not provide**<br><br>**Severity:** MINOR. **Lens:** invalid/overly verbose comments. **Established documentation defects.** <br><br> **A. Cross-file rollback claim:** `loom/src/orchestrator/core/session_lifecycle.rs:146–148` says, “This deletes that record and clears `stage.session` in the SAME locked update … so the two can never be observed apart.” In fact `block_and_undo_session` removes the session file at line 166 before calling `update_stage` at line 171. Another reader can observe the gap; a failed stage write leaves it permanent. Separate session/stage directories also use different locks. Readers implementing recovery must not rely on the advertised cross-file transaction. <br><br> **Repair:** State the real ordering and failure behavior succinctly. If behavior is tightened, unlink the stage under a current-owner check before deleting its now-unreferenced session record; do not claim that a directory lock makes two separate files crash-atomic. Preserve the distinction between pre-spawn cleanup and a possibly live session. A source/doc correction needs no ceremonial test; a reordered rollback needs a failure-injection test proving a failed stage update retains discoverable ownership evidence. This is adjacent to RT-02/RT-03 but a different helper. <br><br> **B. Shutdown bound claim:** `loom/src/commands/stop.rs:121–127` justifies thirty seconds by “join five threads sequentially with a 5s timeout each.” It omits the unbounded `drop(client_pool)` preceding those joins (`daemon/server/lifecycle.rs:222`). Keep comments about non-obvious identity/refusal and crash-recovery constraints; remove historical incident prose only where the shorter current contract carries the same information. <br><br> **Supplement: Follow-up to RT-05 — Lifecycle comments should describe current publication order.** <br><br> `orchestrator/session_registry.rs` still motivates orphan discovery with the claim that the session record is written after spawn. Describe remaining gaps precisely: the post-launch PID/status update may fail, old state can lack a record, and compatibility recovery still matters. Do not describe a missing-record window as the normal current spawn protocol. <br><br> The extraction-history prose in `core/session_lifecycle.rs` and `orphan_candidates.rs` adds little to the ownership contract. Retain the write-ahead ordering and identity constraints, replace file-move/size history with the current reason each boundary exists. `orchestrator/tick.rs`'s daemon-is-two-threads description should account for the current supervisor and host-job architecture rather than imply that daemon lifetime is independent of orchestrator termination; daemon supervision now shuts down on an orchestrator error or panic. <br><br> **Supplement: RT-05 comment followup from full merge and monitor coverage.** <br><br> • `auto_merge.rs`/`core/merge_handler/auto_merge_outcome.rs` still describe deriving missing `completed_commit` from the branch head. State the actual ownership/order. <br> • `monitor/core.rs101–104` says absence of a liveness service falls back to host PID; `Handlers::check_session_alive` returns `Ok(None)` instead. `monitor/detection.rs276–280` claims nothing shells out during heartbeat detection, but it calls liveness and parked-stage git probes. Keep the distinction between cheap timestamp arithmetic and subsequent probes explicit. <br> • `monitor/events.rs` calls SessionHung advisory and says backstop is a fixed multiplier, while recovery and the configured window clamp change both claims. `monitor/parked.rs1–39`, `monitor/session_events.rs22–43,109–125`, and `hung_latch.rs1–25` mix useful current invariants with dated incident narratives. <a id="rt-06"></a>**RT-06 — Two locked-update implementations duplicate the lock/read/publish algorithm**<br><br>**Severity:** MINOR. **Lenses:** DRY and redundant code. **Optional simplification with verified reuse contract.** <br><br> **Locations:** `loom/src/fs/locking.rs:192–227`, `locked_read_modify_write`, and `:235–258`, `locked_update`. <br><br> Both acquire the same exclusive parent-directory lock, read the same UTF-8 contents, treat NotFound as empty, invoke a closure, and atomically replace the file. The meaningful difference is whether the closure returns `String` or `Result<String>`. The infallible version can delegate to `locked_update(path, |content| Ok(modify(content)))`, retaining its public API. This removes one implementation of a concurrency-sensitive contract without adding an abstraction or dependency. <br><br> **Consumers:** `loom/src/fs/knowledge/dir.rs:200,241` and `fs/locking/tests.rs:121,132,205`; the fallible function also supports exact session updates and other store mutations. Keep both public signatures, NotFound creation, closure-at-most-once, lock scope and atomic publication. Error context wording can be retained at the wrapper if callers require it; do not nest two directory locks. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** Existing locking tests for creation, concurrent append/update, and failed fallible closure leaving contents intact suffice. Inspect/test the refactored primitive once; no new mirror-of-implementation test is necessary. Do not merge the unrelated descriptor-relative no-follow replacement implementation into this path: `fs/safe_replace.rs` provides distinct confinement/mode/durability behavior.<br><br>
<a id="rt-07"></a>**RT-07 — Lifecycle lock claims lack ownership cleanup on failed acquisition**<br><br>**Severity: MAJOR. Lenses: resource ownership, non-idiomatic cleanup.** `loom/src/subagent_lifecycle/lock.rs:21–40` creates a claim before acquiring the directory lock. `recover_abandoned(&path)?` at line 29 returns directly on error, bypassing both explicit `remove_claim` calls. `create_claim:90–94` similarly leaves a newly created file on write/sync failure. Normal contention can trigger the first path: the owner removes its directory after the contender receives `AlreadyExists`, before `recover_abandoned:104` calls `symlink_metadata`. The contender then fails with `NotFound` instead of retrying and leaves its claim behind. An unreadable/malformed owner also leaves a claim each attempt. Claim names have sixteen nonce slots per PID/second, and old seconds are never swept. <br><br> The immediate consumer is `append::append_all_locked` (`append.rs:49`), used for Codex lifecycle reconciliation. The append fails before recording evidence; later reconciliation may retry, but these errors needlessly lose that attempt and accumulate files. The acquired `JournalLock` has a Drop implementation, while the acquisition resources do not. This is a concrete ownership gap rather than a request to replace the shared locking protocol. <br><br> **Repair:** introduce a local claim guard immediately after successful exclusive file creation. Its Drop removes only that claim on every exit, including write/sync and abandoned-lock errors; transfer or discharge ownership after publishing the owner hard link. Treat disappearance of the lock/owner during contention as a retryable observation. Preserve the shell-compatible mkdir/owner-hard-link protocol, ownership checks, bounded retry, and refusal to reclaim a live or uncertain owner. Do not turn arbitrary malformed-owner errors into permission to steal a lock. <br><br> **Validation:** deterministic contention test removes the old directory between failed mkdir and recovery stat, and expects a retry with no leaked claim. Error-path tests cover malformed owner and claim write/sync failure; assert the claim directory is unchanged after refusal. Existing append/replay tests exercise successful locking and journal semantics, but the search across `subagent_lifecycle` and integration tests found no targeted acquisition cleanup test. No tests executed.<br><br>

Coordinate with VER09/CTX12: those concern separate read helpers; this finding owns the shared write helper.<br><br>

<a id="rt-18"></a>**RT-18 — CRITICAL — Inbox ledger tolerates a torn suffix without repairing it before the next append**<br><br>**Lenses:** recovery ownership and inconsistent read/write contracts. `fs/inbox/ledger.rs:126-151` ignores malformed JSON on the final line. `append_ledger:57-68` then appends a new JSON record directly, without removing that torn suffix. `orchestrator/core/inbox_drain/session_pass.rs:226-252` reads this tolerant view and immediately appends `UnknownAfterRestart` for unfinished Applying records; ordinary settlement also appends Applying/outcome pairs (`:153-173`). <a id="rt-27"></a>**RT-27 — A subscription has several independent writers to one framed stream**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** architecture, concurrency ownership, excessive work. <br><br> **Evidence:** `daemon/server/client.rs:258-284`, subscribe, clones the stream, inserts it under the subscriber-list mutex, releases that mutex and then writes Response::Ok through the original stream. The broadcaster may immediately acquire the list and write a response through the published clone (`broadcast.rs:290-313`). `wire.rs:165-180` emits the four-byte length and JSON body in separate write_all calls. Interleaving `length(A), length(B), body(B), body(A)` corrupts the frame. This needs only an ordinary subscription coinciding with a broadcast. `client_dispatch.rs:20-24` also keeps the request loop open, permitting Ping responses or subscriptions to both lists on one connection; status and log broadcasters hold different mutexes, so moving the initial acknowledgement alone does not solve all writers. <br><br> **Consequence:** valid clients intermittently see framing/JSON errors and disconnect. A broadcast can also precede the expected subscription acknowledgement. Each retained subscriber serializes the same response again after frame_overflow already serialized it once, so the present ownership also repeats avoidable allocation/work for up to 32 subscribers per list. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** give each connection one response writer/serialization owner. A shared per-connection writer lock around an entire preencoded frame is sufficient if current request-loop behavior must remain; both subscription lists must reference that same owner. Establish acknowledgement-before-publication under the connection protocol, with explicit failure cleanup. Alternatively, if subscription is a terminal connection mode, transfer ownership after acknowledgement and reject later request/multiple-channel use deliberately; verify all clients before changing that contract. Encode a bounded response once per broadcast and share immutable bytes; keep independent slow-peer eviction. Do not hold a global subscriber-list lock to serialize unrelated connections longer than necessary. <br><br> **Consumers/constraints:** status/TUI/web and log subscriptions, Ping/Unsubscribe handling, client lifecycle and broadcast worker shutdown. Preserve frame caps, credential preface policy, byte budget, subscriber cap, overflow error notice and write timeout. Closing one clone is not cancellation of every socket owner; coordinate with RT-01's shutdown design. <br><br> **Acceptance:** deterministic writer barrier after the first frame length proves that subscription acknowledgement and a simultaneous broadcast remain complete ordered frames. Exercise Ping while subscribed and a connection subscribed to both channels (or its explicit rejection), two concurrent broadcasts, slow-reader eviction and shutdown. Existing wire tests cover isolated framing; broadcast tests cover retention only, so neither currently exercises this race. Count serialization once per broadcast after the repair.<br><br>
<a id="rt-28"></a>**RT-28 — RPC clients advertise a bounded exchange but bound only individual reads**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** boundary ownership, duplicated deadline implementation, inaccurate comments. <br><br> **Evidence:** `daemon/rpc.rs:169-192` uses blocking UnixStream::connect; exchange (`:215-222`) sets a read timeout and performs write_message without a write timeout. `daemon/server/shutdown.rs:29-40` repeats the same shape with five seconds. SO_RCVTIMEO applies separately to each read, while wire read_exact can make repeated reads: a peer delivering bytes just before each timeout can exceed the advertised whole-request limit. A saturated accept backlog can delay connect before either timeout is set, and a connected peer that stops reading can delay a request write. The server already has a monotonic DeadlineReader (`server/admission.rs:9-38`) with an explicit slow-drip regression, but clients cannot reuse it through their present ownership. <br><br> **Repair:** put bounded Unix-stream exchange below both RPC and stop clients. Carry one monotonic deadline across nonblocking connect/poll, bounded writes and framed reads; use the remaining duration on each operation. Preserve the semantic distinctions among NotListening, Unreachable and an established connection failure, and keep daemon-stop proof consumption semantics explicit when timeout leaves execution uncertain. Reuse/adapt the existing deadline logic rather than introducing another relative-timeout loop. This is independent of RT-01: even a client that never asks the daemon to stop needs a bounded exchange. <br><br> **Consumers/constraints:** stage one-shot RPC, observer fallback, operator stop and callers of send_request/try_send_request. Preserve masked-socket classification, long-path canonicalization and no fallback after a live daemon answered/refused. Avoid silently retrying a mutating request after an ambiguous timeout. <br><br> **Acceptance:** a peer that accepts but does not read, one that drips a response below the per-read timeout and an accept backlog that cannot progress all return within a small bounded tolerance of the configured deadline. Ordinary complete requests work; a server refusal stays authoritative; timeout never routes around the daemon.<br><br>

<a id="rt-30"></a>**RT-30 — Failed branch lookup becomes affirmative merge attribution**<br><br>**Severity: CRITICAL. Preserve BaseConflict handling, genuine operator/main-checkout merge recovery, and the existing prohibition on reopening final merged stages. The comment claiming that lookup failure is safe because reconciliation runs before cleanup should describe the evidence requirement instead: historical records and independent branch deletion exist regardless of that ordering. <br><br> **Consumers/validation:** startup reconciliation, scheduling reports and all callers of the attribution result must retain the distinction between unattributed and stage-owned merges. <a id="rt-31"></a>**RT-31 — Session inventory discards uncertainty at the pre-spawn ownership boundary**<br><br>**Severity: CRITICAL. Lenses: architecture, DRY, invalid comments.** <br><br> `orchestrator/session_registry.rs:128–176` returns an empty live-session list for any session-directory read error, skips individual entry/read/parse failures, and logs then discards liveness-probe errors. Display and best-effort discovery can consume partial results; spawning, replacement and destructive retirement must defer on uncertainty affecting ownership. Resolve the terminal probe once per inventory pass and retain the existing PID-only fallback when a terminal backend cannot be built. Reuse bounded, descriptor-validated reads and canonical filename/record identity checks. Do not turn an ordinary confirmed-dead process into an indefinite error, and distinguish an intentionally absent initial directory from an unreadable existing state directory. <br><br> **Consumers/validation:** migrate pre-spawn adoption and recovery guards first, then the in-progress handoff scan and repeated per-type scans. Preserve Stage/Contract preference and established multi-session adoption rules. Add cases with only a malformed live-worker record, an unreadable existing directory, and a liveness probe error: no replacement launch or destructive cleanup may occur. Keep positive cases for confirmed dead sessions, empty initialized inventory and terminal-unavailable PID fallback. Existing malformed-record tests also include a healthy record and establish tolerant listing, not safe absence evidence. <br><br> **Supplement: RT-31 follow-up — Two retirement inventories disagree about an absent assigned record.** <br><br> `core/event_handler/stage_takedown.rs::stage_agents` calls `takedown_evidence::include_assigned_session`, which rejects a missing or wrong-stage exact record. `retirable_agents` repeats the same in-memory-plus-persisted assembly but silently ignores a wrong-stage assigned record and only warns when the record is missing. `retire_stage_agents` can then see no survivors and release the assignment. This path serves intentional block and dispute retirement, while the stronger helper serves handoff. Unify inventory acquisition and explicit missing-identity policy under RT-31; do not erase the distinction between intentionally excluded Merge/Adjudication sessions and an unknown assigned worker. Add missing-assigned-record cases for block and dispute retirement alongside the existing handoff cases. <br><br> **Supplement: RT-31 additional consumers and constraints.** <br><br> `MergeLifecycle::deferral_reason` (`orchestrator/merge_lifecycle.rs164–183`) intends to defer destructive cleanup if session inventory fails, but its `live_sessions_for_stage` dependency currently swallows those failures. Include this consumer in strict inventory migration and test unreadable/malformed live-session records before deletion. Verification's full command review confirms `commands/stage/skip_retry.rs349–379` independently returns false on session read/parse/backend/probe failures. That false bypasses the normal retry guard at lines49–68, contrary to its claim that `--force` already guards parallel sessions. Route it through the same strict liveness result: unknown refuses ordinary retry, explicit force retains its documented override, and confirmed missing/dead remains allowed. <br><br> **Supplement: Cross-territory addition to RT-31 — retry liveness errors.** <br><br> `commands/stage/skip_retry.rs:349–379` treats failed session reads/parsing/backend construction and liveness probes as false. The caller at49–68 therefore skips its live-session refusal. The comment that the worst case is a parallel session “which `--force` already guards” is incorrect: false bypasses that guard without `--force`. Runtime owns RT-31 and was sent this consumer: use the shared strict liveness result and canonical loader, refuse unknown/error for ordinary retry, preserve confirmed missing/dead and the explicitly authorized force override. Cover the failed-probe/read/backend case with a still-live session. This is a shared-owner repair, not another liveness abstraction.<br><br>
<a id="rt-32"></a>**RT-32 — File-size pressure moved instance-owned retry state into a global registry**<br><br>**Severity: MAJOR. Lenses: architecture, excessive/non-idiomatic implementation, invalid comments.** <br><br> `orchestrator/core/host_jobs.rs:34–41` puts pending host-job result applications in `static FAILED_APPLIES: Mutex<BTreeMap<PathBuf, Vec<FailedApply>>>`. The stated reason is that `Orchestrator` is at its file-size limit and “cannot take another field.” The runner is already instance-owned (`core/orchestrator.rs`, `host_jobs` field), while the runner's pending result applications are process-global and recovered by lexical work-directory path. <br><br> This introduces a mutex, poison recovery, path-keyed registry and two ownership lifetimes for one sequential tick workflow. `stop_host_jobs` does not retire the registry entry. A subsequent orchestrator for the same lexical path can inherit the old instance's pending records; a path alias gets a different list for the same state root. Ordinary daemon shutdown ends the process, so this is primarily an ownership/maintainability defect, not a claim that every daemon restart replays global memory. <br><br> **Repair:** place the runner and pending applications in an instance-owned `HostJobState` defined in `host_jobs.rs`; `Orchestrator` then needs one field rather than the current runner field. Make retry/apply methods operate on that state. Keep durable startup discovery of unapplied terminal jobs, the ten-attempt bound, runner replacement shutdown, and job identity checks. File-length limits can be met by moving cohesive constructors/methods; they do not justify changing state lifetime. Preserve the existing terminal-marker semantics unless changed by a separately reviewed recovery policy. <br><br> **Consumers/validation:** migrate `daemon_runner`, `tick_host_jobs`, `report_host_job_recovery`, `set_host_job_runner` and shutdown together. Existing `host_jobs_tests.rs` covers success, cancellation, third-attempt success and ten-attempt exhaustion; retain those tests and add two sequential orchestrator instances for the same state root, proving that only durable unapplied records cross the instance boundary. No process-global test serialization should be necessary for pending applications.<br><br>
<a id="rt-33"></a>**RT-33 — Knowledge and worktree spawn preflights duplicate the same sandbox recipe**<br><br>**Severity: MAJOR. Lenses: DRY, architecture.** <br><br> `core/spawn_setup.rs::validate_knowledge_sandbox` and `core/stage_preflight.rs::validate_stage_sandbox` both merge plan/stage sandbox configuration, validate it, expand paths and warn about missing grants. The former returns the merged configuration, but `setup_knowledge_sandbox_and_hooks` immediately discards it; the latter returns only a boolean. Both files also duplicate hook-directory admission and failure classification. The comments explicitly describe these implementations as mirrors. Launch then builds the actual settings capsule separately, with its own required validation (`terminal/native/session_settings/contents.rs:96–100`). <br><br> **Repair:** move the pure merge/validate/expand operation to the existing sandbox configuration owner and have both core paths call it. Keep the worktree/knowledge-specific cleanup at their callers: the worktree path undoes a write-ahead session, while knowledge setup currently blocks through its own path. A shared hook-admission helper already exists (`install_required_hooks`); keep only the necessary adapter-specific error handling around it. Return the resolved value where a consumer uses it, or return an explicit validation result; do not pretend to hand configuration to a launch path that recomputes it. Before passing a resolved snapshot into the capsule, account for launch-time environment/path expansion and freshness requirements; do not remove the capsule's independent boundary validation just to reduce calls. <br><br> **Validation:** run identical plan/stage inputs through both preflight callers and assert identical accepted/rejected policy, missing-grant diagnostics and expanded paths. Retain knowledge-stage tests proving no main-repository settings mutation, standard-stage cleanup tests, all stage-type grants, disabled-sandbox refusal and capsule validation. This is separate from RBR-11's fail-open capability probe and RT-10's path-pattern encoding defect.<br><br>
<a id="rt-34"></a>**RT-34 — Crash handling validates the session too early and latches failures as processed**<br><br>**Severity: CRITICAL. Lenses: architecture, duplicated lifecycle policy, invalid comments.** <br><br> `core/crash_handler.rs::stage_answerable_for_crash` verifies that the event names the current session, but `finish_crash_transition` drops the session identity before `persist_blocked_crash` acquires the stage lock. Its closure checks only Completed, then charges retry count and writes Blocked. A concurrent reset/replacement between the early read and locked mutation can therefore charge the old crash to a newly assigned session. `crash_handler_identity_tests.rs` covers an already-stale event at entry, not replacement inside that window. The neighboring handoff owner already demonstrates the correct pattern: `event_handler.rs::begin_handoff` checks session identity and allowed status inside the locked mutation. <br><br> The handler also inserts into `reported_crashes` before loading or persisting the stage and removes the matching active handle before persistence. If reading or writing the stage fails transiently, later delivery of the same crash returns immediately. The error log and `persist_blocked_crash` documentation promising “will retry next tick” are false for this handler. Other coherence/recovery paths may subsequently diagnose the stranded state, but that is not a retry of the original classified crash and its accounting. <br><br> **Repair:** carry the expected session ID into the locked crash transition and return a typed Applied/Stale/RetryableFailure result. Charge the budget only while the fresh record still names that session and is in a crash-applicable state. Latch only an applied event or a conclusively stale event; keep failed applications retryable, and coordinate removal of in-memory tracking with that result. Reuse the attempt-identity convention proposed in RT-02 rather than inventing a crash-specific reservation format. Do not hold the stage lock while synchronizing permissions or probing external processes. <br><br> **Validation:** insert a successor between initial observation and mutation and verify its status, retry budget, session assignment and active handle survive. Inject one read/write failure, then restore access and deliver the same event: it must apply exactly once, preserving classification and attempt accounting. Keep existing historical-crash, Completed-stage, Remote Control and startup-refusal cases.<br><br>
<a id="rt-35"></a>**RT-35 — Runtime diagnostic reads bypass the existing descriptor and byte-limit owner**<br><br>**CRITICAL; architecture, DRY, invalid comments.** `orchestrator/spawner.rs110–118::read_trailing_bytes` calls `File::open`, seeks using a size snapshot, then performs unrestricted `read_to_end`. The stated 256 KiB read bound at lines20–23 is therefore not a bound: a growing file can supply arbitrarily more bytes after the seek, and a FIFO blocks before metadata inspection. The 16 KiB final string clamp only bounds the report after allocation/read. Consumers include `monitor/handlers.rs174`, `core/crash_classification.rs193`, `terminal/session_tail.rs27`, and `daemon/server/launch.rs331`; recovery and launch diagnostics can themselves hang. <br><br> The same ownership gap appears in small runtime metadata readers: `core/merge_handler/resolver_attempts.rs40–51` separately stats and rereads a counter, following symlinks and leaving a type-swap window despite claiming a FIFO is never opened; `monitor/heartbeat_store.rs10–14` reads arbitrary bytes before JSON parsing; `monitor/core.rs231–235` independently reads each session file. These paths need actual descriptor limits, not more pathname prechecks. Keep this finding scoped to these runtime consumers; RBR-16 owns handoff readers and CTX findings own their data consumers. <br><br> **Repair.** Reuse `fs/safe_read` no-follow, nonblocking regular-file opening. Add only the small shared tail-read operation needed here: seek on that verified descriptor, then `take(MAX_LOG_READ_BYTES)` (or a documented snapshot-bounded length), with the existing lossy UTF-8 and final front clamp. Use appropriately small bounded whole-file readers for heartbeat/counter/session records; retain absent/error distinctions and the counter's fail-closed exhausted-budget policy. Trusted roots and intentional symlinks in the state-directory layout must be resolved at the existing WorkDir boundary, not rejected accidentally or validated then reopened by path. <br><br> **Acceptance.** FIFO and leaf/intermediate symlink fixtures return promptly without outside reads; a stream/file that grows after size observation cannot make the tail reader consume over its cap; normal large regular logs still return their tail rather than being rejected for total size. Retain invalid UTF-8 recovery, missing/blank log behavior, valid heartbeat compatibility, and unreadable-counter-as-exhausted tests. Current spawner tests only check final output size on a static regular file.<br><br>
<a id="rt-36"></a>**RT-36 — The monitor exports a second failure model that execution no longer records**<br><br>**MAJOR; architecture, DRY, excessive implementation, invalid comments.** `monitor/failure_tracking.rs` defines a 325-line subsystem with `FailureTracker`, `StageFailureState`, separate `state/<stage>.yaml` persistence, counters, escalation fields, and tests. Repository references show no production `record_failure` caller. Its only active consumer, `commands/stage/skip_retry.rs154–156`, loads this old store and computes `failure_count + 1` for recovery signals. Fresh execution therefore cannot advance that signal's attempt number through this model. `MonitorConfig.max_failures_before_escalation` is likewise only declared/defaulted (`monitor/config.rs21,31`). The module header promises configurable escalation, but the tracker never sets `is_escalated` or `last_escalation` and the monitor never uses it for decisions. Active accounting belongs to stage fields and core recovery. <br><br> **Repair.** Have retry construct its diagnostic attempt value from the authoritative stage retry/recovery model, with an explicit definition of which attempts count; do not silently equate distinct execution/stall/contract budgets. Remove the unused config field and orphan tracker/persistence exports from `monitor/mod.rs` and `orchestrator/mod.rs`, and remove tests that exercise only that abandoned implementation. If public library compatibility is required, deprecate the facade and document the supported migration before removal. Do not delete historical on-disk state as a side effect of this cleanup. <br><br> **Acceptance.** A stage that has incurred multiple actual recoveries produces a truthful next-attempt signal; fresh stages have the intended first number; all normal retry budgets and reset semantics remain unchanged. Repository call-site/export audit establishes that no runtime writer still relies on the old store. Verification reviewer independently confirmed the command consumer and ceded this finding.<br><br>
<a id="rt-37"></a>**RT-37 — Input-wait reconciliation drops its authorization conditions at the locked update**<br><br>**CRITICAL; architecture, duplicated state ownership.** `monitor/input_wait.rs45–84::stale_wait_progress` requires the current session's main-agent tool progress to be newer than `stage.updated_at`. `resume_stale_wait` at lines87–124 then locks/reloads the stage but rechecks only `WaitingForInput` before calling `end_wait`. Between snapshot and update, the same session can begin a newer legitimate question, or the stage can acquire another session. The stale heartbeat now cancels that newer wait. It also reports `true` and logs a resume when the closure did nothing because status moved on. <br><br> **Repair.** Carry the expected session and wait revision/timestamp into the shared locked transition. Recheck session, status, and that the observed progress is newer than the fresh wait on the locked record; return an explicit applied/stale result and log/collect only applied transitions. Prefer the same expected-owner transition convention proposed by RT-02/RT-34, while keeping this wait-specific evidence rule local. Preserve `end_wait` contract-refusal bookkeeping and the existing subagent/lifecycle/AskUserQuestion exclusions. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Barrier tests change the session or create a newer wait after snapshot classification and before lock acquisition: neither is resumed. Unchanged stale waits still resume, a concurrent status change yields no success log/result, and the current input_wait tests retain main-agent versus subagent behavior.<br><br>
<a id="rt-38"></a>**RT-38 — The handoff watch caches read failure as an immutable negative result**<br><br>**CRITICAL; architecture, excessive optimization, invalid comments.** `monitor/handoff_watch.rs113–131::refresh` records every filename permanently in `examined`, including `None` from transient open/read/parse failures in `takedown_request`/`frontmatter_head` (lines148–180). Its lines20–25 justification—artifacts are atomically published and never rewritten—does not make an unsuccessful read stable. A single temporary unreadable `AgentCeiling` document is never retried during that daemon run, so the stopped agent's document-only handoff request can remain unhandled. Existing cache tests prove positive reuse after deletion; they do not test failed-read recovery. Directory enumeration also occurs once per running session per tick, despite caching parse results. <br><br> **Repair.** Represent successful non-request, successful request, and retryable read failure separately. Cache only successfully inspected immutable artifacts; retain a bounded retry/backoff for read failures and emit a diagnostic rather than treating uncertainty as a final negative. If malformed documents are intentionally final, require a successfully read complete published artifact before applying that policy. Refresh the shared watch once per poll and query its indexed `(stage,session)` requests per session, preserving level-triggered re-emission until the stage moves on. Coordinate its descriptor opening with RBR-16. <br><br> **Acceptance.** An initially unreadable request becomes visible on a later poll without daemon restart; genuine non-request artifacts are not repeatedly parsed; positive cached requests preserve exact stage/session attribution; partial/oversized documents never authorize takedown. Count directory reads in a multi-session poll to prevent repeated refresh work.<br><br>

## Verification, execution plans and adapters

<a id="ver-01"></a>**VER-01 — Exact contract selection is lost in regex-based adapters**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL (reachable wrong verification result). **Lenses:** correctness, DRYness, schema/adapter drift. <br><br> **Locations and symbols:** <br><br> • `loom/src/plan/schema/types_v2.rs:32-33`, `ContractSpec::test`: “Exact test name the runner adapter selects.” <br> • `loom/src/testrun/adapters/bun_test.rs:90-95`, `jest.rs:93-98`, `vitest.rs:93-98`, `mocha.rs:99-104`: `single_test_command` escapes regex metacharacters but supplies no start/end anchors. <br> • `loom/src/verify/contracts/run_dir.rs:98-106`, `resolve_run`: feeds `contract.test` into that selector. <br> • `loom/src/commands/stage/contracts/freeze/prepared.rs:31-39,53-93`: resolves and executes the same selector during freeze. <br> • `loom/src/commands/stage/contracts/freeze.rs:95-130`, `judge_run`: uses aggregate counts/verdicts, not the identity of the test executed. <br> • `loom/src/verify/contracts/completion.rs:235-266`, `judge`: accepts `RunOutcome::Passed`, again using aggregate counts. <br> • `loom/src/testrun/adapters/go_test.rs:110-115`, `cargo_test.rs:122-138`: useful existing counterexamples; Go anchors the name and Cargo uses `--exact`. <br><br> **Evidence / trigger:** A contract names the complete runner name `rejects unsafe input`. The generated Bun command ends in `-t 'rejects unsafe input'`. A file containing a skipped exact test plus an active `rejects unsafe input with legacy mode` test still runs the latter. If that sibling passes, the adapter reports positive executed tests and completion passes. If the sibling fails before implementation, the same mismatch can satisfy the freeze's red phase, so freeze does not establish exact identity either. The two tests can remain unchanged in the frozen file; this does not depend on evading the frozen-file hash. <br><br> The duplication matters because each adapter independently performs `regex_literal` → `shell_quote`, and each independently omits exact matching. The command-shape tests preserve the omission: Bun's `command_selects_a_full_bun_test_name` at `bun_test.rs:140-150` expects an unanchored string; Vitest's test at `vitest.rs:152-170` does likewise. The recorded Bun/Vitest fixture descriptions use distinct `alpha_passes`, `beta_fails`, `gamma_passes`, and `delta_missing`; they do not exercise overlapping names. <br><br> **Consequence:** The red/green evidence can certify a sibling instead of the exact named contract. The zero-test guard cannot help because at least one unrelated matching test ran. This is established by the selector and verdict flow; it was not exercised against installed runners in this audit. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** For these four adapters, form an exact regex from the full runner-visible test name, preserving existing literal escaping and shell quoting. A small shared `exact_regex_literal` helper beside `testrun::command::regex_literal` is justified by the identical operation. Keep file selection semantics unchanged initially. Define full nested-suite names explicitly in the trait/schema documentation. Do not blindly apply JavaScript regex conventions to other runner syntaxes. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** contract freeze preparation, `host_verify::freeze::run_contracts`, contract completion (live and cached command identity), adapter command tests, and fixtures for these four runners. `select_command` for impact tests deliberately selects files and is a separate contract. RSpec `-e`, PHP/Pest `--filter`, Swift `--filter`, and Dart/Flutter `--plain-name` also deserve a runner-specific exactness audit; only their selector sites were surveyed, so they are follow-up scope rather than additional confirmed claims here. <br><br> **Behavioral constraints:** Preserve shell injection resistance, regex literal escaping, full nested test names, named package runners, zero-test rejection, and compatibility with parameterized names. A changed command must produce a new acceptance cache key rather than reuse an old substring-selected pass. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** For each of the four adapters, run a fixture with exact, prefix, and suffix names, plus regex metacharacters. Removing/skipping the exact target while leaving passing siblings must produce no selected test and cannot pass completion. A failing sibling alone must not satisfy freeze for a missing exact target. Existing normal/hostile-name fixtures must retain their intended behavior. Check actual runner selection, not only expected command text. <br><br> **Supplement: VER-01 additional adapter consumers from the full pass.** <br><br> `testrun/adapters/phpunit.rs:102-104`, `pest.rs:93-95` and `swift_test.rs` single-test selection also pass an escaped but unanchored test name to a regex filter. Include them in the existing exact-selection repair and collision tests. Their command tests currently assert the broad strings; recorded or synthetic no-match fixtures do not prove that a sibling name cannot satisfy a requested name. <br><br> Do not blindly prepend `^` to a PHP method name: PHPUnit's selectable identifier includes class/method and potentially dataset text. Specify the adapter's supported identifier form and construct a dialect-correct full identifier or boundary match. Swift likewise needs its runner's class/module separator semantics. Preserve shell-word escaping separately from regex matching. Dart/Flutter `--plain-name` and RSpec description-selection semantics remain specialist compatibility questions; this report does not claim them proven exact or broken without runner evidence.<br><br>
<a id="ver-02"></a>**VER-02 — Host deadline duplicates command budgets and undercounts long postconditions**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL (reachable premature termination). **Lenses:** DRYness, architecture, model/runtime drift. <br><br> **Locations and symbols:** <br><br> • `loom/src/models/stage/checks.rs:57-84`, `TruthCheck::{timeout_secs,timeout}`. <br> • `loom/src/plan/schema/validation/acceptance_command.rs:13-26`: accepted timeout range `1..=3600` applies to extended criteria and truth checks. <br> • `loom/src/verify/goal_backward/truths.rs:23-28`, `verify_truth_checks`: honors `check.timeout(DEFAULT_CHECK_TIMEOUT)`. <br> • `loom/src/commands/stage/complete_verification.rs:134-145`, `run_after_checks`: runs the plan's `after_stage` through that executor. <br> • `loom/src/host_verify/runner.rs:326-344`, `job_deadline_secs`: sums acceptance timeouts correctly, but folds `stage.after_stage.len()` into `commands * 300`. <br> • `loom/src/host_verify/runner.rs:208-215,137-145,377-381`: installs that deadline, then retires and fails the child when it expires. <br> • `loom/src/orchestrator/monitor/stall_line.rs:62-70`, `completion_line_secs`: another independent completion-budget calculation uses acceptance count × 300 and omits contracts, impact selection, and after-stage checks. <br><br> **Evidence / trigger:** A standard v1 stage with one simple acceptance command and one `after_stage` command declaring `timeout_secs: 3600` is valid. The host deadline is `300 + (1 impact allowance + 1 after-stage) * 300 + 1800 = 2700` seconds. The postcondition may legitimately run for 3000 seconds, below its own 3600-second limit, yet the host process is killed after about 2700 seconds. The declared per-command timeout therefore does not bound the outer runner correctly. <br><br> The existing `host_verify/tests_runner_recovery.rs:87-108` test covers a long **acceptance** criterion only. `verify/goal_backward/truths.rs:236-254` verifies that a truth check has its own timeout, but does not connect it to the outer deadline. These two individually correct pieces demonstrate the missing cross-layer invariant. <br><br> **Consequence:** A permitted long postcondition fails as a host infrastructure deadline rather than being allowed to finish. Additional future command kinds can repeat this drift because the execution pipeline and budget inventory are maintained separately. The monitor calculation is related duplication; this review does **not** claim it interrupts an active held host job, since `hung_latch.rs:113-120` substitutes a held-job allowance. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** At minimum sum each `after_stage` entry's actual timeout just as acceptance entries are summed. Then put the stage's verification-budget calculation in a verification-owned helper consumed by the host runner and, where semantically appropriate, the monitor. Preserve a separate named overhead margin and saturating arithmetic. Keep job-kind-specific differences explicit; do not turn this into a generic workflow framework or assume all checks have equal cost. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** host runner startup/deadline tests, truth-check execution tests, completion pipeline, and the monitor's completion allowance/tests. The monitor owner should verify whether its intentional broader/held-job policy needs a separate wrapper over the shared command sum. <br><br> **Behavioral constraints:** The job deadline remains finite; timeout and process-group cleanup still kill truly overdue jobs. Existing command output caps, child identity checks, and bounded job slots remain intact. Use the effective inputs actually executed; plan definitions and persisted stage fields must not silently supply different budgets. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** A 3600-second after-stage check contributes at least 3600 seconds to the command sum; several different timeout values add correctly; unset values use the executor default; saturating overflow remains bounded. Use a fake clock/injected deadline to show that a permitted long check is not retired early and that a genuinely expired job still is. No wall-clock hour-long test is necessary.<br><br>
<a id="ver-03"></a>**VER-03 — Verification engines and host jobs depend on CLI implementation modules**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR (substantial ownership/coupling problem). **Lenses:** architecture, DRYness, redundant orchestration. <br><br> **Locations and symbols:** <br><br> • `loom/src/host_verify/child.rs:18-20`: imports execution paths, criterion rendering, and goal verification from `commands`. <br> • `loom/src/host_verify/child.rs:87-120,172-193`: loads the plan to check whether goals exist, then calls a command helper that loads it again. <br> • `loom/src/host_verify/child_complete.rs:22-25,126-147`: imports `VerificationChecks`, `run_checks`, and impact observation from `commands::stage::complete`. <br> • `loom/src/host_verify/freeze.rs:30-34,204-242` and `freeze_memo.rs:13`: host execution consumes CLI-owned freeze inputs/preparation/judgment. <br> • `loom/src/commands/stage/complete_verification.rs:18-57,58-156`: a reusable sink-driven verification pipeline is already present, but its steps print terminal output and call CLI guidance directly. <br> • `loom/src/commands/verify.rs:44-62,232-261`: plan resolution is duplicated within the command and the shared goal helper. <br> • `loom/src/commands/stage/complete_resolved.rs:7-44`: trusted resolver completion assembles another acceptance/snapshot/check/state-update flow. <br> • `loom/src/orchestrator/core/inbox_drain/merge_resolved.rs:55` and `core/merge_handler/resolver_exit.rs:152`: scheduler code calls that CLI-owned flow. <br><br> **Evidence / consequence:** This is an actual dependency inversion, not just an inconvenient directory name. Host jobs need functions and types from CLI modules to execute domain work, while those functions retain `println!`, colored display/guidance dependencies, plan reloads, and operational policy switches. A change to command presentation or loading behavior can alter daemon verification. The host's `JobTree` resolves from trusted state-root identity, while operator execution resolves through cwd; combining these casually would weaken a deliberate trust boundary. The current structure makes that distinction harder to review. <br><br> `run_checks` and the criteria engine are already shared. Do not create a third verification engine. The immediate target is ownership of existing code, then removal of duplicated assembly/plan parsing. `ParsedPlan` also carries cloned `stages` alongside `metadata.loom.stages` (`plan/parser/mod.rs:49-56`); this reinforces the value of passing a single loaded verification input, but a repository-wide model rewrite is not required for this finding. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair / migration:** <br><br> 1. Move the already shared execution-path value and containment resolver to a neutral verification module; retain separate trusted-state-root and operator-cwd acquisition entry points. <br> 2. Move goal plan loading and completion check orchestration under `verify`, accepting one loaded stage definition/effective policy where possible. Keep `CheckSink` (or a similarly small structured sink), with CLI rendering at the command adapter and host progress persistence at the host adapter. <br> 3. Move freeze preparation/judgment to `verify::contracts`; leave CLI argument handling/relay/output in `commands::stage::contracts`. <br> 4. Make trusted resolver verification use the same neutral service, leaving stage state transitions/merge application in their existing owner. Update scheduler imports last. Avoid changing scheduling mode or converting synchronous landing to jobs in this move; that is a separate lifecycle change. <br> 5. Once both surfaces call the shared service, remove duplicate plan reads and obsolete wrappers/imports. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** direct `loom check`, operator completion, host Check/Complete/Freeze children, resolver landing in both orchestrator paths, contract tests, host child/reuse/impact tests, command completion tests, and criterion output adapters. `host_verify/apply.rs:363` also reaches a CLI session-update helper; move it with the session lifecycle owner rather than enlarging the verification service. Coordinate with the separate runtime architecture review; this finding owns host/orchestrator → completion CLI coupling, not status DTO ownership. <br><br> **Behavioral constraints:** Preserve cache policy (`--no-cache` refreshes), selected criterion indices, streaming outcomes and persistence errors, fail-closed host detection, operator stop-on-first-failure behavior, daemon-owned integrity/review gates, snapshot policy/hash binding, pinned Git identity, knowledge-stage confinement, and cancellation/group cleanup. These differences are policies to name, not duplication to erase. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** Representative Check/Complete/Freeze flows yield the same outcomes and labels before/after extraction; CLI output remains at the adapter; host functions no longer import `commands`; one invocation uses a consistent loaded definition/policy. Existing host snapshot, dirty-tree, criterion-subset, reuse, frozen-file, and resolver ancestry cases remain meaningful. Perform migration in small compile-preserving steps.<br><br>
<a id="ver-04"></a>**VER-04 — Structural preflight has its own weaker shell/path parser**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR (fragmented diagnostic ownership); the demonstrated runtime effects are warning errors, not verification bypasses. **Lenses:** architecture, DRYness, non-idiomatic/redundant code. <br><br> **Locations and symbols:** `loom/src/plan/schema/validation.rs:273-629` (`validate`), `635-699` (build-tool detection/flag parsing), `753-974` (`validate_structural_preflight`), especially `894-918` (config-file warnings) and `922-967` (three redundant path-prefix checks). Compare existing `validation/shell_lex.rs:1-28,80+` and `testrun/recognize.rs:71-102,134-160`; `schema/validation_suite.rs:40-64` already consumes the shared invocation representation. <br><br> **Evidence / triggers:** <br><br> • `criterion_uses_build_tool` searches raw text and checks only the preceding byte. A criterion such as `printf '%s' 'cargo test'` is diagnosed as invoking Cargo even though Cargo is data. <br> • `extract_flag_value` uses `split_whitespace`, so `cargo test --manifest-path 'crate dir/Cargo.toml'` looks for a path beginning with the literal quote and truncated at the space. The already available lexer removes quotes without evaluating the command. <br> • It searches a complete shell string for selectors, so a selector belonging to another simple command can be assigned to the detected tool. <br> • For artifact, wiring, and regression paths the expression `p.starts_with(working_dir) || p.starts_with(format!("{working_dir}/"))` repeats a strict subset of the first condition. With `working_dir: src`, `src_extra/file.rs` falsely looks like a redundant `src/` prefix. The three copies repeat the same mistake. <br><br> The module mixes schema acceptance, sandbox validation, capability advice, filesystem probes, cross-stage checks, and command interpretation. Its 356-line `validate` and 222-line preflight functions are relevant because related checks are owned inconsistently; file length alone is not the finding. Newer hazard/suite checks use the shared lexer while these older checks remain raw-string heuristics. <br><br> **Consequence:** False warnings consume agent/operator attention and invite unnecessary plan edits; each new shell syntax or path case must be fixed in multiple implementations. The quoted-path behavior is directly apparent in the local code; no tool execution is needed to establish it. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Extract diagnostic-specific preflight helpers from `validation.rs` and operate on shared lexed simple commands. Match executable positions and attach selectors to their own invocation. Reuse existing prefix/wrapper handling where relevant, but preserve conservative handling of dynamic/unknown shell expressions. For path redundancy, use a component-boundary-aware helper shared by artifacts, wiring, and regression paths. Keep hard schema errors and advisory warnings as separate public operations with stable stage/index attribution. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** plan check/verify/init preflight callers; `schema/tests/build_tool_path_tests.rs`; `validation_tests.rs:410-439`; `regression_test_tests.rs:228-247`; shared lexer tests. The existing build-tool path fixture verifies only an unquoted `Cargo.toml` selector. <br><br> **Behavioral constraints:** Do not execute shell expansions or package scripts to lint a command. Preserve command-hazard validation, diagnostics for real missing manifests, explicit `working_dir` semantics, and all hard path traversal restrictions. This cleanup must not change warning-level checks into execution blockers. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** Quoted spaces and `--flag=value` select the correct manifest; quoted tool words do not count as invocations; unrelated commands' flags do not cross-contaminate; actual `src/file` prefixes warn while `src_extra/file` does not. Existing unquoted manifest and true redundancy tests continue passing.<br><br>
<a id="ver-05"></a>**VER-05 — Comments describe behavior the tree no longer implements**<br><br>**Severity:** MINOR (misleading policy/documentation). **Lens:** invalid/overlong comments. <br><br> **A. Liveness policy:** `loom/src/plan/schema/types.rs:329-335`, `StageDefinition::subagent_timeout_secs`, says: “The check is ADVISORY: it reports a silent session, it never kills or retries one.” The monitor reads this value at `orchestrator/monitor/hung_latch.rs:71-75`; sufficiently silent sessions can reach `core/event_handler/recover_hung.rs:211-226,246-255`, which begins a handoff, charges recovery, and finishes/requeues with `SessionExitReason::Stalled`. Existing exemptions/budgets remain relevant; “never” is false. Describe the initial warning and later bounded recovery, and link the actual owner instead of promising immunity from teardown. <br><br> **B. Graph sandbox restoration:** `loom/src/plan/graph/loader.rs:17-18` says: “When loading from stage files (which don't carry plan-level config), `SandboxConfig::default()` is returned.” At `loader.rs:73-77`, the loader instead reads the persisted plan sandbox and uses defaults only when absent. Replace the unconditional default claim with the persisted-snapshot/fallback rule. <br><br> **C. Verification process boundary:** `loom/src/plan/schema/validation.rs:706-708` says: “`loom stage complete` runs acceptance from inside the session”. Session completion is relayed to the daemon host child (`host_verify/child.rs:1-8,39-50` and `child_complete.rs:1-13`), which runs plan commands under confinement. The resource warning may still be valid, but its stated execution boundary is wrong. Explain the confined host-command boundary and distinguish it from agent-session sandboxing. <br><br> **D. Child output claim:** `loom/src/host_verify/child.rs:7-8` says “It prints nothing; the CLI wrapper prints the summary the runner captures in `child.log`.” Complete jobs call `complete_verification::run_checks`, whose goal/after/aggregated checks print at `complete_verification.rs:89,99-111,135-154`; freeze preparation also prints at `commands/stage/contracts/freeze/prepared.rs:61-65`. Qualify the claim until VER-03 separates rendering, or remove it. <br><br> **Smallest repair / affected consumers:** Update these four comments in place. No public API or runtime policy change is necessary. VER-03 may make the child-silence statement true later; document today's behavior first. Avoid moving historical explanation into current-state docs. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** Review each sentence against its cited implementation and ensure links name current symbols. No behavioral tests are needed for comment-only changes. No stale knowledge claim was independently established; these are source comments. <br><br> **Supplement: Additional comment correction supporting VER-05.** <br><br> `plan/schema/host_paths.rs:1-13` claims setup, acceptance, wiring, before-stage and after-stage commands all execute inside the same stage session sandbox. That is contradicted by the host completion pipeline already traced in VER-05 and by `schema/tests/v2_lint_environment_tests.rs:264-274`, which explicitly asserts before-stage execution outside the sandbox. The same obsolete premise is repeated in `host_paths/commands.rs:75-79` diagnostics. Update these to describe the actual phase/confinement rules, and check which host-path diagnostics apply to each phase before changing policy. Do not silently relax valid restrictions on session grants or confined commands as a documentation cleanup. This observation is about core execution semantics; the unfinished planner workflow remains excluded. <br><br> **Supplement: VER-05 additional stale provenance comments.** <br><br> `impact_tests/accepted.rs:9-11`, `impact_tests/failure.rs:51-53` and `stage_service/dispute_kinds.rs:296-304` describe observed failures as reports written by the stage session. Current `impact_tests/observed.rs:7-14` correctly identifies the host completion job and operator completion as writers. Replace only the writer attribution and align the comments with that module. Preserve the unauthenticated-output warning: stage-controlled tests can still print arbitrary evidence even when the host persists it. No change to acceptance equality or adjudicator re-run policy follows from this comment correction.<br><br>
<a id="ver-06"></a>**VER-06 — Frontmatter-only readers invoke an inconsistent Markdown section parser**<br><br>**Severity:** MINOR, optional simplification (no observed production consumer of section bodies). **Lenses:** DRYness, redundant abstraction, parser drift. <br><br> **Locations:** `loom/src/parser/markdown.rs:20-70,72-104`, `MarkdownDocument::{parse,extract_frontmatter,parse_sections}`; canonical boundary scan at `parser/frontmatter.rs:74-116`. Production callers found: `fs/session_files/enumerate.rs:58-75`, `fs/session_files.rs:172-196`, `commands/status/diagnostics.rs:50-51`, and `commands/status/validation.rs:20-22`. <br><br> **Evidence:** These callers inspect string-valued frontmatter or only parse success.
