None consumes `sections`, `get_section`, or `trimmed_content`. Nonetheless each read allocates a lines vector, parses frontmatter, rescans for a body boundary, joins the body, and scans every heading into owned strings. The second boundary scan does not share the canonical parser's indentation rule: it stops at an indented `---` inside a block scalar. It also claims to accept `...`, although the first canonical scan only accepts `---` prefixes. A `--- # comment` closing line is accepted by the canonical scan but not by the second exact-match scan. The section parser itself treats `#` inside fences as a heading. <br><br> The canonical embedded-delimiter test exists at `frontmatter.rs:196-224`; the Markdown wrapper has only basic examples at `markdown.rs:125-144`. Because current callers ignore sections, this is a latent public parser inconsistency and unnecessary work, not a demonstrated session identity bug. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** Give frontmatter-only callers a focused parser that preserves their current optional-frontmatter/string-scalar behavior. If the section API is retained for library consumers, make a single boundary helper return frontmatter and remainder; both parsing paths must use it. Avoid inventing another Markdown parser. If no supported external consumer needs sections, deprecate/remove that unused surface according to project API policy. <br><br> **Affected consumers / constraints:** The four callers above, `parser::markdown` exports, canonical frontmatter tests, session enumeration tests, and status validation. Preserve current null/string conversion behavior and explicitly decide whether missing frontmatter is accepted at each caller; silently switching to a stricter required-frontmatter parser would be an unrelated behavior change. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** Embedded indented delimiters, CRLF, optional frontmatter, and delimiter suffixes use the same boundary; basic session identifiers remain identical. If sections remain supported, add a fenced-heading case. No need to add a generalized parser framework.<br><br>
<a id="ver-07"></a>**VER-07 — Graph loading's custom trait abstracts identical operations**<br><br>**Severity:** MINOR, optional simplification. **Lenses:** unnecessary abstraction, non-idiomatic API shape. <br><br> **Locations:** `loom/src/plan/graph/loader.rs:19-60,86-95`, `build_execution_graph`, `AsWorkDir`, four implementations, `build_graph_impl`, and `load_graph_from_plan_file`. `fs/work_dir.rs:370-375` shows `WorkDir::load_config_required` simply forwarding to the same free function with its root path. <br><br> **Evidence:** The trait is named as a conversion (`AsWorkDir`) but performs graph construction through `build_graph`. Four implementations exist for owned/borrowed `WorkDir` and `Path`; they thread an `Option<&WorkDir>` through two helpers solely to choose between equivalent config-loading calls. The implementation on unsized `Path` cannot itself be supplied by value to the sized `impl AsWorkDir` argument. Actual callers are the run foreground path and the daemon graph-loading wrapper, plus focused tests; they already have a work-directory path or can call `root()`. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** Make the graph loader accept `&Path`, adapting the foreground caller with `work_dir.root()`, and remove the trait/optional branch. If a common path-conversion trait is already needed elsewhere, use it at that shared owner; do not add one merely for this function. Preserve the public symbol or provide a compatibility wrapper if external Rust consumers rely on it. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** `commands/run/foreground.rs:66`, `daemon/server/orchestrator.rs:243-244`, `commands/run/tests/preflight.rs`, `fs/tests_stage_loading.rs`. **Behavioral constraints:** Keep persisted-stage precedence, manifest equality validation, plan fallback, contextual errors, and persisted sandbox restoration exactly as they are. **Acceptance:** Both existing calling contexts load equivalent graphs/configuration, including missing-stage and missing-plan cases. Existing graph-loader tests are sufficient; no new abstraction-specific tests are needed.<br><br>
<a id="ver-08"></a>**VER-08 — Request-state ownership check races its deletion**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL (reachable loss of pending request tracking). **Lenses:** architecture/concurrency ownership; invalid comment. **Status:** confirmed source-level interleaving, not reproduced by execution. <br><br> **Evidence:** `loom/src/host_verify/wait_state.rs:39-47` atomically replaces the shared request-state pathname with `NamedTempFile::persist`. `remove_state_if_owned` at lines 49-58 reads that pathname, compares its request ID, then separately calls `remove_file`. Its comment promises, “one waiter's end must not discard a newer request another wrote.” No common lock spans the comparison and deletion or participates in replacement. <br><br> **Trigger and consequence:** waiter A finishes request r1 and reads r1 from the shared state file. Waiter B requests a check with changed criteria, tree or cache policy and persists r2 at the same pathname. A then removes that pathname, deleting r2. The daemon may still execute r2, but the next invocation has lost its request ID and issues another ticket instead of following it; changed-policy requests can supersede one another and duplicate work. `wait.rs:164` invokes this on ordinary terminal outcomes, and `wait.rs:183` invokes it when releasing an applied completion, so both paths need the same repair. <br><br> **Existing test limitation:** `host_verify/tests_wait.rs:347-368`, `a_terminal_outcome_leaves_a_newer_request_the_other_waiter_wrote`, writes r2 before making r1 terminal. This proves the comparison rejects an already replaced file; it does not exercise replacement between the comparison and removal. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** give the per-stage/per-kind scratch state a stable sibling lock and acquire it in both `write_state` and compare-and-delete. Keep polling and relay emission outside that lock; take it only for the state-file mutations. Do not lock the replaceable JSON inode, because rename changes the inode and splits lock ownership. An existing repository lock helper is preferable if it supports a stable sibling file in session scratch. Propagate or deliberately log lock failures rather than silently claiming the ownership invariant holds. Preserve the current atomic replacement and private file permissions. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** `wait::request`, ordinary terminal handling in `drive`, completion `release`, check/complete session relay adapters and the tests above. Stage/kind path naming and session scratch isolation remain compatible. A completed waiter must still retain another request's state; it need not cancel that request or change the daemon's job state. Do not hold the host store's stage lock while waiting on a session scratch lock. <br><br> **Acceptance:** a deterministic concurrency test pauses A after it has observed r1, attempts B's r2 replacement, and proves A cannot delete r2. Test both ordinary terminal cleanup and completion release via the shared helper. Retain the existing already-replaced-state case and the failed-state-write refusal behavior. No timing-only sleeps as the proof of ordering.<br><br>
<a id="ver-09"></a>**VER-09 — Bounded receipt reads can block before validating file type**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL (reachable indefinite blocking on a FIFO). **Lenses:** shared ownership/DRY; defensive boundary consistency. **Status:** confirmed by open flags and call paths, not executed. <br><br> **Evidence:** `models/forward_receipt/locator.rs:15-39`, `read_bounded_prefix`, opens with `O_NOFOLLOW` and only afterwards checks descriptor metadata for a regular file. Opening a FIFO read-only without `O_NONBLOCK` waits for a writer before returning, so neither the file-type check nor the byte limit runs. `job_record.rs:64-73` checks only pathname size before this open, so a direct reader can block on an already present FIFO. Callers which validate a regular pathname first remain exposed to replacement between validation and open. The adjacent `models/forward_receipt/load.rs:55-77` already uses `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK` and explains this exact hazard; its test at `forward_receipt/tests.rs:353-369` checks prompt FIFO rejection. <br><br> **Reachable consumers:** companion-job reads in `commands/hook/forward_receipt.rs:163`, `commands/subagents/forward_jobs.rs:223`, `commands/subagents/wait/codex_binding.rs:128`, and `codex_lifecycle/jobs.rs:56`; forwarding transcript reads in `transcript.rs:61`; task-output marker reads in `transcript.rs:287`. The lifecycle scanner filters directory entries to regular files before reading, but that pathname check does not make the subsequent open atomic. An unexpected FIFO or a replaced path can stall hooks/status/wait/reconciliation instead of reporting unreadable evidence. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** add `O_NONBLOCK` at the shared bounded-prefix open, preserving descriptor-based regular-file and ownership checks, `O_NOFOLLOW`, byte caps, and incomplete trailing UTF-8 behavior. Consider sharing the open-and-inspect primitive with the adjacent loader only if the ownership-policy difference remains explicit; do not replace these checks with a pathname-only precheck. This finding does not claim the existing helper protects intermediate path components against replacement. <br><br> **Related finding:** CTX-12 in `context-extra-02.md` covers similar ledger/quota readers. Prefer the existing `fs/safe_read` owner or its open-and-inspect primitive where semantics fit; receipt ownership checks and bounded-prefix UTF-8 handling, complete-newline ledger parsing, and quota-cache failure policy remain distinct caller requirements. Coordinate the migration rather than adding another generic reader. <br><br> **Acceptance:** a reader of a FIFO with no writer returns an error promptly without leaving a blocked test thread/process; regular-file bounded prefixes, torn UTF-8 and symlink rejection retain their current behavior. Add the regression at the shared helper and one public companion-job reader boundary, and confirm the callers still convert unreadable evidence to their current failure/unknown result.<br><br>
<a id="ver-10"></a>**VER-10 — PlanPatch documentation describes an unfinished migration that already has consumers**<br><br>**Severity:** MINOR. **Lens:** stale comments. **Evidence:** `models/dispute.rs:281-294` calls `PlanPatch` a “Forward-declaration” that “Stage 3 will replace” with `AmendmentField` and `AmendmentPatch`; `inner` is described as opaque only until that stage lands. Those typed amendments exist and `orchestrator/adjudication/plan_patch.rs:26-126` already normalizes the persisted JSON into typed requests, including array, nested and flat compatibility shapes. Contract and freeze-refusal handlers use different envelopes, and accepted impact disputes can carry an empty object. The wrapper remains an on-disk verdict envelope, not an unimplemented stage placeholder. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** replace the staged-development narrative with the current persisted-envelope contract and links to the relevant per-kind decoders. Keep the JSON wrapper and compatible wire shapes in this documentation-only repair; changing its type requires a separate compatibility design across adjudication handlers, verdict writers, stored records and amendment retry tests. Also remove the obsolete “pre-Stage 2” historical aside from `models/stage/methods.rs:339-351`, retaining the reason for the two-step state transition. <br><br> **Acceptance:** comments describe the current decoding boundary and supported consumers; no schema or serialized output changes and no new tests needed.<br><br>
<a id="ver-11"></a>**VER-11 — Wiring equality serializes YAML instead of comparing the typed values**<br><br>**Severity:** MINOR (optional simplification). **Lenses:** redundant/non-idiomatic code; DRY. **Evidence:** `plan/amendment_fields.rs:134-151` and `plan/amendment_catch_up.rs:100-117` independently serialize wiring checks/tests on both sides and compare strings, using `unwrap_or_default` because the types lack `PartialEq`. `models/stage/checks.rs:39-52` (`WiringCheck`) and `models/stage/types.rs:89-127` (`SuccessCriteria`, `WiringTest`) contain only strings, integers, booleans, options and vectors of those values. They can derive structural equality directly. <br><br> **Consequence:** a domain equality decision used for amendment reconciliation and invalidating `completed_commit` depends on serialization formatting, allocates temporary YAML strings, and would treat two serialization errors as equal empty strings. Existing types do not demonstrate a reachable serialization failure, so this is a maintenance simplification rather than a claimed current failure. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** derive `PartialEq` (and `Eq` where desired) on the three model types, replace the four serialized comparisons with typed comparisons, and remove the obsolete explanatory comments. Keep field-scoped reconciliation and commit invalidation unchanged. No macro or generic field-traversal framework is needed. <br><br> **Consumers/acceptance:** both amendment comparison helpers and their recovery tests; serialized YAML must remain unchanged. Existing wiring amendment/catch-up tests should retain behavior; add only a focused test if current tests omit a relevant wiring-success-criteria field. Reordered vectors must continue to compare unequal.<br><br>
<a id="ver-12"></a>**VER-12 — Reexport scaffolding and repeated validation loops obscure their small contracts**<br><br>**Severity:** MINOR (optional simplification). **Lenses:** excessive comments/module indirection; DRY; idiomatic Rust. <br><br> **Evidence:** `plan/schema/types/stage_reexports.rs:1-56` contains only reexports, with four-line descriptions repeatedly explaining that each canonical type lives elsewhere. Its module comment says the file exists to keep `types.rs` below a line-count baseline. `types.rs:71-98` repeats the same forwarding explanation for five sandbox types, and lines 357-366 import the extra forwarding module's exports back into the parent. The extra module is not an ownership boundary: its only job is to relay names. Separately, `schema/validation.rs:90-125` repeats the same glob-validation loop for three field lists, and lines 128-153 repeat the same domain-validation loop twice. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** use one documented grouped `pub use crate::models::stage::{...}` block at the existing public schema owner and a direct grouped v2 reexport, then remove the now-orphaned forwarding module. Retain useful canonical type documentation at the definitions. For validation, iterate a local ordered array of `(diagnostic_label, slice)` pairs, retaining the existing leaf validators and error vector. This requires neither a generic validator trait nor a macro/framework. These changes reduce the line count by removing repetition before considering another file split. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** preserve every existing public path exported through `plan::schema` and `plan::schema::types`; do not move the canonical models or change serde definitions. Preserve validation traversal order (deny_read, deny_write, allow_write; allowed_domains, additional_domains), stage IDs and exact diagnostic labels. Existing callers/tests remain source-compatible; no new test solely for grouped imports is useful. Existing schema validation tests suffice for the loop rewrite.<br><br>
<a id="ver-13"></a>**VER-13 — Sandbox lints duplicate phase resolution and reject commands that run on the host**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** architecture; DRY; representation choice. **Status:** confirmed validation-path inconsistency. <br><br> **Evidence:** `plan/schema/validation/v2_lints/sandbox_capability.rs:129-162` scans `stage_commands(stage)`, including before-stage checks, and emits `error_in_v2: true` whenever a recognized network command has no allowed domains. Its caller at lines 18-38 resolves network settings but never checks effective `sandbox.enabled`. Thus a before-stage `curl https://example.com` is reported as impossible under the stage sandbox even though before-stage runs on the daemon host. An acceptance command with effective sandbox disabled is also rejected on this premise. The before-stage behavior is explicitly asserted by `schema/tests/v2_lint_environment_tests.rs:264-274` for the sibling registry lint, but no equivalent curl test exists for this lint. <br><br> The sibling `registry_domains.rs:102-123` does resolve effective enablement, while `v2_lints/mod.rs:145-151` excludes before-stage commands by parsing the human-facing label prefix. `registry_domains.rs:125-151` separately infers confined-runner execution from four more label prefixes. The two lints therefore do not share a reliable execution model: one has phase/enablement handling, the other has none, and presentation text controls behavior. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** add a small enum for the existing command kinds to the internal `StageCommand` record and derive labels from it. Centralize effective sandbox/confinement resolution used by these lints, with explicit distinctions between daemon before-stage execution, confined completion commands, and inherited/session-style paths. Have capability and registry lints consume that record; avoid introducing a general workflow framework. The plain substring invocation detection is separately covered by VER-04 and should use the same existing lexer. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** core schema lint collection, registry-domain and sandbox-capability checks, structural hazard warnings and their fixtures. A disabled session sandbox must not accidentally disable the independent restrictions of a command whose completion confinement is still `Confined`. Preserve the actual runtime policy rather than equating all host-spawned commands with unrestricted commands. Keep messages and severity stable for cases that truly cannot run; changing wording must no longer change phase selection. <br><br> **Acceptance:** before-stage curl and registry fetches receive the same host-phase treatment; an effective disabled sandbox is honored on inherited commands; confined acceptance still reports no-network regardless of allowed session domains; stage overrides still replace plan defaults. Existing socket-risk and in-worktree-grant findings remain intact. Tests should target those policy distinctions, not enum labels. <br><br> **Supplement: Additional comment correction under VER-13.** <br><br> `tests/plan_environment_contracts.rs:344-345` says an inherited criterion runs inside the session sandbox. Current acceptance runs in a host completion/check job, and inherit selects no additional criteria confinement. Correct the test explanation together with the lint policy owner already identified by VER-13; preserve the actual phase-specific filesystem/network guarantees.<br><br>
<a id="ver-14"></a>**VER-14 — Malformed amendment audit rows become destructive orphan evidence**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR (data-loss handling during recovery). **Lenses:** durable-state architecture; validation consistency. **Status:** confirmed source interleaving with corrupted journal data, not executed. <br><br> **Evidence:** `plan/amendment/journal.rs:127-149` returns a successful row list while silently dropping any row that `parse_audit_row` cannot decode. The parser at lines 203-228 returns `None` for malformed version, field, operation, index or timestamp. `amendment/recovery.rs:10-12` immediately passes the resulting incomplete list to `remove_orphans`; lines 43-51 delete every numbered snapshot absent from that list. A damaged timestamp in a committed row therefore causes its valid snapshot to be deleted as if the snapshot had never been committed. If an older row survives, recovery may then reconcile that older amendment field over the live plan. Cap and dispute-id retry readers also consume the shortened history. <br><br> **Trigger and scope:** corruption or manual editing of the human-readable audit table, including a malformed committed row. Current audit writers atomically publish whole tables (`journal.rs:152-169`), so this finding does not claim normal interrupted append produces partial rows. The problem is treating malformed durable state as trustworthy evidence of absence. Existing `plan/tests/amendment.rs:463-487` checks a genuinely orphaned snapshot with an intact audit; catch-up tests protect an unparseable live plan, but do not protect an unparseable journal. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** distinguish the known table header/separator/prose from attempted data rows, and return a line-specific error when a data row cannot be decoded. Recovery and amendment application must stop before deleting snapshots or applying/capping/retrying against incomplete history. Keep malformed files and snapshots available for diagnosis. Validate enough journal structure to detect missing/truncated table framing rather than silently treating a nonempty damaged journal as an empty one; choose explicit compatibility rules for existing audit layouts. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** `read_audit_rows`, next-version allocation, per-stage amendment/verdict counts, dispute-id idempotence, recovery and pending-transaction completion. Preserve lock ordering and atomic audit publication. Legitimate orphan cleanup remains valid when the journal parses successfully; a missing journal during the documented pre-commit crash window must retain its intended handling. Do not resolve corruption by overwriting the entire current plan with a snapshot: field-scoped catch-up and unrelated prose/field preservation are real safeguards. <br><br> **Acceptance:** corrupt a committed audit row's timestamp/version/field independently, invoke recovery and amendment retry, and assert a diagnostic error plus byte-identical audit, snapshots and live records. Keep the valid-orphan cleanup and crash-before-plan-swap recovery regressions. Verify a malformed row cannot reduce the cap count or make an already-applied dispute appear new.<br><br>
<a id="ver-15"></a>**VER-15 — A foreground startup error abandons the already-running pressure worker**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** process ownership/lifecycle architecture; incomplete cleanup abstraction. <br><br> **Evidence:** `commands/pressure/terminal.rs:35-55` starts background Codex, then uses `?` on `run_claude_foreground`. That call can fail before Claude even starts: `claude/session.rs:116-128` creates/cleans the marker path and spawns the executable, propagating each error. On that return, the local `CodexRun` is dropped. `pressure/codex_run.rs:50-73` contains only a thread handle and atomic PID, with no cancellation/Drop ownership; its worker owns a `Child` and waits in `run_attempts:135-166`. Dropping the join handle detaches the thread; it neither stops nor reaps the external process on behalf of the failed pipeline. `pressure/mod.rs:91-100` removes the report after a failed step while that worker can still recreate it. <br><br> **Observable result:** a missing/unspawnable Claude executable or marker filesystem error reports that pressure failed while Codex continues consuming resources and writing its review. In a longer-lived caller, the detached worker can even advance to a fallback model. In the terminal binary, returning and exiting does not supply a child shutdown protocol. The report cleanup is therefore not final and subsequent pressure work can read stale/unowned output. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** make cancellation and joining part of background-run ownership, and explicitly cancel/reap on every foreground error before deleting output. Use an owned child/process-group control mechanism that cannot race a fallback spawn; the current instantaneous PID alone is insufficient because it is zero between attempts. A shared cancellation flag plus synchronized child ownership/process-group cleanup is preferable to an uncoordinated `kill(current_pid())`. Keep normal pressure concurrency, fallback diagnostics and foreground TTY ownership. Ensure the worker cannot launch another candidate after cancellation and that errors preserve the original foreground failure. <br><br> **Consumers/acceptance:** terminal pressure driver, CodexRun/run_attempts and shared pipeline cleanup. Shared runner API consumers need compatible shutdown handling, but the excluded planner driver itself is not audited here. A controlled fake Codex remains active while Claude startup fails; the call must return only after Codex and its owned descendants have stopped, no fallback may start, and the report must not reappear after cleanup. Existing normal fallback/join tests remain valid.<br><br>
<a id="ver-16"></a>**VER-16 — Pressure record replacement can overwrite a concurrent plan edit**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR (lost plan edits). **Lenses:** durable-write ownership; misleading concurrency contract. <br><br> **Evidence:** `pressure/record.rs:134-153` reads the plan and constructs replacement text. `same_file:174-176` compares only device/inode, and `replace_plan:242-250` checks that identity before a separate rename. An editor that updates the same inode after the read is invisible to both identity checks; the final rename overwrites those newer bytes. An atomic editor replacement between the last check and rename also passes the check and is overwritten. The comment at lines 132-133 promises replaced plans return `PlanMoved`, but the check is not conditional replacement. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** coordinate Loom's own record/amendment writers with a stable per-plan lock across read/rewrite/replace, and detect content changes since the snapshot before publishing. Decide and document the boundary for external editors: an advisory lock protects only cooperating writers, and an extra metadata check alone does not make compare-and-rename atomic. If a non-cooperating edit cannot be protected by the chosen publication mechanism, fail conservatively or use a separately stored review record rather than claiming unconditional edit preservation. Avoid locking the replaceable plan inode as the sole coordination mechanism. <br><br> **Constraints/acceptance:** preserve the existing no-follow/nonblocking regular-file reads, 8 MiB cap, retained source handle, sibling temp creation, permissions, sync and prose/CRLF placement rules. Tests at `pressure/record/tests.rs:262-335` cover ordinary replacement, symlink/missing paths and directory permissions; add deterministic interleavings for an in-place edit after read, another Loom record writer, and replacement before publication. A conflict must preserve the newer plan and clearly report a skipped/conflicted record, with no leftover temp file. No arbitrary retries that overwrite newly changed user text.<br><br>
<a id="ver-17"></a>**VER-17 — Re-freezing overwrites authoritative copies before committing the new record**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** architecture, invalid atomicity assumptions. <br><br> **Evidence:** `loom/src/verify/contracts/store.rs:246-287` (`refreeze_set`) loads the existing record, calls `copy_files` at265, and only later replaces `freeze.json` at283. `copy_files:323-333` writes into the existing `files/<path>` namespace. Its `safe_locked_write_in_workdir` dependency (`fs/safe_fs.rs:335-353`) truncates the existing inode and writes/fsyncs it; neither a per-file flock nor the contracts-directory lock makes the multi-file update transactional. <br><br> **Trigger and consequence:** start with frozen A/B and their old hashes, accept a dispute that changes both, then fail while copying B or publishing the record (or stop the process after copying A). The old record still names A's old hash but its only frozen copy now contains new or partially written bytes. `commands/stage/contracts/restore.rs:76-92` checks that hash and refuses restoration. The original authoritative snapshot has been lost even though the new freeze did not commit. A retry may complete from the live worktree, but does not preserve the old committed freeze during failure or guarantee the same bytes after the worktree changes. Initial freeze has no prior snapshot to destroy; the defect is the replacement transaction. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** stage a complete immutable replacement generation, validate all copy hashes, make it durable, then atomically publish a record that identifies that generation. Keep the prior generation until publication succeeds and readers can finish. Alternatively use an explicit recoverable transaction with old copies retained; merely replacing each file atomically still leaves a mixed generation. Keep this ownership in the freeze store rather than reproducing transaction logic in adjudication and restore. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** `orchestrator/adjudication/apply_contract.rs:39-69` accepted-dispute re-freeze; `load_freeze`, `frozen_file_path`, restore, completion, contract display and any direct `files/` readers. Update the record format/compatibility reader and all copy-path consumers together. Preserve legacy version1 reads, path validation, no-follow/bounded I/O, stage→contracts lock order, recorded cwd/adapter semantics, dropped-path reporting and safe orphan collection. The comment at `store.rs:12-15` establishes existence ordering only; document hash/generation consistency explicitly after repair. The retry claim at `apply_contract.rs:36-38` also needs the transaction's actual assumptions. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** inject failure after the first replacement copy and just before record publication; restart, then prove every file named by the committed record still has its committed bytes and can be restored. Exercise successful replacement, dropped files, legacy records and post-publication orphan cleanup. Readers must observe a whole old or whole new generation.<br><br>
<a id="ver-18"></a>**VER-18 — Acceptance cache has two incomplete shell parsers beside the existing lexer**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** DRY, architecture, excessive implementation. <br><br> **Evidence/trigger:** `criteria/cache_ignore.rs:37-58` splits on whitespace and strips quotes independently from each word. For `cat 'ignored data.txt'`, where the actual file is ignored, it probes fragments such as `data.txt'` rather than the shell's single filename. The ignored input is absent from `cache_manifest.rs`, so editing only that file leaves the fingerprint unchanged; a previously passing stdout assertion can be replayed without reading its changed contents. This is a false cache hit, not merely reduced cache effectiveness. <br><br> Separately `criteria/cache_executable.rs:89-112,201-220` splits shell text on whitespace and recognizes separators only as complete words or suffixes. A valid command such as `printf ok;tool` records only the builtin and shell/containment executable, omitting the adjacent second executable. Updating that external tool can leave the key unchanged. Existing tests cover spaced wrappers and simple quoted paths, not these token boundaries. Both helpers coexist with `plan/schema/validation/shell_lex.rs`, already used by `cache::runs_git` and `testrun::recognize`. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** use the existing lexer's decoded words, control tokens and substitution metadata as the shared lexical owner. Keep cache policy local: reject unsupported/dynamic syntax rather than pretending every lexed command is understood. Decode quoted/escaped filenames before the ignored-input query; handle redirection operands and option-attached paths conservatively. Use command-position walking over real control tokens for executable resolution, retaining the existing env/default-PATH restrictions. Do not replace these two helpers with another generic shell interpreter. <br><br> **Consumers/constraints:** all cached acceptance commands, contract probes that reuse certified passes, CLI check and host completion. Preserve pinned checkout Git, original command digest, confinement/environment hashing, ignored-path fail-closed errors and deliberate rejection of external inputs. Bump the cache input or record revision so entries certified under incomplete parsing cannot be reused after repair. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** a quoted or escaped-space ignored input must bypass cache, including a criterion with stdout assertions; mutate only that ignored file and observe a fresh failing execution. Adjacent `;`, `&&`, pipeline and redirection syntax must either fingerprint every executable/input needed or be explicitly uncacheable. Keep existing env-wrapper/default-PATH cases and single-quoted metacharacter cases distinct from actual operators.<br><br>
<a id="ver-19"></a>**VER-19 — Fingerprinting's byte budget is not enforced during the read**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** architecture, invalid boundary comment. <br><br> **Evidence:** `criteria/cache_fingerprint.rs:111-142` (`digest_regular_file`) deducts the pre-open metadata length from `remaining`, opens with no-follow/nonblocking flags, then reads until EOF. It tests bytes read and regular-file metadata only after the loop. A file that grows after the metadata snapshot is read beyond the stated64MiB budget before the function returns a miss; a continually extended file can keep this optional cache step busy. The comment promises reading “within remaining bytes,” which the loop does not enforce. `cache_manifest.rs:206-260` uses it for dirty/untracked worktree files, so this is not limited to administrator-owned executable replacement. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** verify the opened descriptor is regular before reading, enforce a checked byte limit inside the loop (at most the admitted length plus a detection byte), and return a cache miss immediately on growth/identity uncertainty. Reuse the existing bounded-open/read policy where practical; a streaming digest helper may retain its own loop to avoid materializing all bytes. Keep metadata/identity checks after reading as well: an early bound does not replace them. <br><br> **Consumers/constraints:** changed checkout file blob IDs and executable digests; preserve SHA1/SHA256 blob header semantics, aggregate budget accounting, no-follow/O_NONBLOCK, conservative miss behavior and pinned Git. Cross-reference VER-09 and CTX-12 for the shared bounded-I/O owner; this consumer already has O_NONBLOCK, so its defect is the delayed bound/type enforcement. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** supply a descriptor whose content becomes larger than the initial admitted length and assert an immediate miss after the limit rather than EOF; include a growing stream/special-file substitution through a controlled fake reader or injection point. Unchanged files within budget must hash identically.<br><br>
<a id="ver-20"></a>**VER-20 — Probe projection drops the executor's incomplete-output verdict**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** architecture, duplicated result interpretation/model drift. <br><br> **Evidence:** `criteria/executor.rs:102-131,200-230` records incomplete capture for truncation, read failure or collection timeout; `criterion_eval.rs:51-56` explicitly fails such a criterion. `criteria/probe.rs:12-24,41-59` projects its result into `ProbeRun` without `output_complete` or the evaluated failure. Contract completion then reparses the retained text (`contracts/completion.rs:235-309`) and impact testing does the same (`impact_tests/runs.rs:82-125`). <br><br> **Trigger/consequence:** a command exits0 but prints more than10MiB, or its output collection fails. Acceptance rejects it as incomplete. Through `CriteriaProbe`, a retained passing summary—or unparsed text with exit0—passes the contract/impact check. Missing diagnostic/test output has become trusted success across an adapter boundary. Failed-build sharing can also infer a compiler rejection from an incomplete prefix. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** carry capture completeness as a mandatory typed property of the shared probe result (or retain the underlying execution result), and check it before parsing, cache handling or failed-build sharing. Contract completion should fail with an incomplete-capture diagnostic; impact should produce its existing inconclusive/cannot-evaluate outcome, with downstream policy preserved. Do not use mere exit0 or marker substring detection to reconstruct a property the executor already knows. Preserve certified-cache tails as a distinct intentionally partial diagnostic representation: their stored verdict/count is the authority. <br><br> **Consumers/constraints:** `CriteriaProbe`, contract `judge`/`compile_rejected`, impact `judge`, base/worktree comparison, and all replay/fake-runner constructors in completion and impact tests. Keep intentional raw nonzero contract testing, fallback behavior for complete-but-unparsed runner output, certified cache counts and process-retirement semantics. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** an exit0 result with a passing test prefix and incomplete capture must never pass either gate; an incomplete compiler-error prefix must not be shared as a conclusive build rejection. Exercise truncation and read-error/collection-timeout representations, plus ordinary complete passes and certified cached diagnostic tails. <br><br> **Supplement: VER-20 additional consumer — freeze consumes the same incomplete probe output.** <br><br> `commands/stage/contracts/freeze/prepared.rs:65–94` calls `run_spec_in_optional_tree`, handles timeout, then parses `stdout`/`stderr` and computes `compile_rejected`. Like the completion/impact consumers in VER-20, it has no output-completeness signal because `ProbeRun` dropped it. `freeze.rs:96–134` may accept the retained prefix as failed/build-failed, or accept an unparsed nonzero exit; shared failed-build handling can propagate that incomplete evidence. Extend the VER-20 repair and regression matrix to `run_prepared` and freeze's build-sharing path. Preserve the deliberate complete-output/nonzero-unparsed fallback policy; truncated or unreadable capture must be distinguished from genuinely complete but unrecognized output.<br><br>
<a id="ver-21"></a>**VER-21 — Major: review freshness treats executable multiline-string edits as comments**<br><br>**Lenses:** correctness through an excessive heuristic; architecture of the freshness certificate. <br><br> **Evidence:** `verify/review/prose.rs:20-28` drops every blank line and whole line whose trimmed text begins `//` (Rust/JS/TS/Go) or `#` (Python), without lexical context. `gate.rs:176-183` accepts unequal content fingerprints when these behaviour maps match and the base is unchanged. The comment at prose:20-21 explicitly acknowledges strings; this is a present bypass, not merely a future parser limitation. A reviewed Rust raw string containing a line `// old payload` can change to `// new payload`; a JS template or Python triple-quoted string has the same problem. Blank lines inside any such string can also be added/deleted. Runtime data changes, but the review remains current. An ordinary changed code file suffices for the required nonempty behaviour map. <br><br> **Repair and owner:** keep the exact content fingerprint as the authoritative default. The smallest safe repair disables source-comment normalization until existing language parsers can prove changed ranges are ordinary comments/irrelevant whitespace. If retained, reuse lexical information from the existing source parsing owner rather than grow a hand-maintained delimiter/pragma recognizer here. Documentation-path exemptions need their own explicit policy; they do not justify treating string contents as comments. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** update `fingerprint_from`, `covers_review`, harvested round compatibility and the gate/prose tests. Old persisted normalized behaviour hashes cannot safely authorize the repaired exemption: version the normalization evidence or require exact matching for legacy rounds. Preserve base equality, exact-match fast path, protected agent/skill/fixture Markdown and pragma sensitivity. Do not make any promise that a line comment never affects tool behavior. <br><br> **Acceptance:** a stored round followed only by a multiline-string payload/blank-line change must become stale for Rust raw strings, JS/TS templates, Python triples and Go raw strings; truly ordinary comments may remain exempt only when proven by the selected implementation. Existing exact matching and malformed-round handling remain unchanged.<br><br>
<a id="ver-22"></a>**VER-22 — Major: Go benchmark flags falsely make a full test run scoped**<br><br>**Lenses:** schema/adapter drift and shared-policy correctness. <br><br> **Evidence:** `testrun/adapters/go_test.rs:47-55,98-108` lists `-bench` as a test-filter flag and returns false for any invocation carrying it. `tests/integration/hooks_subagent_verify_guard_cases.rs:329-332` explicitly allows `go test -bench . -benchtime 1s` on that premise. Benchmark selection does not restrict regular tests: the installed Go primary implementation, `/usr/local/go/src/testing/testing.go:2337-2355`, invokes `runTests` independently before `runBenchmarks`; regular matching uses `-test.run` at 2460. Thus `go test ./... -bench .` runs the whole test tree plus benchmarks while the guard/lint sees a scoped command. The plain-current-package form pinned by the test also remains a full package run under this adapter's documented definition. <br><br> **Repair and owner:** remove benchmark selection from the flags that prove regular-test scope in the Go adapter. Parse its argument as a value-taking flag, including `-benchtime`, so option values do not become fake package operands. Preserve real `-run`/`-test.run` filtering and package scope. Do not add another Go-specific rule in the hook or plan lints. Audit `-fuzz` separately against pre-fuzz regular-test execution; that extension is not required to establish the benchmark defect. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** shared test-runner classification drives the verification guard, `plan/schema/validation_suite.rs:58` standard-stage warnings, and `validation/v2_lints/contracts.rs:218-240` integration-verify full-suite requirements. Fix the incorrect allow-table expectation and adapter tests together; retain exit/output parsing and generated single-test commands. <br><br> **Acceptance:** unfiltered `go test -bench .`, `go test ./... -bench=.` and their benchtime forms classify full; an explicit regular-test filter or already-supported narrower package remains scoped. Verify the guard and both plan lint directions consume the same answer. No Go test process was run during this audit.<br><br>
<a id="ver-23"></a>**VER-23 — Minor: unify dependency predicates and correct their cache contract**<br><br>**Lenses:** architecture, DRY, unnecessary implementation and stale comments. <br><br> **Evidence:** `verify/transitions/state.rs:220-310` and `313-371` independently implement the same dependency gate: final/merged state, knowledge/assumed-merge exceptions, missing completed commit and target ancestry. The first returns diagnostic detail, the second repeats the reads and decisions for a boolean. This duplication already obscures error semantics: the cache comment at 385-386 promises transient git errors return Err and are never cached, while the boolean function catches ancestry errors and returns Ok(false), which 417-427 caches for 60 seconds. Comments at 99-119, 134 and the error at 153 still call WaitingForDeps/Queued Pending/Ready, and the long gate explanation is attached to DependencyBlock instead of the predicate. <br><br> **Repair and owner:** make a single small dependency evaluation return satisfied, a typed blocking reason, or an operational error; derive the boolean and diagnostic formatting from it. Keep first-dependency order, knowledge/assumed-merge exceptions, phantom-merge refusal and the diagnostic distinction between self-resolving and operator-needed states. Choose and document the intended transient-error retry rule; do not silently preserve the contradictory promise. Cache only outcomes explicitly eligible for caching, with work-directory/target/dependency identity in its key if the public helper remains reusable across contexts. <br><br> **Consumers and acceptance:** scheduler spawn guard, trigger_dependents and status diagnostics must agree for every existing status, missing commit and ancestry result. Preserve trigger_dependents' fresh locked status/dependency recheck. Add a focused transient ancestry-error case that establishes whether the next poll retries. Existing real-git and diagnostic suites supply the other behavior cases. Trim argument/return boilerplate while retaining locking and git-history constraints.<br><br>
<a id="ver-24"></a>**VER-24 — Minor: share the host-job contract fixture instead of copied environment setup**<br><br>**Lenses:** DRY and excessive implementation. <br><br> **Evidence:** `tests/daemon_completion_contracts/support.rs:1-5` explicitly says its fixture is copied from host verification; both that file and `tests/host_verification_contracts/support.rs` independently define Fixture, isolate_git, git, repository/worktree creation, stage/session/PID records, plan/config writing, scratch setup and session_loom. The meaningful variations are the job kind and completion-specific helpers. Gate-dedup already reuses the host verification support module, demonstrating a workable shared owner. Separately `verify/transitions/tests/dependency_satisfaction_real_git.rs:25-126` repeats unchecked Command::output calls: spawn success is treated as git success, so setup failures can surface as misleading ancestry results. <br><br> **Repair and owner:** extract the existing common host-job fixture into test support used by both completion and verification contracts; keep kind-specific record constructors and scenario methods near their tests. Reuse a checked, isolated git helper for the real-ancestry fixture. Do not build a generic test-fixture framework or combine independent regression assertions. <br><br> **Constraints and acceptance:** retain private HOME/XDG lifetime, real process start-time attestation, symlinked shared state, host/session environment separation, quoting of acceptance commands and real executable selection. Both contract suites and gate-dedup retain the same scenario observables; setup failures must name git argv/stderr immediately. This is a maintainability proposal, not a claim that those suites failed in this audit.<br><br>

## Stage administration and adjudication

<a id="ver-r01"></a>**VER-R01 — CRITICAL — Replay identity depends on the spelling of an authenticated MAC**<br><br>**Lenses:** correctness at an authorization boundary; implementation simplification by canonical identity. <br><br> **Evidence:** `loom/src/commands/stage/admin_proof.rs:159–177` parses the MAC using `hex::decode`, accepting upper-, lower-, and mixed-case hex. `verify_and_consume_admin_proof:135–156` compares the decoded bytes to the expected HMAC, so those spellings authenticate identically. `consume_replay_marker:204–218` hashes the canonical request message followed by the *raw proof string*. A valid proof consumed once can therefore be submitted again with a letter in its MAC changed from lower to upper case; authentication succeeds and the replay filename differs. A MAC with several hexadecimal letters admits many equivalent spellings. This is a static, direct data-flow finding, not a claim that an unauthorized caller can forge the original proof. <br><br> **Consumers/consequence:** `complete_authorization.rs:15–25,28–53` uses this verifier for privileged stage completion; `daemon/server/client.rs:70–84` uses it for the daemon-stop capability. The intended one-use grant can be reused for the same exact action, stage, flags and nonce. Existing `commands/stage/tests/admin_proof.rs:60–72` checks only byte-identical replay; binding and symlink tests do not cover equivalent encodings. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** define one accepted/consumed identity for the authenticated request and nonce. Either require canonical fixed-length lowercase MAC encoding before verification and consumption, or derive the marker from authenticated binary/canonical fields rather than presentation text. Preserve atomic `create_new` consumption, exact action/flag/stage binding, owner-only replay storage, bounded no-follow credential reads, and consumption before action. Treat already issued proofs/markers explicitly during rollout: normal minted lowercase markers can retain their current identity, while a credential rotation or proof-version transition can invalidate historical mixed-case tokens without guessing which spelling was previously consumed. Do not merely add another independently named replay marker per accepted spelling. <br><br> **Acceptance:** a minted proof and variants changing one/all MAC letters to uppercase cannot authorize more than one operation collectively, including concurrent submissions. Cover both completion and daemon-stop request shapes; wrong stage/action/flags remain rejected without consuming a valid proof. Verify migration behavior for pre-change lowercase markers and the chosen policy for older noncanonical proofs. No test was executed during this audit.<br><br>
<a id="ver-r02"></a>**VER-R02 — MAJOR — Handwritten JSON display fails to escape strings**<br><br>**Lenses:** DRY and excessive/non-idiomatic implementation, with observable serialization errors. `commands/stage/output.rs:160–171` manually matches every `serde_json::Value` variant. Its string arm uses `format!("\"{s}\"")`, so quotes, backslashes, newlines and control characters are emitted without JSON escaping. `get:90–108` explicitly exposes this formatter for scripting; `set:58–60` and `list:79–85` also consume it. Setting a JSON string containing a newline and then getting it yields a literal multi-line quoted value rather than valid JSON. Simple string tests at208–214 cannot detect this. <br><br> **Repair:** use `Value`'s existing JSON serialization (`value.to_string()`) for all variants; remove the duplicated per-variant renderer and redundant `serde_json::to_string`/fallback pair. This preserves the already quoted-string display contract while correcting escaping. In `parse_value:128–157`, the explicit true/false/null checks after successful-JSON parsing are unreachable fallbacks and can be removed separately. Preserve the non-JSON numeric fallback deliberately: forms such as a leading plus or leading zeros may be accepted by the numeric parser although they are invalid JSON, so replacing the entire parser with `serde_json::from_str(...).unwrap_or(String)` would change behavior. <br><br> **Acceptance:** `serde_json::from_str(format_value(value)) == value` for strings containing quotes, backslashes, newline/tab, Unicode and for scalar/container values; cover the actual output-get representation as well as the helper. Preserve existing plain-string input and non-JSON numeric compatibility unless the CLI deliberately changes its documented policy. No new serializer or abstraction is needed.<br><br>
<a id="ver-r03"></a>**VER-R03 — MAJOR — Merge retry admission and its counter update are separate decisions**<br><br>**Lenses:** architecture/DRY of transition ownership and incorrect concurrency boundary. `commands/stage/merge.rs:120–155` obtains `RetryPreflight`, checks `stage.is_at_fix_limit()` on that snapshot, then uses `update_stage` only to increment the fresh counter. The closure neither rechecks the limit nor calls the allowed-state predicate in `merge/preflight.rs:41–66`. Two retries observing count 2/max 3 can both pass, then serialize increments to 3 and 4 and both attempt the merge. A stage finalized between preflight and the closure still gets its counter changed. The comment claiming the count is accurate explains a fresh read but omits that admission must also use it. Existing `merge/tests.rs:100–130` only tests counter and limit methods separately. <br><br> **Repair:** keep filesystem/worktree/active-merge preflight before charging, but perform the authoritative current-status and current-limit checks together with the increment in one stage-lock closure. Reuse the same merge-state predicate for the early diagnostic and the locked admission; return the resulting fresh stage to the merge path rather than retaining every stale field except the count. `skip_retry.rs:268–320` already demonstrates a comparable locked revalidation pattern; share a small domain admission operation if needed, not a generic transaction framework. Preserve the allowed Completed-but-unmerged manual landing case and refuse already-final stages. Do not hold the stage lock throughout slow git work; existing proof/claim checks still guard later outcome writes. <br><br> **Acceptance:** two synchronized retries competing for the final available attempt admit exactly one and leave the count at the cap; moving the stage to final merged between preflight and admission refuses without a counter write or merge attempt. Current configured cap and state determine admission. A rejected filesystem preflight consumes nothing. Preserve valid conflict/blocked/unmerged-completed retries and existing ancestry/control-path protection.<br><br>
<a id="ver-r04"></a>**VER-R04 — MAJOR — Completion tests discard the verdicts they claim to verify**<br><br>**Lenses:** excessive/redundant test implementation and invalid/verbose comments, with missing regression protection. `commands/stage/tests/complete_router.rs:137,201,255,341,382` evaluates `matches!(route, ...)` as a discarded expression. These tests pass for any successful returned route, including a Proceed result in cases named as refusals. At385–389 `_unused_session_compiles` tests only that a standard constructor compiles. `commands/stage/merge/tests.rs:57–95` writes a stage fixture and asserts a locally repeated `matches!` predicate rather than calling production admission, while `merge/preflight.rs:116–149` already exercises the real predicate. <br><br> `commands/stage/tests/complete.rs:140–175` is named `test_complete_with_passing_acceptance`, claims “We assert that acceptance ran successfully (no panic before merge)”, and then ignores `result`. Its fixture has no git repository/worktree; `complete_pipeline.rs:147–158` resolves execution paths and captures a verification snapshot before calling acceptance. The test cannot establish that acceptance ran, and its long commentary about possible Ok/Err and former NoBranch behavior conceals that missing observation. The analogous no-repository standard-routing case at235–271 also discards the result and describes a later merge path it need not reach. `tests/progressive_complete.rs:31–75` and `tests/complete.rs:38–52` further ignore setup git exit status, allowing fixture failures to resemble intended refusal scenarios. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** assert every router predicate, remove constructor-only/repeated-local-predicate tests covered by real production tests, and make completion tests observe their intended boundary. Use an existing real committed worktree fixture and an acceptance side effect or narrowly injected executor to prove execution, then assert the specific expected result/state. If a test is only about refusing an invalid repository, rename it and assert that error rather than claiming passing acceptance. Reuse the checked, isolated git fixture utility proposed in VER-24. Replace historical/conditional narratives with the current invariant and fixture reason. Keep valid control-path, ancestry, race, knowledge and relay cases; this is targeted repair, not wholesale test deletion. <br><br> **Acceptance:** changing a refusal route to Proceed fails the corresponding router test; suppressing acceptance execution fails the acceptance test; an unsuccessful git fixture command fails at setup with diagnostics. Tests call the production admission predicate and do not retain separate copies of the policy. Existing actual race and privileged-path tests remain intact. No tests were run in this review.<br><br>
<a id="ver-r05"></a>**VER-R05 — MAJOR: retrying a verdict can spend the same evidence round repeatedly**<br><br>**Evidence.** `loom/src/orchestrator/adjudication/apply.rs:80–118` calls application again whenever `applied.marker` is absent. Its empty `.applying` file is written best-effort, is never tested as an exclusive claim, and carries no recovery state. `persist_verdict_result` at 159–180 writes the stage first and the applied marker afterward. `apply_needs_more_evidence` at 321–333 increments the freshly loaded evidence count; `apply_freeze_refusal.rs:90–109` does the same for refusal questions. `scan.rs:58–103` finds verdicts solely by verdict/applied/closed files, without requiring the stage still be NeedsAdjudication. <br><br> **Failure.** A crash or marker-write failure after a successful stage write leaves a replayable verdict whose evidence increment already persisted. The next application loads that increment and charges it again. Repeated replay can exhaust MAX_EVIDENCE_ROUNDS without any new question/answer round. Feedback is overwritten, not appended; the defect is the counter and lifecycle replay, not duplicated feedback text. The existing idempotency test in `tests_verdicts.rs:60–84` only repeats a fully completed application whose marker exists. <br><br> **Repair and owner.** Make adjudication application an explicitly durable, idempotent operation keyed by dispute/verdict identity. Commit the evidence charge and its consumed-operation identity together in the stage transaction; recovery can then finish secondary effects/marker publication without charging again. Reuse the existing amendment idempotency keys for patch effects; do not infer that they make counters idempotent. Either give `.applying` a defined recovery role or remove it. Preserve sibling-dispute holds, the five-round limit, feedback replacement, and contract-refusal semantics. Consumers include direct apply, periodic pending-verdict apply, invalid-deferral questions, and freeze-refusal questions. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Interrupt after stage persistence but before applied-marker publication, restart and replay repeatedly: one verdict spends exactly one round and reaches the same state as uninterrupted application. Test both question paths, marker write failure, and two actual distinct verdicts. Rewrite the “Idempotent under crash recovery” and “taking the .applying lock” comments only after the mechanism supports them.<br><br>
<a id="ver-r06"></a>**VER-R06 — MAJOR: verdict recording and application lack a shared lifecycle claim**<br><br>**Evidence.** `record.rs:168–197` checks stage status, closed state and verdict absence without holding their common mutation boundary; `session.rs:287–317` subsequently publishes with plain `std::fs::write`, which can overwrite a competing recorder's verdict. The recording module claims a recorded verdict cannot be overwritten. During application, `apply.rs:87–93` checks closed/applied and loads the stage once, then runs effects. Its later stage update at 159–170 overwrites the fresh status with the earlier computed verdict status and explicitly force-transitions when the fresh status does not allow that move. It also assigns the earlier evidence count. The long comment at 125–150 rationalizes forcing a transition against an “intermediate” state without establishing that the writer still owns that state. <br><br> **Failure.** Two recording callers can both pass the no-verdict guard and the second replaces the first. A concurrent reset/escalation after an application has loaded the stage can be overwritten when that application persists its stale target status. A closed-marker check performed only before effects does not protect this interval. `requeue.rs:12–27` and escalation tests deliberately preserve another verdict/operator's later status, but that intention is defeated by the final forced write. These are distinct from runtime RT-02's reset-side ownership check. <br><br> **Repair and owner.** Define one adjudication lifecycle operation shared by record, apply, close and operator transitions. Use the existing dispute-lock-before-stage-lock order; validate current dispute generation/claim and allowed state under that boundary. Publish a complete verdict atomically without replacing an existing record, and compare the authoritative application identity before stage effects commit. Avoid holding a stage lock through slow Git/amendment execution: prepare effects, then commit against the retained claim, with explicit recovery for external effects. A bare status check is insufficient for legitimate crash replay after the stage already became Queued; combine this with VER-R05's consumed-operation identity. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Race two different recorders: exactly one complete verdict wins and the loser gets “already recorded.” Pause application after its initial read, reset/close or escalate the stage, then resume: no old verdict revives or overwrites the newer lifecycle. Preserve sibling-dispute sequencing and restart recovery. Replace the unconditional force fallback with an explicit, documented recovery case only when ownership is proven.<br><br>
<a id="ver-r07"></a>**VER-R07 — MAJOR: an unreadable request changes the meaning of its verdict**<br><br>**Evidence.** `apply_kinds.rs:119–141` converts every `read_request` error to `None` and deliberately falls back to criterion handling. Reject routing immediately above chooses restore/requeue or notice/requeue for contract, integrity, impact and freeze-refusal disputes, but the fallback calls criterion rejection, which escalates to human review. A malformed or temporarily unreadable request therefore changes policy rather than postponing application; successful fallback then gets an applied marker. <br><br> **Repair and owner.** Load the typed request once at the application boundary and carry its kind through the operation. Propagate read/parse errors through bounded apply-failure handling instead of selecting another domain policy. If truly missing legacy requests must remain supported, recognize an explicit legacy record/version whose meaning is known; valid older requests already have model compatibility handling and do not justify treating arbitrary I/O errors as Criterion. This also removes repeated request reads during kind-specific effects. <br><br> **Acceptance.** For each non-criterion kind, make request reading fail after verdict recording. Application must not write feedback, alter stage status, restore/refreeze files or publish an applied marker under criterion semantics. Once the request is readable, the original kind's behavior must apply. Keep diagnostic context and apply-failure escalation.<br><br>
<a id="ver-r08"></a>**VER-R08 — MAJOR: persisted retry budgets are best-effort and reset on errors**<br><br>**Evidence.** `session.rs:214–253` reads the attempts file with `.ok().and_then(parse).unwrap_or(0)`, then logs write failures and still returns the incremented count. The caller can issue the job even though no durable attempt was spent. `mod.rs:291–322` implements the same read-default/write-best-effort pattern for verdict application failures. A truncated, malformed or unreadable counter reads as zero, and repeated failed writes keep returning the same small number. The registry holds no authoritative in-memory counter to compensate. The module comment at `session.rs:11–17` promises restart-stable budgets without this caveat. <br><br> **Repair and owner.** Give adjudication one small durable counter operation with typed errors and atomic replacement under the relevant lifecycle lock. Only missing, never-created counters default to zero; malformed/unreadable existing state needs a surfaced recovery/error path. A session slot must not be handed out until its charge persists. Application-failure accounting must either persist or stop further application with an observable error; logging cannot enforce a limit. Preserve separate per-dispute spawn and apply counters, current caps and restart semantics. Share the persistence mechanics, not the two policies. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Cover corrupt existing counters, write failure and restart after a successful charge; no free spawn or unbounded apply retry occurs. Existing happy-path tests in `session_tests.rs` and `tests_verdicts.rs` do not cover these cases. Liveness errors in `session.rs:269–286` are a separate consumer of runtime RT-31 and should adopt its strict unknown/error result.<br><br>
<a id="ver-r09"></a>**VER-R09 — MAJOR: adjudication evidence is assembled with a broken Git argument and a second plan parser**<br><br>**Evidence.** `prompt/sources.rs:31–53` validates a SHA-shaped evidence commit, then executes `git show --no-color --stat -p -- <commit>`. Git's `--` ends revision parsing: the commit is a pathspec, not the requested revision. Ordinarily there is no file with that hexadecimal name, so the requested diff is missing; a matching path would describe the default revision's path instead. The source comment incorrectly says this places the SHA in its intended positional slot, and tests at 132–167 only reject bad SHA shapes, never assert a successful requested diff. <br><br> The same file's `read_plan_excerpt/find_stage_block` at 74–125 reimplements metadata/YAML selection with substring searches. It finds the first arbitrary yaml fence and searches `id: s1` as a prefix, so a preceding `id: s10` can be quoted as s1. Quoted IDs and alternative legal indentation degrade differently. Its comment says this avoids “pulling in a full YAML parser” even though this crate already owns and uses the execution-plan parser. `run_listing` always lists the main project root while the prompt labels it “Worktree top-level files”; this can show a different tree from `ExecutionSite`. <br><br> **Repair and owner.** Put the validated revision before the path separator, preserving option-injection rejection and project-owned Git execution. Use the existing core-plan metadata/excerpt owner for exact stage identity and canonical markers; no new generic Markdown parser. Pass the resolved execution-site root to the listing or label it accurately as the main repository. Retain graceful “unavailable” diagnostics, evidence budgets and missing-worktree warnings. <br><br> **Acceptance.** A fixture with two commits and distinguishable content must quote the requested non-HEAD commit; malformed/dash-leading revisions stay rejected. An s10-before-s1 plan, quoted ID and unrelated earlier yaml fence must quote exactly s1. A file existing only in the stage worktree must appear under a worktree listing. These checks should exercise actual generated evidence rather than reconstructing the command's intended behavior.<br><br>

## Context, source graph and accounting

<a id="ctx-01"></a>**CTX-01 — Retrieval can compose an overlay with a different revision's base**<br><br>**Locations and symbols:** `loom/src/context/retrieve.rs:314-321` (`retrieve_for_stage`); `loom/src/context/retrieve/graph.rs:91-105` (`load_resolved_graph`); `loom/src/context/graph_store/mod.rs:250-277` (`GraphStore::resolved_with_schema`); `loom/src/context/view/store.rs:163-175` (`view_identity`). Producers: `loom/src/context/refresh/source_graph.rs:196-220,244-246,280-286` and `loom/src/context/refresh/source_graph/layer.rs:313-329`. <br><br> **Evidence and trigger:** <br><br> 1. An overlay records `tree.head` in `GraphLayer.revision`. Its persisted files are a delta against `load_base(tree.head)`: `persist_layer` drops entries equal to that base. <br> 2. The semantic freshness pointer is stored in the canonical main checkout's shared `state.json` (`ContextStore::open`, `store.rs:44-56`). Both base and overlay reconciliation stamp their revision into that one pointer. <br> 3. Retrieval passes `state.semantic.revision` into `load_resolved_graph`. That function loads the requested overlay, but ignores its `revision`, then calls `graph_store.view(semantic_revision, Some(overlay))`. <br> 4. `view_identity` combines the requested revision with only the overlay generation. `resolved_with_schema` loads the requested base and unconditionally applies that overlay; neither verifies that the two revisions match. <br><br> A concrete scenario is a stage overlay based on A while another checkout publishes B and leaves the shared semantic pointer at B. If a file unchanged by the stage differs between A and B, it was omitted from the overlay and retrieval reads B's version. The returned graph describes B plus A's delta, not the stage tree. No concurrent instruction timing is needed: the last publisher can remain B indefinitely. Conversely, a stage publication can change the pointer to A for a main checkout at B. A stale freshness label cannot make the mixed graph correct. <br><br> **Consequence:** source symbols, relationships, omissions, and ranking can be taken from a revision that the requesting checkout never contained. The view can be persisted under a formally valid identity that still represents the wrong base/delta combination. This can also make `loom map` and retrieval disagree: `commands/map.rs:326-343` uses the checkout snapshot's revision, whereas retrieval uses shared metadata. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair and concrete owner:** bind a graph read to the requested checkout/overlay snapshot before loading its view. An existing overlay's recorded base revision is the identity of the delta; do not substitute the shared latest-published revision. With no overlay, select the checkout's HEAD when available and explicitly label any deliberate older-base fallback. Put the base/overlay compatibility check in the graph read owner, so `view`, raw `resolved`, and future callers cannot construct mismatched layers. The desired mismatch policy may be return a typed degraded result or resolve against the recorded overlay base; it must never silently union mismatched revisions. <br><br> Migration sequence: introduce a small resolved-read descriptor carrying the selected base revision and loaded overlay; route retrieval through it; make view identity and composition consume the same descriptor; then adjust shared freshness presentation to describe what was actually served. Keep global metadata as published-cache information, not request identity. Reuse the already loaded overlay rather than reading it again in `view_identity` and yet again in a cold `resolved_with_schema`: these repeated reads currently add work and permit the validation and composition to inspect different overlay generations. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** `retrieve_for_stage` callers: knowledge context/eval, prompt retrieval (`commands/hook/user_prompt.rs:148-162`), worker briefs (`commands/hook/worker_brief.rs:165-178`), and signal-generation retrieval. Prompt stage selection is real: `commands/hook/target.rs:34-50` constructs `OverlayScope::Stage`; `user_prompt.rs:154-155` forwards it. Also review `GraphStore::view`, `resolved`, map loading, and snapshot materialization when introducing the compatibility invariant. <br><br> **Constraints:** preserve overlay-only answers when no base is available; missing-base degradation; whole-file replacement; deletion tombstones; immutable current bases; schema/parser checks; read-only cache fallback; stage/local separation; and the rule that prompt retrieval does not rebuild source layers. For legacy overlays with an empty revision, make degradation explicit rather than treating an empty value as proof of compatibility. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** build two bases A/B with an unchanged-by-stage file whose symbol differs; write an A overlay; set shared semantic state to B; retrieve in the A checkout and prove the B-only symbol never enters its graph/pack. Repeat with a B-only added file and an A tombstone. Exercise both warm persisted views and cold views. Verify base-missing overlay-only behavior remains usable and explicitly degraded as appropriate. Existing `graph_store/tests.rs:54-78` proves replacement only against one supplied base, and even uses an overlay with default empty revision; it does not establish revision compatibility. Add cross-checkout coverage in `context/tests/retrieve_source.rs` and compatibility tests at the shared graph owner. <br><br> **Deduplication:** any other report identifying a global semantic-revision pointer or wrong worktree source snapshot is the same root issue. CTX-03 concerns the separate knowledge catalog and should remain a distinct acceptance case.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-02"></a>**CTX-02 — A corrupt knowledge cache defeats the fallback intended to bypass it**<br><br>**Locations and symbols:** `loom/src/context/retrieve.rs:185-217` (`resolve_catalog`), `:229-247` (`evaluate_state`); `loom/src/context/refresh.rs:106-122` (`evaluate_inner`), `:228-234` (`refresh`); `loom/src/context/store.rs:90-104,117-129` (`load_catalog`, `load_state`). Recovery writer: `store.rs:164-182` (`update_state`). <br><br> **Evidence and trigger:** <br><br> • Malformed `state.json` makes `evaluate_inner` fail at its first `store.load_state()?`, so `refresh` fails before it can repair anything. <br> • `resolve_catalog` catches the error, successfully ingests the valid knowledge tree in memory, and immediately calls `evaluate_state`, which invokes `evaluate` and reaches the same failing `load_state` again. The query returns the cache parse error. <br> • With valid state naming a built structural revision but malformed `catalog.json`, the same loop occurs through `evaluate_inner`'s `store.load_catalog()?` at line 119. <br> • `update_state` has deliberate malformed-cache recovery, but these read failures happen before execution can reach it. `loom knowledge sync` also calls `refresh` directly (`commands/knowledge/sync.rs:50-57`), so normal sync cannot repair these cases. <br><br> **Consequence:** a derived artifact can disable retrieval even when all authoritative Markdown is readable; the fallback performs the full ingest and then discards its useful result. CLI retrieval fails; prompt retrieval converts the error to no pack and emits an abstention (`user_prompt.rs:156-161`). This is materially different from a missing knowledge tree, which is intentionally supported. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** make knowledge-cache resolution treat invalid derived catalog/state bytes as a cache miss with diagnostics, while continuing to propagate unreadable authoritative knowledge. Use the catalog and structural revision just built in memory to describe the served snapshot; load optional semantic freshness best-effort and mark it unavailable/unknown when its state cannot be read. Do not call the strict disk evaluator again after selecting the in-memory fallback. Let explicit sync repair malformed cache files through the same owner. Pair with CTX-03 so the repair does not add another load/evaluate path. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** all retrieval callers named in CTX-01; `loom knowledge sync`; structural refresh callers and cache-state diagnostics. Preserve strict parsing where an operator explicitly requests inspection of corrupt cache bytes, if such an API requires it; the derived-cache serving path should not inherit that failure policy accidentally. <br><br> **Constraints:** report the corruption; do not silently invent a current semantic revision; do not swallow authoritative file errors, unknown required IDs, or source window validation failures; do not require write access to answer the query. Existing best-effort graph-layer parsing (`graph_store/mod.rs:284-306`) is the local precedent, not a reason to build a generic persistence framework. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** after a normal query, corrupt `state.json`, then independently corrupt `catalog.json` with a valid built state. In both cases, retrieval must return the expected knowledge item with truthful freshness and diagnostics. Repeat with writes denied to ensure answers still work. Run explicit sync with writable malformed artifacts and assert the next load is valid. Keep the existing single-fingerprint successful-path regression (`context/tests/retrieve.rs:237-248`) and add actual fingerprint-count coverage for the fallback if its design makes that relevant. <br><br> **Comment evidence:** `retrieve.rs:178-180` promises, “A read-only query must never die because the cache is unwritable,” and says refresh failure falls back to an in-memory catalog. The implementation re-enters strict cache reads, so this promise is incomplete for unreadable/malformed derived artifacts. Correct the implementation and then state the exact fallback contract briefly.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-03"></a>**CTX-03 — Refresh publishes a catalog but returns no catalog, forcing a racy reload**<br><br>**Locations and symbols:** `loom/src/context/refresh.rs:106-148` (`evaluate_inner`), `:274-321` (`rebuild_and_persist`); `loom/src/context/retrieve.rs:198-209` (`resolve_catalog`); `loom/src/context/store.rs:107-138,164-182` (catalog/state persistence). Root selection: `retrieve.rs:149-164`, `store.rs:44-56`. <br><br> **Evidence and trigger:** the knowledge tree comes from the requesting worktree, while the derived cache is shared under the canonical main root. `rebuild_and_persist` builds catalog A, writes `catalog.json`, updates state separately, and returns freshness/report fields but drops the actual catalog. `resolve_catalog` subsequently loads whatever catalog is now at that shared path. Even a warm refresh reads the catalog in `evaluate_inner` to validate it, discards that object, then retrieval parses it again. <br><br> Permitted interleaving: <br><br>

```text
A: refresh knowledge tree A; save catalog A; stamp state A; return outcome A
B: refresh knowledge tree B; save catalog B; stamp state B
A: resolve_catalog loads catalog B; combines it with outcome A; ranks B

```

Every individual atomic write succeeds; directory locks around individual writes do not prevent this read-after-return sequence. A second interleaving lets A's catalog and B's state be the final persisted pair because catalog publication and state mutation acquire their locks separately. A later evaluation can detect the latter mismatch, but it does not protect A's already returned answer. Different worktrees make the mixed-content trigger concrete; a user editing knowledge between two same-checkout queries also suffices. <br><br> **Consequence:** a request can rank another tree's knowledge while reporting its own structural freshness. Required-ID rejection may depend on which process published last. Warm queries also deserialize the entire same catalog twice, and cold queries serialize an object only to immediately deserialize it. The extra I/O is an API consequence, not an essential freshness safeguard. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair and owner:** have one context snapshot operation return the exact validated/built `Catalog` together with its freshness; callers should use that owned value. A warm evaluation should retain the catalog it validated. A cold refresh should return the catalog it built. Preserve `RefreshOutcome` as a CLI projection if changing that public shape would unnecessarily broaden the patch. Make catalog/state publication a coherent operation at `ContextStore`: either one lock spans both writes and snapshot reads, or immutable revision-addressed catalogs are referenced by an atomic state pointer.
