The former is smaller; it must use already-locked write primitives rather than recursively calling `locked_write` under a non-reentrant directory lock. <br><br> Migration sequence: add the resolve-snapshot operation and tests; adapt `resolve_catalog` and `refresh`; remove the now-redundant post-refresh reload and fallback re-evaluation; then isolate persistence publication under its owner. Keep expensive fingerprinting and ingestion outside the critical section where possible, and return the request's snapshot even if a newer writer publishes after it. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** `retrieve_for_stage`, `refresh`, knowledge sync/eval, any direct `ContextStore` catalog/state writers, and source reconciliation's semantic-field update. The existing `update_state` behavior preserving concurrently changed semantic fields is necessary and must survive the change. <br><br> **Constraints:** no long-lived global process cache is required; different checkouts may legitimately request different knowledge revisions; cache failures cannot invalidate an already built snapshot; never overwrite semantic state with a pre-lock copy. Keep deterministic ranking and authoritative-tree error handling. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** use deterministic synchronization points around publication/return to interleave two distinct knowledge roots sharing one `ContextStore`. Assert each returned snapshot contains its own distinctive chunk, its catalog revision matches its data, and its structural revision matches its request. Verify a cold snapshot uses its built object without reopening `catalog.json`; a warm snapshot reads the catalog once. Concurrent semantic publication must survive a structural update. Keep the existing missing-catalog/revision validation cases. <br><br> **Deduplication:** this is the knowledge-snapshot transaction issue; it is related to CTX-02's duplicated resolution paths but is not fixed merely by ignoring parse errors. Avoid splitting the extra deserialization into an independent performance refactor before the ownership fix.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-04"></a>**CTX-04 — Two project-manifest detectors maintain incompatible language evidence**<br><br>**Locations and symbols:** `loom/src/language.rs:105-167` (`detect_project_languages`, `root_entry_extensions`); `loom/src/skills/project/markers.rs:6-27,84-145` (`FILE_MARKERS`, `detect`, `dotnet_project`, `detect_dependencies`); `loom/src/skills/project/scan.rs:53-89` (`discover`); `loom/src/skills/recommend.rs:12-43` (`for_files`). <br><br> **Evidence:** both systems detect languages from a directory's project manifests. Their traversal scopes differ legitimately, but the underlying evidence rules are duplicated and have diverged: <br><br>

| Same root evidence | `language::detect_project_languages` | Skill project markers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `package.json` with a TypeScript dependency, no `tsconfig.json` | JavaScript | TypeScript |
| `App.slnx` only | C# | No C# |
| `thing.gemspec` only | Ruby | No Ruby |
| `meson.build` only | C++ | No C++ |
| `setup.py` only | No Python | Python |
| `build.gradle.kts` only | Java | Kotlin |
The root detector also uses `Path::exists` and extension enumeration without checking regular-file type; markers explicitly reject symlink final components. Consequently a directory named `Cargo.toml` counts as Rust in one path and does not in the other. The two tests suites independently pin some of these differences: `language/tests.rs:179-202` covers `.slnx`, gemspec, and Meson; `skills/project/tests.rs:93-123` covers dependency-driven JS classification and malformed manifests. There is no shared evidence contract in these inspected tests. <br><br> **Consequence:** plan initialization's registry-domain suggestions (`commands/init/plan_setup.rs:144-170`) and the orchestrator's language fallback (`orchestrator/core/orchestrator.rs:187-207`) can disagree with package-scoped skill routing and `loom project`. Every new manifest format needs multiple updates, with different filesystem safety semantics. This is a maintainability finding with demonstrated divergence; Kotlin-versus-Java policy and the exact supported language set should be resolved deliberately, not silently normalized by a mechanical deduplication. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair and concrete boundary:** establish one single-directory project-evidence probe with regular-file/no-follow semantics and typed evidence. The existing `skills/project/markers.rs` + `probe` boundary is the concrete starting point; extract its language-only evidence into a neutral owner or expose a narrow projection usable by `language.rs`. Keep recursive discovery, package boundary handling, framework/infrastructure tags, and scoped recommendation logic in `skills::project`. The root-only detector should consume only that root's evidence, preserving its API and ordering; it must not start a recursive scan just to reuse the code. Reuse the existing `DIALECTS` source-extension mapping, already shared by `language_for_path`, rather than inventing a second registry. <br><br> Migration sequence: specify the shared fixtures and supported evidence rules; centralize the probe; adapt the two projections; remove duplicate manifest tables and root extension scanning; retain explicit projections for types `DetectedLanguage` cannot represent. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** initialization suggestions and orchestrator fallback above; skill recommendations; `commands/hook/project_types.rs`; `commands/project.rs`; project profile users in verification contract adapter resolution and plan contract/JS-provision linting. Their behavior must remain scoped to the appropriate directory/package. This is not authorization to alter sandbox-domain policy or adapter selection incidentally. <br><br> **Constraints:** preserve discovery depth/entry limits, deterministic ordering, unparseable-manifest behavior, symlink protections, CMake nested-project handling, and distinct root-only versus recursive semantics. Do not map every Kotlin project to Java merely because the old enum lacks Kotlin. <br><br> **Focused acceptance:** run both public entry points on one shared table of ordinary manifests, TS dependency-only packages, `.slnx`, gemspec, Meson, `setup.py`, Kotlin Gradle, directory-shaped manifests, and symlinks. Assert shared evidence agreement and explicitly documented projection differences. Existing monorepo/scoping tests must continue to prove root-only detection does not become recursive and nested package recommendations stay scoped. <br><br> **Supplement: CTX-04 additional consumer — census manifest evidence.** <br><br> `context/census/subprojects.rs:7-26` maintains a third build-manifest table for nearest-ancestor project grouping. It includes `setup.py` and `meson.build`, excludes `.slnx` and `.gemspec`, and duplicates the evidence already interpreted by language detection and skills selection. Reuse the proposed shared manifest-evidence classification while preserving this consumer’s tracked-path/nearest-ancestor traversal policy. It need not load or infer languages merely to group paths. Add census grouping cases to the cross-consumer marker matrix.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-05"></a>**CTX-05 — Graph-cache comments misstate the side-effect and concurrency boundary**<br><br>**Locations:** `loom/src/context/retrieve/graph.rs:39-78`; `loom/src/context/graph_store/mod.rs:194-197`; `loom/src/context/lexical_index/cache.rs:256-279` (verbosity-only example). This is a small cleanup with no behavioral redesign. <br><br> **Incorrect claim 1:** retrieval's graph loader says, “Retrieval itself never builds or refreshes this graph” and “this function only ever reads what a prior `loom map` run, or a merge, already wrote.” It does avoid source-layer extraction, but line 96 calls `GraphStore::view`; a view miss enters `view/store.rs:198-225`, resolves/relinks edges and persists a derived view. The same doc block earlier acknowledges that the resolved view can be built, so its broad read-only statement is internally misleading. <br><br> **Repair:** say precisely that retrieval does not rebuild source layers, while it may resolve and cache a missing derived view. Retain the distinction between local/stage overlays and the missing-base fallback. The approximately 40-line loader explanation repeats `degraded_reason`'s immediately following explanation; give the loader a short side-effect contract and leave the detailed empty-base/overlay truth table with the predicate that implements it. <br><br> **Incorrect claim 2:** `save_overlay` says overlays are private to the stage, “so no other reader can observe a torn write.” Stage ownership of writes does not make readers absent. Retrieval and view consumers read the same file. The actual torn-write protection is `write_layer`'s `locked_write`/atomic replacement (`graph_store/mod.rs:310-322`), not privacy. <br><br> **Repair:** state that each stage owns its overlay writes and replacement is atomic for concurrent readers. This matters while implementing CTX-01: neither stage privacy nor atomic replacement guarantees two separate reads see the same generation. <br><br> **Verbosity example:** `lexical_index/cache.rs:256-279` gives multiple paragraphs of admonitions, emphatic capitalization, approximate repository node counts, and unbenchmarked microseconds-versus-milliseconds comparisons for a straightforward deterministic hash loop. Keep the required ordering, hashed inputs, invalidation reason, and truncated-digest choice; remove repository-size/performance anecdotes and repeated warnings. Do not remove the parser-version requirement or change the hash algorithm as part of comment cleanup. <br><br> **Affected consumers and constraints:** maintainers of retrieval, cache/view persistence, and source lexical-cache invalidation. Comments only; preserve every behavior and test. Validate documentation against the named call paths and ensure intra-doc links remain accurate; no new test is needed solely for prose changes. <br><br> **Deduplication:** fold any broader graph/comment cleanup into this targeted item; do not create a separate rewrite for each long doc comment. CTX-02's fallback promise should be updated with its behavioral repair.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-06"></a>**CTX-06 — Equal declaration spans turn sibling functions into nested definitions**<br><br>**Severity/lenses:** CRITICAL; architecture and incorrect implementation. <br><br> **Evidence:** `loom/src/context/extract/javascript.rs:44-53` and `extract/tsx.rs:46-55` capture `lexical_declaration`/`variable_declaration` as the definition while capturing one nested declarator's name. In `const first = () => alpha(), second = () => beta();`, two named definitions receive the same whole-declaration span. `extract/treesitter/collect.rs:103-109` sorts those spans; `extract/treesitter/build.rs:143-163` considers an open definition the next one's parent whenever its end covers the next end, including equal spans. It constructs a scope such as `first::second` and containment between siblings. `collect.rs:264-270` also attributes references to the smallest containing span; equal spans cannot distinguish these bodies. This is a source trace, not an executed reproduction. <br><br> **Consequence/consumers:** Published IDs, containment, caller ownership and local/cross-file resolution are wrong; they feed map outline/neighbors/impact and retrieval ranking/expansion. TypeScript `.ts` captures the variable declarator instead, so equivalent `.ts`/`.tsx` declarations disagree. Existing JavaScript/TSX tests cover one function-valued declarator per declaration; the TSX whole-declaration assertion preserves the presentation choice without exercising siblings. <br><br> **Repair owner:** Capture each variable declarator as its definition consistently across ECMAScript dialects, or separate syntactic ownership spans from optional declaration display expansion. Attribute references to each actual body. Strict containment in `place` alone leaves overlapping reference attribution broken. Preserve whole declaration display only if required by its output contract. Bump affected extractor identities to rebuild cached wrong IDs/edges. <br><br> **Acceptance/constraints:** Add JS and TSX cases with two arrow functions and two function expressions in one `const`/`var` declaration. Both must be sibling nodes with independent IDs and their own calls. Repeat inside an enclosing function and compare `.ts`/`.tsx`. Preserve legitimate nested functions, duplicate-name disambiguation and declaration windows.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-07"></a>**CTX-07 — Import text reparsing loses comments and can invent local targets**<br><br>**Severity/lenses:** CRITICAL; architecture, DRY and incorrect implementation. <br><br> **Evidence:** Tree-sitter already parsed the import, but `extract/treesitter/collect.rs:217-243` passes owned statement/path text into reparsers. `extract/typescript/imports.rs:10-73` splits comma groups and `as` aliases as strings; its scanner (`75-105`) tracks quotes/nesting but not comments. `import { /* explanation */ parse } from "external-package"` binds a name containing comment text instead of `parse`. JS and TSX share this helper. Rust separately parses braces/commas/aliases in `extract/rust/imports.rs:16-124`; whitespace compaction leaves the comment in `use external::{ /* explanation */ parse };`. <br><br> `resolve/bindings.rs:105-120,331-337` looks up exact `local_name`. The contaminated name does not match a `parse()` reference. `resolve/rules.rs:348-373` then reaches graph-wide uniqueness without the external-import refusal a correct binding would supply. If another project file holds the sole `parse` definition, the call can bind to that local namesake instead of remaining an external gap. <br><br> **Repair owner:** Extract import bindings from grammar fields/children while tree-sitter nodes are available, skipping comment nodes. Keep the existing shared ECMAScript projection for JS/TS/TSX. A narrow comment-aware tokenizer is an alternative if changing the harness interface is disproportionate, but must preserve quoted module strings and Rust nested block comments; a global regex is insufficient. Bump relevant extractor identities; keep resolution policy unchanged. <br><br> **Acceptance/constraints:** Compare bindings for commented/uncommented equivalent imports in Rust and all ECMAScript dialects, including aliases/groups. A commented external import plus a cross-file local namesake must behave like its clean counterpart. Relative imports must retain targets/aliases. Preserve side effects, type-only imports, re-exports, parse-error coverage and confidence.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-08"></a>**CTX-08 — Extraction copies definition bodies before discarding them, then copies again**<br><br>**Severity/lenses:** MAJOR; excessive implementation. <br><br> **Evidence:** `extract/treesitter/collect.rs:125-160` unconditionally calls `node_text` for every query capture. That helper (`284-288`) creates an owned lossy UTF-8 string. Definition captures only retain a tree node through `definition_capture`; the whole-body string is unused. Ignored helper captures also pay conversion. `materialize` (`245-261`) then copies the exact body into `Definition.body: Vec<u8>`; `build.rs:165-185` consumes it only to hash it. Nested definitions retain overlapping source copies as well as paying for the discarded strings. <br><br> **Repair owner:** Convert text only in capture arms that consume it. Materialize the hash and first-line signature directly from the source slice, retaining those owned results instead of `Definition.body`. Keep tree lifetimes inside collection and outputs independent of the tree. This is a local collect/build boundary change, requiring no new cache/framework. <br><br> **Acceptance/constraints:** Preserve extraction output exactly: IDs, signature, original-byte hash, invalid UTF-8 behavior, coverage and diagnostics. An allocation-focused measurement on a large nested module/class should establish improvement; no numerical speedup is claimed. Preserve the extraction byte cap. <br><br> **Supplement: CTX-08 supplementary receiver-allocation evidence.** <br><br> The full-body extractor pass also confirms that collect.rs:150 retains raw receiver expression text and collect.rs:197-203 clones it into deduplication keys. Nested call/member chains retain overlapping receiver strings, so total copied bytes can grow quadratically with chain depth. This is adjacent to the discarded definition-body copying already reported under CTX-08. Keep receiver spans borrowed until the supported binding grammar requires a value; retain only that structural identity and deduplicate without cloning full expression bodies. Do not truncate arbitrary receiver strings into potentially false bindings. Acceptance should cover deep chained expressions with bounded retained text and unchanged resolution of supported identifier/path receivers. The nested-normal-function this-binding correctness issue is separately owned by concerns_data.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-09"></a>**CTX-09 — A tiny source window materializes an entire oversized file**<br><br>**Severity/lenses:** MAJOR; excessive implementation and inconsistent resource ownership. <br><br> **Evidence:** `context/source_graph/read.rs:26-52` streams a file, hashes all bytes and retains only the extraction prefix. `context/window.rs:73-116` calls `safe_read::read_bounded` with the recorded oversized file's full byte length, hashes the full buffer, then slices. `commands/map.rs:300-320` exposes this through `--window`; even `--window-lines 1` retains a very large file. `window_tests.rs:239-255` intentionally supports unchanged oversized files but tests only just above the extraction cap. Retrieval's automatic windows exclude non-full coverage (`retrieve/windows.rs:64-85`); this is the direct map window path. <br><br> **Repair owner:** Stream the safely opened regular file, retaining the requested line-aligned window and computing full identity before returning source text. Reuse safe-open/streaming-hash conventions from `SourceRead`; do not simply reject oversized files, which are supported. Retain growth checks and distinct changed/unreadable/unknown outcomes. <br><br> **Acceptance/constraints:** Large unchanged files return identical text/line metadata with memory proportional to the selected window plus a fixed buffer. A changed byte beyond the selected range must still reject output, as must symlink swaps and growth. Cover an unterminated final line and a single huge line; explicitly define any needed maximum-line policy instead of silently truncating bytes.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-10"></a>**CTX-10 — Whole-file span constructors omit the final unterminated line**<br><br>**Severity/lenses:** MINOR; DRY and incorrect metadata. <br><br> **Evidence:** `extract/mod.rs:171-180` counts newline bytes and uses `lines.max(1)` for inclusive `line_end`. `a\nb` produces 1 instead of 2, contrary to its comment. The streaming oversized path repeats the rule in `source_graph/read.rs:26-52,75`. `window.rs:178-185` already correctly accounts for nonempty final text without a newline. <br><br> **Impact limits:** File-node/oversized metadata violates the public graph span contract (`source_graph/node.rs:51-62`). Ranking and map outlines exclude file nodes, while windows recompute line ranges, so this review does not claim ordinary outlines/windows lose the last line. <br><br> **Repair/acceptance:** Share a line-count policy: newline count plus one iff nonempty bytes lack a final newline, keeping the empty-file line-1 convention. Track the final byte while streaming. Cover empty, one unterminated line, two lines with/without a trailing newline and oversized unterminated input. Invalidate affected cached metadata through the appropriate version bump.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-11"></a>**CTX-11 — Ranking comments preserve implementation diaries and obsolete worker rationale**<br><br>**Severity/lenses:** MINOR; invalid/verbose comments and small DRY opportunity. <br><br> **Evidence and concrete edits:** <br><br> • `rank.rs:59-99` spends about forty lines motivating the four-line prose-prior helper, including historical arithmetic and test-preservation rationale. Keep admission/rescue conditions, cache parity, repeated-term semantics and why rescued terms cannot count toward the hook emit floor; remove historical duplication and unstable corpus sizes. <br> • `rank/ladder.rs:4-11`, `rank/corpus.rs:5-11`, `rank_source/paths.rs:4-7`, `rank_source/expand.rs:163-165,188-190` and `tests/rank_source_expand_fusion.rs:4-6` explain past splitting/line limits. Describe the shared extension registry and distinct manifest policy. <br> • `source_graph/node.rs:127-140` omits `kind:` from documented IDs and describes signatures as declaration text “without the body.” IDs include kind; signatures are trimmed first source lines and may include one-line bodies or incomplete multiline declarations. Document actual contracts. <br> • `window.rs:1-8` implies accepted IDs are only those the graph names; `resolve_target` accepts arbitrary numeric `path@start-end` ranges for a live graph file. Document graph-backed paths and clamped ranges; preserve that supported behavior. <br><br> **Consumers/constraints/acceptance:** These changes help maintainers and must not alter ranking thresholds/output/cache formats/arithmetic. <a id="ctx-12"></a>**CTX-12 — CRITICAL: provider ledger and shared quota readers bypass existing bounded safe reads**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, DRY, reachable incorrect behavior. Related: verification reviewer VER-09 (forward-receipt prefix reader); these are distinct consumers of the same existing filesystem owner. <br><br> Evidence: `loom/src/provider_data/ledger.rs:352-386` (`json_lines`) checks pathname metadata for regular-file type and the 4 MiB limit, then opens with only `O_NOFOLLOW`. A replacement FIFO between those operations blocks the open before any descriptor check. An intermediate directory can also change to a symlink. The descriptor is never checked for regular-file type. The reader takes limit + 1 bytes but never rejects the extra byte: growth after the stat can yield a parsed prefix, silently omitting later conflicting ledger records. Identity resolution deliberately requires agreement across records, so accepting an incomplete oversized prefix defeats that conservative policy. <br><br> `loom/src/quota/shared.rs:161-171` (`read_state`) similarly uses a pathname symlink check followed by unbounded `std::fs::read_to_string`. A FIFO at the state path blocks even without a replacement race, and a large file allocates without a cap. This read happens while `poll_shared` holds the account lock (`:137-142`). The quota poller runs providers sequentially, so a stuck Claude state read prevents the remaining poll work and shutdown progress. <br><br> Existing shared owner: `loom/src/fs/safe_read.rs:48-81` (`read_bounded`, `read_capped`) already does a dirfd-relative no-follow walk, nonblocking open, descriptor regular-file check, and a checked cap + 1 read. `read_to_string_bounded` at `:136-139` adds UTF-8 validation. Do not build another bounded-reader abstraction. Migrate the ledger and account-state read to these primitives at a trusted root with an explicit relative path. Keep each caller's interpretation of missing/refused files local. The account-state reader needs a small explicit JSON limit; the ledger keeps its existing 4 MiB limit and complete-newline-only JSONL semantics. <br><br> Affected consumers: `StartedAgentTypeIndex::load` feeds `tokens/discover.rs`, `commands/usage/mod.rs`, `subagent_lifecycle/claude.rs`, and subagent classification/wait identity. `agent_type` additionally feeds subagent classification and forward-job status/rendering. Shared quota state feeds `quota/poller.rs` and project quota snapshots/history. This is broader than a standalone reporting read. Keep stage/session-scoped exact identity, ambiguous-row rejection, best-effort unavailable-ledger behavior, secret-free diagnostics, per-account serialization, and cached-success-on-fetch-error behavior. <br><br> Acceptance: a FIFO or parent symlink cannot block or redirect either read; deterministic growth past the cap rejects the entire ledger rather than authorizing its prefix; a complete ledger followed by an incomplete final line still retains complete rows; oversized quota JSON defaults safely without allocating its entire size. Preserve the separate VER-09 prefix reader's ownership and truncated-UTF-8 policies when sharing lower-level opening code; its requirements are not interchangeable with these full-body UTF-8 reads. <br><br> **Supplement: CTX-12 additional consumer: requested-model ledger reader.** <br><br> `commands/usage/spawn_join.rs:196-224` repeats the same metadata-then-`O_NOFOLLOW` reader as the provider ledger, without `O_NONBLOCK`, descriptor type validation, or directory anchoring. It takes exactly 4 MiB without an overflow sentinel, so growth can silently truncate a group and alter spawn/start pairing or conflict detection. Include `RequestedModels::load` in CTX-12's migration to the existing safe reader, preserving unknown/ambiguous model outcomes and newline policy. Its tests currently cover join cases only, not descriptor confinement or cap races.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-13"></a>**CTX-13 — CRITICAL: quota cleanup unlinks live account lock inodes**<br><br>Lenses: architecture and invalid lifecycle comment. `loom/src/quota/shared.rs:174-185` creates a per-token `.lock` file with `truncate(false)` and never updates its modification time. `prune_stale` at `:188-207` deletes another token's `.json` and `.lock` files independently once their own mtime exceeds a day. A lock's age therefore measures creation, not account inactivity. The comment “other keys untouched for a day” incorrectly treats an actively used lock as untouched. <br><br> Reachable sequence: account A's lock was created more than a day ago; daemon A acquires it and begins fetching. Daemon B successfully polls a different account and unlinks A's old lock file. A third poll for A creates the pathname again and locks the new inode while daemon A still holds the old inode. Both perform the supposedly serialized account fetch and write account state. Fresh A JSON does not protect the old A lock because cleanup tests each file independently. Concurrent writers can also collide at the existing fixed temporary state filename. <br><br> Smallest repair: keep persistent account lock files and prune only stale account data. A small lock inode per observed token is preferable to deleting the synchronization identity. Taking a lock immediately before unlinking is insufficient: other processes may already have opened, or be waiting on, the old inode. Do not introduce a second lock namespace or lock garbage collector without a demonstrated storage requirement. Repair belongs to `quota/shared.rs`; the poller and atomic state writer should retain their current single-account serialization assumption. <br><br> Acceptance: age an account lock while its JSON remains fresh, hold its lock, run another key's cleanup, and verify the original inode remains and a second A poll cannot enter its fetch callback until release. Also cover a process that opened the old lock before cleanup. Existing `shared_tests.rs` covers stale JSON pruning and different-token isolation, but not live lock deletion. Preserve token hashing, rate-limit backoff, fetch-under-lock, and pruning of obsolete data files.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-14"></a>**CTX-14 — MAJOR: live Claude revision handling reparses lines and retains redundant usage state**<br><br>Lenses: DRY, excessive implementation, architecture. `tokens/claude_line.rs:21-56` parses JSON and an RFC3339 timestamp, then returns only integer seconds. `tokens/messages.rs:37-42` reparses the whole JSON and timestamp with `expect`/`unwrap` to recover the subsecond timestamp needed for revision ordering. `MessageTable::is_stale` (`:62-65`) does this for an existing message; `Observation::new` (`:18-34`) does it again for accepted observations. `tokens/accumulate.rs:140-183` then checks `supersedes` both before updating model metadata and again before replacement. Accepted updates to existing messages therefore parse their potentially large JSON body three times. The panic assumptions cross a module boundary solely because the parser discarded a value it already validated. <br><br> The same path keeps `MessageTable.max: HashMap<String, TokenUsage>` alongside `observations: HashMap<String, Observation>`, whose observation already includes usage. The only production call to `increase` (`accumulate.rs:179`) discards the returned positive delta and instead retracts the old observation and adds the replacement in full. Both maps, the FIFO, tuple serialization, and compatibility tests must remain synchronized. The field name `max` and delta-oriented API describe a different operation from selected latest-observation replacement. <br><br> Repair at the existing parser/message-table owners: carry the validated `DateTime<Utc>` in the parsed usage observation (derive integer seconds for buckets), perform the stale check once before metadata mutation, and expose one selected-observation replacement operation that returns the prior observation for retraction. Preserve the bounded FIFO and one authoritative usage vector per retained ID. Do not replace latest-timestamp selection with per-field maxima or throw away subsecond precision. If eliminating the redundant serialized vector changes checkpoint shape, use the existing versioned rebuild policy; do not guess attribution for old records. The table's optional observations and standalone `increase` test fixtures need deliberate migration, not silent interpretation changes. <br><br> Affected consumers: live `AgentState` accumulation, checkpoint serde/cap shedding, tracker replay, and tests in `accumulate_tests`, `checkpoint_tests`, `pass_memo_tests`, and usage `cross_surface_tests`. Acceptance: equal/older observations leave metadata and totals unchanged; newer observations can decrease any usage field and retract the original bucket/model attribution; millisecond differences within one second order correctly; no-ID records remain additive; eviction/checkpoint roundtrip/restart behavior remains explicit. Inspect the parser call graph to confirm one JSON parse per line. No benchmark claim is needed. <br><br> A smaller related simplification: `accumulate.rs:57-87` duplicates all four fields of `codex_line::CodexTotals` as `SavedTotals` solely for serde, with two field-for-field conversions. Deriving serde on the existing owned data type removes this duplicate representation while preserving the checkpoint field names. This can be a separate small commit; it does not require the Claude table redesign.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-15"></a>**CTX-15 — CRITICAL: telemetry duplicates the spool implementation and omits its reader cap**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, DRY, excessive implementation. `telemetry/spool.rs:30-62` duplicates append/lock/serialize logic, checking only the file's old size before appending. One record can cross `SPOOL_MAX_BYTES`. More seriously, `drain_into_events` (`:89-111`) reads the entire session-writable regular spool into a string and then a vector, without enforcing that cap. A stage can write a large regular spool directly and make the trusted daemon allocate its full contents. Safe descriptor opening protects path confinement but does not bound this memory use. <br><br> Concrete existing shared owner: `fs/jsonl_spool.rs:11-32` already checks projected append size and a per-record limit. Its `contents` (`:35-43`) reads at most cap + 1 bytes from the locked descriptor, rejects oversized content before delivery, and validates UTF-8. Its generic `read`, `drain`, `drain_file`, and `parse` already implement the same complete behavior as telemetry's local copies, including malformed-row counts and truncation only after all sinks succeed. Migrate telemetry to this helper with `TelemetryRecord`, wrapping return values in `DrainOutcome` and keeping telemetry-specific error context. The current 1 MiB total limit matches. Set an explicit record limit without inventing a new domain restriction: at minimum enforce that a serialized record plus newline can fit the existing total limit. <br><br> Consumers: daemon telemetry spool drains, direct CLI fallback emission, telemetry summary reporting, and relay delivery only insofar as it shares event types. Preserve no-follow traversal, regular/single-link checks, the same descriptor throughout lock/read/truncate, retry on sink failure, malformed-line counts, and best-effort emission. Do not substitute a pathname-based second open after acquiring the lock. Acceptance: an externally oversized regular spool errors before the sink and remains untouched; append cannot cross the total cap; a small valid spool and malformed-row mix drain as before; symlink/hardlink/FIFO protection remains. Existing telemetry tests exercise symlink confinement and ordinary drains but not oversized direct writes. Runtime reviewer confirms memory/request spools already use the generic owner; deduplicate there rather than inventing a telemetry-specific bounded reader.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-16"></a>**CTX-16 — MAJOR: generic cache publication lives under quota and has outgrown its ownership comment**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, DRY, invalid comments. `tokens/checkpoint.rs:8`, `tokens/tracker.rs:14`, and quota history import generic `quota::cache` filesystem operations. `quota/cache.rs:122-125` says “Single-writer (the quota poller thread)” although the same helper publishes token checkpoints/flows, locked multi-process account state, and locked history. Its directory-fsync claim is also stronger than its behavior: `:141-144` ignores both opening and syncing the parent directory. These are policy decisions different consumers now inherit from a feature module. <br><br> Coordinate with runtime RT-13, which owns the broader consolidation of `fs/locking`, `fs/safe_fs`, and `fs/safe_replace`. The existing dirfd-anchored publisher in `fs/safe_replace` is the concrete core migration target, with caller-policy wrappers; none of those whole functions is a drop-in replacement. Move generic publication/directory handling into filesystem ownership, then update quota, history, and token consumers. Keep quota sanitation and provider paths under quota. Explicitly preserve or deliberately revise absent-target creation, replacement of a bad checkpoint leaf, directory/file modes, caller byte limits (8 MiB checkpoint versus other owners' 4 MiB limits), and post-rename sync-failure semantics. Do not import a lower cap or an existing-target-only rule into token recovery. <br><br> Acceptance: existing cache/history/checkpoint/flow roundtrip and failure tests retain their contracts, concurrent independent publishers cannot unlink each other's temporary files, and callers can distinguish failure before publication from a durability warning after publication. The comment should state the actual synchronization requirement and durability result, not identify one original caller as the universal owner. RT-13 owns the shared-core implementation; this finding owns these concrete feature consumers and the layer correction.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-17"></a>**CTX-17 — CRITICAL: skill trigger extraction slices original UTF-8 with transformed offsets**<br><br>Lenses: nonidiomatic implementation and correctness. `loom/src/skills/index.rs:174-200` (`extract_description_triggers`) searches `trimmed.to_uppercase()` and `trimmed.to_lowercase()`, then uses the returned byte position to slice the original `trimmed`. Unicode case conversion can change byte length. For example, a description containing `İTrigger keywords:é` lowercases the leading capital dotted I to two code points, adding one byte. The computed end of the marker is consequently one byte into the original final `é`, and string slicing panics. Less dramatic changes return the wrong trigger text. This is ordinary valid Unicode metadata, not malformed YAML. <br><br> Consumer path: `SkillIndex::load_from_directory` / catalog loading parses metadata, then `add_skill` invokes this fallback when explicit trigger fields are absent. Per-file error handling catches `Result` errors, not this panic, so one valid installed skill can abort index construction during runtime startup or recommendation loading. <br><br> Smallest repair: search these ASCII markers using an ASCII-only case-folded copy, which preserves every original byte position, or find ASCII-insensitive marker spans directly in the original string. Keep slicing offsets tied to the original bytes; using `.get()` to discard the panic would still silently misparse valid descriptions. Preserve marker precedence, CSV punctuation rules, and the existing explicit-trigger/CSV/fallback precedence. <br><br> Acceptance: Unicode prefixes that expand or contract under full case conversion, multibyte text immediately after either marker, mixed-case ASCII markers, and explicit triggers bypassing fallback all behave without panic and produce exact trigger text. Existing tests cover ASCII descriptions and precedence only. This requires no dependency or parser abstraction. <br><br> **Supplement: CTX-17 additional consumer — command-side skill indexing repeats the Unicode offset defect.** <br><br> `commands/skill_index.rs:303-321` independently joins description lines, calls `to_lowercase`, finds ASCII trigger markers in that transformed copy, and slices the original text with the resulting byte offset. `İ Triggers:é` panics for the same reason as `skills/index.rs:174-200`. This is integration finding INT-E31, consolidated under CTX-17. Give the two consumers one small original-byte-safe ASCII marker span operation; retain each caller's marker precedence, sentence-boundary and CSV handling rather than unifying unrelated parsing policy. Add both concrete Unicode cases to the acceptance set and exercise the command-side index build as well as runtime skill loading. <br><br> <a id="int-e31"></a>**Reconciled evidence from INT-E31: INT-E31 — CRITICAL: Unicode lowercasing offsets can panic skill-index generation.** <br><br> **Lenses:** non-idiomatic string handling, correctness. `skill_index.rs:303–321` finds a marker byte offset in `text.to_lowercase()` and slices the original text at that offset. Unicode case conversion does not preserve UTF-8 length: a description `İ Triggers:é` lowers the first character from two bytes to three, so the offset after `triggers:` points into the original `é`, and Rust panics. Valid external skill frontmatter without explicit trigger fields reaches this fallback through173–183; index generation/install cannot finish. Current parser tests cover ASCII descriptions. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** search with ASCII-insensitive matching on original byte boundaries, or extract from the already normalized string when preserving original spelling is unnecessary. Do not carry byte offsets across transformed strings. Preserve marker specificity, sentence boundaries and multiword keywords. Acceptance: length-changing Unicode before every supported marker never panics and extracts the intended keyword; existing ASCII and multiline fixtures retain results.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-18"></a>**CTX-18 — MAJOR: two skill-directory loaders duplicate traversal and disagree on duplicate-name ownership**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, DRY, excessive implementation. `skills/index.rs:41-77` and `skills/index_catalog.rs:92-137` each enumerate skill subdirectories, locate `SKILL.md`, parse metadata, and print the same warning on failure. The catalog copy adds a name-existence check; the primary copy does not. `SkillIndex` then stores the same metadata identity in a vector, a description map, and a trigger map (`index.rs:15-22,96-125`). Two primary directories with the same frontmatter name retain both metadata entries: `get_by_name` returns the first while the description map retains the last, and triggers from both accumulate. Filesystem enumeration order decides which description each recommendation path sees. Catalog name precedence is explicit and tested, but duplicate primary names are internally inconsistent. <br><br> Repair at `SkillIndex`: one internal directory-loading operation, parameterized only by the existing root precedence, and one authoritative name-to-metadata owner. Insert/skip/replace duplicate names atomically across metadata and trigger membership. Define deterministic duplicate handling, such as stable path ordering with first-wins plus a warning, and keep the documented primary-over-catalog priority. Do not make source directory location part of the public skill identity or introduce a new plugin system. A description should be projected from authoritative metadata rather than independently replaced in a parallel map. <br><br> Consumers: `load_with_catalog`, runtime `Orchestrator::load_skill_index`, prompt/skill recommendations, exact-name lookup, and core-plan declared-skill validation. Preserve permissive per-file parse failures, missing-directory behavior, ordinary user skills, trigger-source precedence, public matching thresholds, and catalog fallback invocation. Acceptance: two directories declaring one name cannot produce a lookup description different from that name's keyword recommendation; repeated normalized triggers do not multiply the same logical trigger; stable primary/catalog precedence is independent of directory enumeration. Existing catalog collision test covers only one entry per root.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-19"></a>**CTX-19 — MINOR: skill comments retain procedural narration and overstate filesystem checks**<br><br>Lenses: verbose/invalid comments. `skills/mod.rs:40-45` spends six lines explaining the compiler effect of a test-gated re-export; `skills/matcher.rs:23-31,43-85` repeats the loop and collection operations step by step, including “Track scores,” “Convert,” “Sort,” and “Return top N.” Keep only the scoring/threshold contract and the test-only ownership reason. `skills/index_catalog.rs:44-60` can state the actual rule and allowed-tools constraint without the historical catalog-move narrative. These are cleanup targets, not reasons to alter invocation semantics. <br><br> `skills/project/probe.rs:79-80` says the preliminary `lstat` “keeps a FIFO from blocking the open”; the actual protection is the shared safe reader's `O_NONBLOCK` and descriptor check, and the pathname check cannot secure a race. Remove that redundant check if no caller-specific policy needs it, or describe it only as an early filter. `skills/project/runners.rs:51-53` says a file reached through a symlink “anywhere below the checkout root reads as absent,” but marker-only `any_file` checks in `rust`, `python`, `javascript`, `jvm`, and `ruby` use final-component `symlink_metadata` and can see through intermediate symlinks. Either narrow that comment to content probes or route marker probes through the same anchored policy; do not claim the current marker calls enforce it. Existing symlink runner test checks JSON/TOML content, not `.config/nextest.toml`, `pytest.ini`, or lockfile markers. <br><br> Acceptance: comments accurately distinguish traversal/content/marker guarantees; any policy-strengthening marker change receives a focused intermediate-symlink case and preserves legitimate runner precedence. No test is needed for narration-only removal.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-20"></a>**CTX-20 — CRITICAL: duplicated memory stage enumeration hides spool-only search results**<br><br>Lenses: DRY, architecture, reachable incorrect behavior. `commands/memory/handlers/read.rs:66-74` (`query`) enumerates only `list_journals` when no explicit stage is supplied. A current worktree whose only note is still in its spool has no journal filename, so `loom memory query <matching text>` reports no journals/results. The same entry appears in `list`, `show --all`, and `pending`: their repeated stage-enumeration code explicitly adds `spool_only_stage_with_pending` (`read.rs:192-200,235-239,322-343`; `pending.rs:182-190`). `query_stage` already reads the merged journal correctly if a stage is explicitly supplied; the omission is before that call. <br><br> Repair at the existing command read boundary: centralize the small operation that lists canonical journals plus the current worktree's spool-only stage, with deterministic ordering and an optional stage restriction. Use it in query/list/show/pending rather than adding another one-off query branch. Preserve `read_journal_with_pending` as the merge owner and the existing `fs::memory::read_staged_entries` deduplication/ordering behavior where applicable. This does not authorize discovering other worktrees' pending spools or creating state during reads. <br><br> Acceptance: with an existing work root, no journal, and one current-stage spooled note, unscoped query finds its content and scoped query agrees with list/show/pending. An unrelated stage spool stays invisible. Existing tests cover the spool-only list/show/pending paths but have no corresponding query assertion. Keep empty-workspace degradation and type filtering unchanged.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-21"></a>**CTX-21 — MAJOR: every edit records an unused dirty-path artifact**<br><br>Lenses: excessive implementation, architecture, invalid comments. `commands/context/record_edit.rs:9-14` explicitly states that `dirty-paths.json` has no consumer and exists for a future incremental refresher. If external CLI compatibility must be retained, keep a short deprecated no-op dispatch for one compatibility window rather than maintaining speculative persistence. Do not invent the missing incremental invalidation subsystem to justify a producer with no current requirement. Existing refresh/source-graph owners already compute actual checkout changes; preserve their behavior. <br><br> Concrete comment/document errors: README.md:597 says this command keeps a stage's context overlay current, although it changes no overlay or resolved graph. `context/graph_store/mod.rs:217-220` says both dirty paths and session-retrieval records “are read by other stages after this one merges”; that is false for dirty paths. These corrections should happen with the producer removal, or immediately if removal is deferred. <br><br> Acceptance: ordinary Write/Edit/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit and Codex apply-patch hooks retain their real heartbeat/reconciliation behavior without invoking this artifact writer; context retrieval still refreshes actual source changes; session-retrieval records survive graph overlay discard. Remove tests that only prove unused path accumulation, retaining any path rules needed by another real consumer. Relevant shell tests are owned by the assets/hooks reviewer and require coordinated edits. No production changes made in this review.<br><br>

<a id="ctx-23"></a>**CTX-23 — CRITICAL: usage selection is fragmented across providers and applied after Codex deduplication**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, DRY, reachable incorrect behavior. `commands/usage/args.rs:78-84` promises stage/plan filtering. `mod.rs:179-209` passes those selections only through Claude discovery (`:232-233`) and a separately resolved session inventory stage filter (`:259-271`). Codex receives `CodexScope::for_selection(args.all, &forward_join, &range)` with no stage selection. `forward_join.rs:41-46,70-75` reduces receipts to thread and observation time, discarding their already validated stage identity; `load_root` loads every project stage. Thus `usage --provider codex --stage alpha` includes beta's forwarded threads. An unresolved `--plan` empties Claude discovery while Codex can still report the project's full usage. `--project` also resolves plan stages using the current directory's work root in `discovery::plan_stage_ids`, rather than the explicit project's selected root. <br><br> A second ordering problem sits at `provider.rs:83-94`: `codex_provider::normalize` globally classifies response IDs (`codex_provider.rs:39-52,317-322`) before scope removes foreign threads. Two exact response copies with the same timestamp/tokens but different thread metadata keep the first canonical and mark the second duplicate. If the first is foreign and the second belongs to this project, scope removes the canonical row, leaving the selected row excluded from measured totals. The report can have zero measured tokens, no missing thread, and a measured completeness state. Global malformed-file diagnostics also survive removal of foreign rows, so an unrelated damaged rollout makes a clean project's report partial (`completeness.rs:48-85`). These defects follow directly from the current ordering; no race or external service behavior is needed. <br><br> Repair at the existing `usage` orchestration boundary: resolve one selection from the requested project/work root and optional stage/plan, reuse it for Claude files, session inventory and receipt thread ownership, and retain stage identity in the receipt projection. Preserve ambiguous receipt identity conservatively: selecting a stage must not turn a thread with conflicting ownership into a confident attribution. Keep per-file Codex diagnostics and identity until scope is established, then classify IDs among selected rows and aggregate diagnostics for that scope. Files with unknown identity need an explicit conservative completeness reason rather than silently being treated as known foreign. Keep the inclusive event-time window, receipt-after-until ownership rule, and separate missing-thread grace window. <br><br> Affected consumers: ordinary CLI human/JSON usage, all provider reports/groupings and completeness, and comparison evidence generated from these totals. Execution receipts are deliberately unscoped today (`codex_scope_tests.rs:119-139`); decide how a stage filter treats receipts without stage evidence, retaining an unknown/inapplicable diagnostic instead of silently claiming them for a stage. Do not remove the explicit receipt-root facility or infer stage from model names. <br><br> Acceptance: two stages with distinct receipt threads produce disjoint stage totals; a foreign explicit project cannot consult the current project's plan config; unresolved plan scope is consistently empty/unavailable across providers; a foreign exact copy before a selected response cannot suppress selected tokens; damaged evidence confidently attributed to another project does not make the selected project's totals partial. Existing tests cover project roots, exact tuples and time windows, but do not exercise stage selection or scope-before-ID-classification end to end.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-24"></a>**CTX-24 — CRITICAL: comparison checks lose provider/model/effort associations**<br><br>Lens: architecture and excessive decomposition of a value's invariant. `commands/usage/comparison_eval.rs:143-176` (`assignments_comparable`, `dimensions`) sorts provider, model and effort columns independently. The manifest validates one assignment per provider (`comparison_validation.rs:174-180`), so provider is already an available stable association key. With unchanged providers/models but swapped efforts between Claude and Codex, each independently sorted column matches and the pair is deemed comparable even though no effort intervention was declared. Swapping complete model assignments has the same issue. This can feed supported token/subscription verdicts for an intervention whose controlled dimensions changed. <br><br> Smallest repair: compare assignment tuples projected onto the dimensions that must remain equal, preserving associations instead of comparing independent marginals. When provider assignment is fixed, compare by provider; when it is deliberately changed, match the remaining fixed tuple dimensions as a multiset. Keep duplicate-provider validation and explicit allowed-change semantics in the existing comparison owner; no generic matching framework is required for the current two-provider schema. <br><br> Affected consumers: `usage --compare` pair comparability, dominance and net-total aggregation, emitted verdict/reason codes. Preserve finite-fixture claim scope, independent quality/latency rejection, and provider-specific token semantics. Acceptance: undeclared effort swaps fail comparability even when global effort sets match; declared effort changes pass without allowing undeclared model changes; assignment order changes alone remain comparable; provider reassignment honors the remaining fixed dimensions.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-25"></a>**CTX-25 — MINOR: usage comments retain measurements and implementation history as contracts**<br><br>Lens: invalid/verbose comments. `commands/usage/transcript.rs:1-11` states that counting stream lines overstates usage “by about 2.3x”; the parser's invariant is latest whole-vector selection, independent of that dataset-specific ratio. `transcript_types.rs:1-5` explains that it was split to meet the line cap, and `:85-105` narrates all consumers' sentinel filtering. <a id="ctx-26"></a>**CTX-26 — MAJOR: legacy usage groupings duplicate accounting fields and aggregation**<br><br>Lenses: DRY, excessive implementation. `commands/usage/sections/agents.rs:32-48,191-229,336-348` and `sections/by_model.rs:19-33,43-60,102-114` repeat the same six usage counters, three accounting counters, zero initialization and field-by-field additions. Compute one per-transcript count map and reuse it. `sections/lengths.rs:168-175`, `peaks.rs:79-84`, and `lifecycle.rs:153-154` clone and sort the same sample vector for each percentile even though `fmt::percentile` sorts in place; sort once and select all requested ranks. Preserve the current nearest-rank definition; provider-ledger percentiles currently use another definition, so do not silently unify their values. <br><br> Additional comment corrections for CTX-25: `sections/agents.rs:263-309` spends two long blocks recounting prior misclassification and every rationale for its fallback; retain the delimiter/cost-annotation/ambiguity rule and the ordinary-word exclusion in a few sentences. Several section test headers cite a different agent's rule number and historical file splits. `sections/polling.rs:20-23` claims repeated commands “only observe” and their resident tokens are what a wait/event “would save,” but `tool_kind` accepts any repeated command, including writes, and prefix-matches compound shells such as `git status && apply_changes`. Label this as a heuristic upper bound and distinguish repeated activity from known observation; a full shell parser is unnecessary for that reporting correction. `sections/peaks.rs:4-5,30-31` says “never exceeded twice,” while `:116` uses strict `< 2x`; make the wording match the existing display and code.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-27"></a>**CTX-27 — Delivery bookkeeping can block a hook on an optional record**<br><br>**Severity/lenses:** CRITICAL; architecture and DRY. `context/delivery.rs:205` (`merge_with_recorded`) and `:278` (`load_delivery_dir`) use unbounded `fs::read_to_string`. The former runs under `locked_dir_update`; the latter accepts any directory entry whose extension is `json`. A FIFO named as a delivery record blocks before parse/error fallback, a symlink reads outside the intended record directory, and a large file allocates without a ceiling. This contradicts the module contract that optional bookkeeping cannot fail a hook/spawn. Consumers include prompt retrieval (`commands/hook/user_prompt.rs:242-281`), spawn delivery (`orchestrator/signals/retrieval.rs:282`), dependency boosts and stage telemetry (`orchestrator/core/stage_telemetry.rs:24`). Existing tests exercise absent/malformed records but not these filesystem objects. <br><br> **Repair/constraints:** use the existing anchored `fs/safe_read` regular-file/nonblocking bounded read underneath the delivery owner, preserving missing/malformed-as-no-record and the existing lock around read/merge/write. A bounded reader does not acquire the directory lock, so there is no need to retain unsafe plain reads to avoid recursive locking. Define a record size limit and enforce it at both write and read; preserve the union of delivered `(id,hash)` pairs, epochs, worker-directory inclusion and deterministic sort. Parent symlinks require anchored resolution from the actual work root, not reopening a previously checked path. Coordinate publication changes with RT-13; this is a distinct caller/policy from CTX-12 and VER-09. <br><br> **Acceptance:** FIFO, leaf/intermediate symlink and over-limit records return promptly without external reads; missing/corrupt records remain best effort; concurrent union updates retain both records; legitimate large accumulated sessions have an explicit limit policy.<br><br>

Consumers are prompt-hook deduplication and compaction reset tests. Acceptance: identical set for own/spawn/both/different epochs and no cloning of entire delivery records. <br><br> The same module spends lines 1-20 retelling the old missing-session bug, lines 32-48 explaining the hash defensively, and lines 94-103 defending the helper arrangement. `delivery.rs:28-31` explains a line-count split rather than behavior; `graph_store/prune.rs:77-82` preserves a previous stage ownership restriction as a reason not to change a signature. Replace these histories with the current identity/epoch/compaction and shared-directory constraints. Keep the non-reentrant lock explanation, but correct its suggestion that `fs::read_to_string` is necessary: safe unlocked reads satisfy that constraint.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-29"></a>**CTX-29 — Edge evaluation selects the favorable binding and does not gate ambiguity correctness**<br><br>**Severity/lenses:** CRITICAL; architecture and excessive implementation. `context/eval_edges/metrics.rs:154-180` finds edges by path, line and symbol suffix, then deliberately prefers one bound to the expected target. If one line contains two same-named calls with different receivers, one correct binding can hide the other wrong high-confidence binding. The labels lack a column/occurrence identifier, so the expected answer participates in selecting the observation being judged. This is not an independent quality measurement. Separately, `Thresholds::check` (`eval_edges/mod.rs:162-219`) never gates ambiguity mismatches: `is_correct` records them only in `failures`, which the threshold decision ignores. The existing `an_ambiguous_label_is_correct_only_when_the_candidates_match` test (`tests.rs:190-209`) checks `failures.len()` but never checks the CLI pass flag; its mismatched corpus otherwise keeps the same passing metrics and existing IDs. <br><br> **Repair/constraints:** keep `eval_edges` as the scoring owner. Resolve a label to an unambiguous source site independently of its expected target; reject labels that match multiple distinct observations unless they carry an explicit site discriminator. Count incorrect ambiguous candidate sets in a dedicated gate, preserving the deliberate published tolerances for recall/precision rather than simply failing every diagnostic string. Reject duplicate labels in `labels::load_labels` before `LabelCounts::of` so repeated labels cannot meet corpus-count floors or overweight one observation. Existing declaration identity intentionally ignores line; duplicate rejection should use the actual scoring key. `map/views/eval_edges` text/JSON/exit consumers and labelled fixture tests must use the same decision. Syntax-error presence-only and unchecked external-label locations are related existing backlog concerns, not claimed as newly discovered. <br><br> **Acceptance:** two same-line same-symbol calls with different receivers cannot be scored by choosing the expected answer; mismatched ambiguity exits nonzero; a duplicate-label corpus is rejected before scoring; published clean corpora still pass within declared metric tolerances.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-30"></a>**CTX-30 — Evaluation thresholds accept values that cannot express a valid floor**<br><br>**Severity/lenses:** MAJOR; architecture and nonidiomatic boundary validation. `commands/knowledge/eval/cases.rs:55-60,78-102` accepts floating pass/precision floors without validating finite values or the probability range. `context/eval_edges/mod.rs:149-152` likewise deserializes threshold floats without validation. Both gates compare `actual < floor` (`knowledge/eval/report.rs:125-134`, `eval_edges/mod.rs:198-201`), so a negative floor disables the gate, values above one make it impossible, and NaN makes the comparison false. The loaders validate other document properties, giving the impression these are checked evaluation inputs. <br><br> **Repair/constraints:** validate all ratio floors as finite and within `[0,1]` at each existing typed loader; a small shared ratio validator is sufficient if both need identical diagnostics. Keep integer ceiling fields and deliberate zero thresholds valid. Do not broaden this into a configuration framework. Acceptance: negative, above-one, NaN and infinity floors fail with field names; zero and one remain valid, default and published documents retain behavior. No parser execution was run; negative/out-of-range behavior is directly established, and special YAML float acceptance should be pinned by the future regression tests.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-31"></a>**CTX-31 — Local overlay identity collides across equal-basename worktrees**<br><br>**Severity/lenses:** CRITICAL; architecture and invalid comments. `context/local_overlay.rs:13-37` canonicalizes the root but derives the stage key only from its final component. Two linked worktrees `/a/shared` and `/b/shared` therefore both use `_local/map-shared` under their shared work root. Canonicalization fixes aliases of one directory; it cannot establish uniqueness between different directories. The long comment claiming this keeps each worktree private is false for valid Git worktree layouts. Existing tests cover alias equivalence, not distinct equal-basename roots. Consumers include CLI map, local retrieval and dirty-tree semantic refresh through the shared `local_overlay_key` owner. <br><br> **Repair/constraints:** include a stable digest of the full canonical checkout identity in the existing local key, retaining a readable basename if useful. Keep the same directory's alternate spellings equivalent, define the canonicalization-failure fallback consistently, and use the same helper for every reader/writer. Old derived overlay names can be abandoned/rebuilt; do not delete unrelated shared-directory records. Acceptance: equal-basename linked worktrees share cache/work roots but have distinct local overlay addresses and cannot see each other's edits; aliases of one root still agree.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-32"></a>**CTX-32 — Blob reuse mistakes an overlay's index identity for the bytes it parsed**<br><br>**Severity/lenses:** CRITICAL; architecture and cache ownership. `refresh/source_graph/layer.rs:79-98` copies every `enumeration.known` index OID into an overlay's `blob_index`, including dirty paths whose `FileEntry` was parsed from working-tree bytes. `resolve_file_entry:112-125` then calls `reuse_by_oid:201-219` before reading bytes, and that helper trusts matching OIDs whenever the path is currently clean. Build an overlay with modified `a.rs`; restore `a.rs` while another tracked file remains dirty; the next overlay build reuses the old dirty `a.rs` entry because its stored index OID still equals the current clean OID. `persist_layer` retains that entry because it differs from the clean base. The same contaminated entry can enter a new base because `source_graph.rs:239-258` prefers the previous local overlay as a reuse source. Hash reuse cannot correct this: OID reuse returns first. <br><br> **Repair/constraints:** the layer builder must record whether an entry's bytes are proven to represent the associated OID. OID reuse may only consume such entries. The smallest representation change may omit dirty working-tree entries from an overlay's reusable blob index, while preserving index data separately if a consumer needs it; otherwise add explicit provenance. Base entries read from committed blobs may retain OID reuse even when the working tree is dirty. Preserve parser/schema checks, tombstones, hash reuse and incremental counters. Bump the persisted identity/schema as needed so existing overlays carrying ambiguous provenance cannot be trusted. Consumers are every snapshot policy, local/stage retrieval, map and merge/base reconciliation. <br><br> **Acceptance:** modify two files, build overlay, restore one, rebuild and observe restored declarations/body hashes; restore a dirty file then create an unrelated commit and build its base, proving no dirty symbols enter the immutable base. Existing one-file edit/reuse tests are insufficient. Clean repeated snapshots must still reuse entries without rereading source.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-33"></a>**CTX-33 — Rust anchored paths lose the crate evidence already available to the resolver**<br><br>**Severity/lenses:** CRITICAL; architecture and DRY. `resolve/paths/rust.rs:93-104` resolves `crate::` against every root in `PathIndex::crate_roots`; the same module already has `own_crate_root:126-133`, used by unanchored paths and cross-file receiver matching. In a workspace, a call in crate A to `crate::helper` can find `helper` defined only in crate B and receive `Import` confidence; if both define it, the valid own-crate call becomes ambiguous. The all-roots policy is explicitly documented at lines 85-89, but that documentation does not make it Rust's scoping rule. Existing `tests_paths_packages.rs` correctly verifies unanchored paths stay within the citing crate; `tests_qualified.rs` checks anchored resolution only in one crate. <br><br> **Repair/constraints:** reuse `own_crate_root` for `crate::` and ensure fallback suffix matching cannot escape a known anchor. Preserve a conservative unresolved/candidate result when the graph lacks enough root evidence. A related existing heuristic in `package_named:140-156` drops any external leading segment from a test/bench path and assumes it denotes the containing package: `serde::helper` can resolve the local `helper`. One-segment external names are already refused by the empty-relative guard, and ordinary unanchored paths are already crate-scoped; do not repeat the stale broader backlog claims. Validate the package-name interpretation against existing manifest evidence if available, otherwise avoid upgrading the positional guess to an asserted import bind. Do not introduce a full compiler or new project-scanning layer solely for this fix. <br><br> **Consumers/acceptance:** the shared path index serves import edges, named bindings, qualified calls and incremental relinking. Bump resolver identity; preserve pathset/name dependency-key recording. Add two-crate `crate::` cases with only the foreign definition and with both definitions; own-crate resolution must never be displaced by the foreign one. Add test/bench external-prefix cases alongside genuine own-package imports and confirm uncertain imports remain gaps/candidates.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-34"></a>**CTX-34 — Overlay discard leaves denied-write layers alive in the same store**<br><br>**Major; architecture and DRY.** `context/graph_store/mod.rs:224-233` removes the overlay's view and disk layer, but does not remove its entry in `memory_fallback`. `graph_store/fallback.rs:106-109` preferentially returns that entry. A denied `save_overlay` followed by restored permissions and successful `discard_overlay` therefore leaves `load_overlay`, `resolved`, and `view` serving the discarded layer for the lifetime of that `GraphStore`. This is distinct from successful-write fallback replacement, which is implemented correctly in `write_or_fall_back:29-31` and `view/store.rs:234-241`. <br><br> Make the existing `GraphStore` layer lifecycle own disk and memory invalidation together. Clear the overlay's held layer when discard succeeds (including absent-file success), alongside `discard_overlay_view`; define failed disk deletion consistently rather than silently claiming removal. Preserve the shared overlay directory, session delivery records, and other owners' files. Add a focused denied-save → restore permissions → discard → load/view regression and retain the existing disk-only discard and delivery-preservation tests. A store-level memory injection seam can avoid permission-dependent skips if the test environment runs privileged. <br><br> The suspected generationless overlay/base cache collision is **not current**: `view/store.rs:99-108` refuses to retain an unidentifiable overlay, path keys distinguish locations, and `view/tests_store_cache.rs:99-116` exercises it. Successful persisted replacement clearing `view_fallback` is also covered at `tests_store_cache.rs:248-286`.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-35"></a>**CTX-35 — JSON map identities are passed through a destructive display sanitizer**<br><br>**Major; architecture and excessive reuse.** `map/views/json.rs:40-42` routes `safe` to `context/untrusted.rs:195-199`'s `inline_safe`: it replaces backticks, collapses whitespace, and truncates at 200 characters. Machine-readable identities in `find_all.rs:108-114`, `impact.rs:201-216`, and `neighbors.rs:263-278` use that function. A legitimate source path containing a backtick or a long nested node ID is returned under a different ID; passing that JSON ID to the exact-ID lookup in `matching.rs:51-53` fails. Two IDs differing after the truncation boundary become indistinguishable. JSON escaping already protects serialized line/control structure; a display transformation changes the API's data contract. <br><br> Keep raw stable IDs and paths in JSON, using serde's escaping, and keep the existing sanitizer on human text. If a terminal-facing JSON consumer needs safe labels, expose labels separately rather than mutating lookup keys. Apply consistently to outline, find-all, neighbors, impact, and source-window identity fields; preserve schema compatibility deliberately. Acceptance: serialize/parse a >200-character ID and paths containing backticks, tabs and newlines, feed the parsed ID to an exact graph lookup, and obtain the same node. Human rendering must still neutralize controls and cap display labels. Existing JSON tests assert key sets and ordinary values, not identity round trips.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-36"></a>**CTX-36 — Impact explanations lose the distinction between traversal and filtered display**<br><br>**Moderate; invalid comments and duplicated policy.** `map/views/impact.rs:60-98` filters traversal results for display, then `render_start:159-160` uses an empty displayed list to claim “no resolved edge reaches this node”. A graph with a real caller filtered out by `--path` or `--lang` receives that claim; evidence/confidence/depth restrictions can likewise exclude real edges. Preserve the useful unresolved/coverage caveat, without asserting it explains every absence. Add cases where a real caller is filtered away, where only an import/contains edge exists, and where default vs explicit-all kind labels match actual traversal.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-37"></a>**CTX-37 — Required knowledge IDs bypass source-only scope validation**<br><br>**Major; architecture, DRY, and invalid contract comments.** `context/retrieve/request.rs:19-29` offers the source graph to ID validation only when `Channel::Source` is requested, but always offers the whole knowledge catalog. `retrieve.rs:265-295` accepts every catalog chunk ID. A source-only request requiring a valid knowledge chunk therefore passes the check, while `retrieve/channels.rs:120-180` never ranks that chunk. Required reservation sees only ranked candidates, so the pack succeeds without either the requested item or an unmet-required record. The comments claiming rejection of IDs outside the query's scope describe only the opposite direction correctly. <br><br> Make request validation use the same scope-to-corpus selection as ranking, with knowledge and source handled symmetrically. Preserve the existing explicit policy of rejecting IDs outside scope; do not silently widen the query. Keep lifecycle eligibility and budget failure distinct from an unknown/out-of-scope ID. Consumers are the context CLI, eval requests, prompt retrieval, and stage signal retrieval through `retrieve_for_stage`. Acceptance: a valid knowledge ID with source-only scope errors, a valid source ID with knowledge-only scope errors, and mixed-scope requests accept both; an admitted required candidate that cannot fit still produces the existing unmet-budget diagnostic.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-38"></a>**CTX-38 — Bootstrap embeds an unquoted completion-marker shell command**<br><br>**Major; DRY and boundary correctness.** `commands/knowledge/bootstrap/prompt.rs:85-95` interpolates `marker.display()` directly after `touch` in a command the session must execute exactly. A project path containing spaces turns the one marker into several paths and leaves the driver's expected marker absent; shell metacharacters change the command. Escaping the outer Claude argv cannot escape this shell command embedded in the prompt. The comment also names a stale copy in `commands/pressure/spawn.rs`, which now delegates to the shared pressure owner. <br><br> The shared pressure owner, `pressure/instruction.rs:13-32`, has the same raw interpolation, so merely delegating to it does not fix quoting. Make that existing owner provide a shell-quoted marker command and use it from both instruction renderers; retain each caller’s lifecycle wording. Preserve bootstrap's knowledge-check and final-action conditions, workspace marker placement, and cleanup behavior. Quote the path as one shell argument and guard option interpretation. Existing bootstrap fake-agent tests create a hard-coded relative marker rather than executing the rendered instruction, so they do not cover this boundary. Acceptance: render the command for project paths containing spaces, quotes, and shell metacharacters, and verify argv resolves to exactly the intended marker; retain driver completion and cleanup tests.<br><br>

## Signal generation and review guidance

<a id="ctx-s01"></a>**CTX-S01 — Delivery publication precedes the signal it claims was delivered**<br><br>**Major; architecture, DRY, invalid comments.** `signals/retrieval.rs:264-267` promises that recording a pack *before* writing its signal means a record “never describes a signal that does not exist yet”. The ordering has the opposite effect. `generate.rs:91-92,199-200`, `knowledge.rs:49-52`, `contract.rs:113-116`, and `recovery.rs:65-70` publish delivery first, then call `helpers::write_signal_file`, which can fail creating/writing `signals/<session>.md`. A failed signal write leaves durable delivery claims for content never published. This is independent of CTX-27's unsafe delivery-file reader. <br><br> Delivery records feed `context/delivery.rs:295-321` epoch dedupe and dependency chunk boosts, plus session accounting. Every generator should use one signal-publication operation that first publishes the complete signal, then records that signal's pack. Preserve best-effort delivery-record failure and successful signal generation; do not make an optional receipt failure prevent a launch. Do not claim this proves the agent read the signal: publication and consumption are distinct. If crash consistency across two files is required, the receipt needs a signal-content identity and readers must validate it; merely changing the order supplies the narrower no-receipt-for-failed-write guarantee. <br><br> The case in tests_brief_e2e.rs:58-110 named delivery_record_exists_before_the_signal only checks that both exist after generation returns. It cannot establish ordering or detect a signal-write failure after recording. <br><br> Acceptance: make the signal target unwritable (a directory occupying the expected file is portable), generate a nonempty brief, assert generation errors and no new delivery record exists; successful publication records exactly the published pack; a receipt-write failure leaves a usable signal. Run these through the shared publisher rather than separately reproducing policy in five generators. Correct the contradictory before-write comments at each caller.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-s02"></a>**CTX-S02 — Cross-stage enrichment invents added files and duplicates dependency discovery**<br><br>**Major; architecture and DRY.** `generate/enrichment.rs:23-48` collects dependency `Stage.files` verbatim, while `append_file_concerns:85-108` counts paths mentioned by exactly one dependency as “new file(s) added”. A dependency assigned an existing `src/lib.rs` produces an added-file assertion even if no file was added. Two packages both declaring `src/lib.rs` are reported as a shared-file conflict because `working_dir` is ignored; two distinct spellings of the same repository-relative path can avoid the warning. HashMap iteration also makes multi-stage concern order unstable. <br><br> The existing `retrieval::DependencyChange` owner already resolves accepted Git changes and normalizes declared fallback paths. `retrieve_stage_pack` uses it via `StageQuery::dependency_paths`, and `generate/findings.rs:87-105` calls it again, explicitly accepting repeated Git work because “sharing them costs a signature”. Compute dependency changes once per signal build and pass a borrowed snapshot to source boosts, dependency findings, and enrichment. This removes repeated process launches and prevents the three sections from observing different dependency states during one build. Preserve fallback behavior, dependency order, stage working-directory joining, and excluded globs. The current change record has paths, not statuses: label them changed/assigned paths, or obtain Git added status before asserting “new”. Use deterministic iteration for rendered concerns. <br><br> Acceptance: existing modified file is not labelled added; identical package-relative paths in different working directories are distinct; equivalent normalized paths overlap; fallback declarations are identified as declarations rather than observed changes; one build uses one dependency snapshot across ranking and prose. This is a bounded orchestration refactor around an existing type, not a new repository indexing layer.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-s03"></a>**CTX-S03 — Sandbox prose mixes stage overrides with effective permissions**<br><br>**Major; architecture and misleading output.** `generate.rs:252-292` builds most of `SandboxSummary` from `stage.sandbox` alone; only Unix sockets and missing grants use `sandbox::stage_sandbox`. `sandbox/config.rs:78-119` inherits plan filesystem/network configuration when a stage has no override. A plan allowing `example.com` with a default stage therefore produces `allowed_domains=[]`, and `format/sandbox_section.rs:67-84` states “No network access allowed.” Plan-level deny paths and excluded commands likewise disappear. `SandboxSummary`'s “Merged sandbox configuration” documentation is false for these fields. The renderer also nests the allow-write list inside a deny-list condition (`sandbox_section.rs:18-44`), so an allow-only summary omits its grants. <br><br> Resolve `MergedSandboxConfig` once using the existing `stage_sandbox` owner, then project the display summary and missing-grant check from it. Keep the deliberate knowledge-write-grant policy: a capability intended only for the privileged knowledge command must not become a generic direct-write recommendation merely because it appears in the merged filesystem grants. Explicitly filter or label that grant at the presentation boundary. Render allow-only rules independently of deny rules.
