Consumers are fresh standard/integration/distill signals; recovery currently omits the sandbox section entirely. <br><br> Acceptance: plan-only network grants, stage network replacement, plan-only filesystem denies, allow-only summaries, inherited excluded commands, and missing absolute grants all match the effective launcher configuration. Keep existing missing-grant and Unix-socket tests; they cover only the fields already merged correctly.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-s04"></a>**CTX-S04 — A quoted merge header can turn a stage session into a live merge resolver**<br><br>**Critical; architecture and validation ownership.** `merge.rs:130-145` identifies a merge signal with `content.contains("# Merge Signal:")`, so a normal signal whose assignment, handoff, or knowledge excerpt quotes that string qualifies. `parse_merge_signal_content:359-389` requires only a stage ID, which every normal signal has; source/target branch may be empty. In `find_live_merge_session_for_stage:67-90`, a successfully parsed signal's session record is checked only for PID liveness, not `SessionType::Merge` or matching record stage. Those identity checks exist only in the unreadable-signal fallback at `attribute_unreadable_signal:108-126`. A live ordinary stage signal quoting merge-source text can therefore be returned as its stage's resolver. <br><br> Affected consumers include resolver spawn/give-up/blocked-retry/stop in `orchestrator/core/merge_handler/` and `commands/stage/merge_resolver.rs:77`. The false identity can suppress the actual resolver or route resolver lifecycle handling to an ordinary session. Classify the actual top-level signal header and use the session record as the authority for type/stage in both readable and unreadable branches. Keep the existing distinctions: absent records are stale, unreadable unattributed records retain uncertainty, and unrelated-stage records are left alone. Do not weaken the recovery protections added for unreadable signals. <br><br> Acceptance: a standard signal with a fenced or prose `# Merge Signal:` mention is ignored even with a live PID; a readable merge-shaped signal naming a Stage session is ignored; record/signal stage mismatch does not adopt another stage's process; real readable and unreadable merge-session cases retain current behavior. `merge_liveness_tests.rs:48-134` covers type attribution only for unreadable signals, leaving the readable branch untested.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-s05"></a>**CTX-S05 — Test-only metrics generation forks production signal assembly**<br><br>**Major; DRY and test representativeness.** `generate_signal_with_skills:59-92` appends v2 sections and stage feedback after formatting. `generate_signal_with_metrics:179-204` independently builds and writes the formatted signal without either append. Repository search finds the metrics generator used by signal tests, including cache/size/doctrine/commit-timing suites, but no runtime caller. It remains publicly exported. A v2 stage measured through this path omits frozen contracts, review instructions, carried findings/suggestions, merge handback, adjudicator feedback, and review dimensions that production can append. Size/metrics assertions over that generator therefore do not describe the complete production artifact. <br><br> One assembly function should produce the final signal and its metrics; the production writer and test inspection path should consume it. Measure appended content explicitly in the appropriate section or a named suffix bucket rather than silently dropping it. If the public metrics generator must remain compatible, delegate it to that assembly with empty skill inputs; otherwise reduce it to a test seam. Acceptance: for identical session/stage/context inputs, the measured artifact equals the ordinary generated artifact and metrics total equals its byte length, including v2 and feedback blocks. Preserve stage-specific signal kinds and the stable-prefix split used by the launcher.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-s06"></a>**CTX-S06 — Signal parsing repeats incomplete Markdown recognizers**<br><br>**Major; DRY and nonidiomatic implementation.** Numbered-list parsing strips only one digit in `format/helpers.rs:121-133`, `parse.rs:177-183`, and `recovery_parsing.rs:143-151`; generators enumerate arbitrary-length lists, so item 10 and later disappear when parsed. Knowledge's `extract_tasks_from_stage` duplicates `format/helpers.rs:98-112` verbatim. Section parsing is independently implemented by `helpers::parse_signal_sections`, `parse::parse_signal_content`, format validation, and recovery parsing; none tracks fences. A quoted `## Target` or `## Acceptance Criteria` in a source window/assignment is treated as signal structure. Recovery reason selection (`recovery_parsing.rs:40-48`) searches the entire document, so a Manual recovery mentioning “Session crashed” in its assignment reads back as Crash. <br><br> Keep structured authority in existing `Stage`/`Session` records wherever callers already have it. For the remaining signal-reader APIs, share a small section iterator that preserves heading boundaries and fence state, an anchored field parser, and an ASCII numbered-list parser accepting all digits. Reuse the existing task extraction function from knowledge generation. Avoid adding a full Markdown dependency for these constrained emitted forms. Recovery reason must come from the Reason field in Recovery Context, not a body-wide substring. Public read/update APIs currently have only in-repository test consumers, so describe their compatibility surface honestly rather than inventing a runtime dependency. <br><br> Acceptance: round-trip 12 tasks/actions, a Manual recovery whose prose contains all other reason labels, fenced headings/field examples, and ordinary supported legacy Target field spelling. Keep handling malformed required identity as an error; warning-only format checks must not be mistaken for validation. Update `EXPECTED_SECTIONS` only for real emitted top-level sections or derive that vocabulary from the shared section owner; its “across every signal kind” claim currently omits v2/contract sections.<br><br>
<a id="ctx-s07"></a>**CTX-S07 — Comments describe historical extraction mechanics instead of current contracts**<br><br>**Minor; verbose/invalid comments.** `signals/mod.rs:23-37,76-83`, `helpers.rs:11-25`, `cache/blocks.rs:1-20`, `format/codex.rs:1-11`, and `recovery.rs:17-22` repeatedly explain file moves, visibility mechanics, maintainability-ledger pressure, and old test filters. Replace with concise current ownership/behavior notes; file-size debt belongs in the ledger, not every sibling module. `generate/context.rs:27` promises “both stage and session info” although its signature has no session input. `format/skills.rs:5` calls skill rendering “task progression”. `format/brief.rs:139-141` claims the spawn query label is the whole free-text query although `retrieval::STAGE_QUERY_INPUTS` deliberately supplies a fixed label. <br><br> Retain non-obvious policy explanations: unavailable Codex fallback, required-vs-advisory skills, byte-identical doctrine cross-surface assertions, bounded untrusted excerpts, absent-vs-unreadable lifecycle handling. Remove unsupported guarantees rather than deleting defensive code. No tests are required for purely documentary cleanup; behavior corrections above have focused acceptance criteria.<br><br>

## CLI, hooks, installation and integration

<a id="int-01"></a>**INT-01 — Embedded skill writes follow leaf symlinks outside the installation tree**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** architecture, correctness, contradictory boundary comments. **Status:** established from the write path; not executed during this audit. <br><br> **Locations:** `loom/src/assets/install/placement.rs:29-48` (`place_skill`), `:51-65` (`write_skill`), `:81-109` (`clear_non_directory_ancestors`), `:133-146` (`prune_dir`). Callers: `assets/install/claude.rs:20-38`, `assets/install/codex.rs:17-42`, `assets/install.rs:65-72`. <br><br> **Trigger/evidence:** An existing real skill directory contains `SKILL.md`, or another shipped asset file, as a symlink to an unrelated writable file. `place_skill` checks the skill directory, and `clear_non_directory_ancestors` checks only the destination's parent chain. `fs::write(&dest, content)` at line 64 follows the remaining leaf symlink and truncates its target. `prune_dir` preserves that same leaf because its relative name belongs to the keep set. A dangling file symlink can similarly create the target. This is reachable through `loom install-assets`, installer/update paths, and both Claude and Codex skill placement; no malicious embedded asset key is required. <br><br> **Consequence:** Updating an installed skill can overwrite an unrelated file outside the selected skill tree without a backup. The explicit directory-symlink containment policy at lines 25–28 is incomplete at file leaves. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Before writing each shipped leaf, inspect it with `symlink_metadata` and replace a symlink with an ordinary file, consistently with the skill-directory policy. Prefer a sibling temporary regular file and rename if adopting the existing atomic-writing facility is straightforward. Decide and test directory-at-file behavior explicitly. Keep doctrine/agent/command write-through behavior separate: those paths intentionally support dotfile symlinks and take backups. <br><br> **Constraints/consumers:** Preserve destination-before-other-root removal, pruning only known skill names, traversal rejection, nested directory-link replacement, and core/all layout migration. Do not change global installation-root symlink semantics as part of this leaf fix. <br><br> **Acceptance:** Extend `assets/tests/placement.rs` with live and dangling leaf links at both `SKILL.md` and nested asset paths; target bytes/existence remain unchanged, installed leaf is regular and correct. Exercise a real Claude/Codex installer entry as well as `place_skill`. Existing placement tests cover only an intermediate directory symlink and `..` asset key (`:13-58`).<br><br>
<a id="int-02"></a>**INT-02 — Codex preflight advertises versions the forwarding wrapper refuses**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** DRY, architecture, incorrect comments. **Status:** established contract mismatch. <br><br> **Locations:** `loom/src/codex.rs:265-281` (`codex_companion_installed`), `:291-310` (`codex_lane_status`, cached `codex_lane_available`); `loom-hooks/codex-forward.sh:311-322` (companion resolution). <br><br> **Trigger/evidence:** The Codex CLI is present, the installed cache has a companion at a version other than `1.0.6`, and the wrapper selects companion mode. Rust returns available if *any* version directory contains `scripts/codex-companion.mjs`. The wrapper constructs exactly `.../codex/1.0.6/scripts/codex-companion.mjs`, rejects absence or a symlink leaf, and fails before launching. Rust's `.is_file()` also accepts the symlink leaf rejected by the wrapper. Its comment explicitly says “the presence of any version's companion script is enough,” which is false for this consumer. <br><br> **Consequence:** Startup omits the unavailable-lane warning, generated stage context licenses the lane, and the assigned forward fails. The result is cached for the daemon lifetime. The direct lane's conditional sandbox probe does not make the companion-mode scenario unreachable. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** `commands/run/checks.rs::advisory_codex_lane_preflight`; `orchestrator/signals/generate/context.rs::build_embedded_context_with_stage_and_session`; `commands/repair/settings_checks.rs:169-183` (`codex_slash_tmp_issue`); `agents/loom-codex-forwarder.md`; wrapper fixtures in `loom-hooks/tests/codex-forward-wrapper.sh:11-25`. Graph impact and literal search confirm the first three Rust consumers. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Align preflight with the pinned version and file-safety requirements now. Then centralize the supported version/path contract in a small build input emitted to Rust and the installed wrapper, or an existing embedded asset contract mechanism. Keep version pinning until a version's wire behavior is explicitly supported; replacing the wrapper's pin with an arbitrary “latest” glob would discard a compatibility safeguard. Treat CLI-only direct availability separately if changing preflight to model that mode. <br><br> **Acceptance:** One temporary-home contract fixture should cover supported version, only unsupported version, supported plus unsupported versions, absent script, and symlink leaf; preflight and companion-mode wrapper agree. Existing wrapper fixtures pin `1.0.6`; inspected `codex_tests.rs` tests config and model fallback, not this parity.<br><br>
<a id="int-03"></a>**INT-03 — Development installation kills every same-user `loom` process**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** ownership architecture, invalid user-facing claim. **Status:** reachable wrong scope. <br><br> **Location:** `dev-install.sh:70-76`, especially `pgrep -x loom` and `pkill -x loom`; test fixture `scripts/tests/test_dev_install.py:30`. <br><br> **Trigger/evidence:** Complete a successful development install while another repository has a Loom daemon, a status/web server, a foreground command, or another long-running `loom` operation. Matching only the executable name supplies no repository, daemon-role, or recorded-identity restriction. `pkill` can signal all matching processes the caller may signal. The printed “Killing loom daemon...” claims a narrower operation than the command performs. The development installer tests always stub `pgrep` to exit 1, bypassing this branch. <br><br> **Consequence:** An install in this checkout can interrupt unrelated work and UI processes, including operations whose lifecycle should be coordinated by their own daemon. Suppressing `pkill` errors and waiting one second does not establish that the intended daemon exited. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Reuse the repository-aware daemon shutdown operation for this checkout, or adopt the existing updater's stale-daemon notice and let the operator restart the intended run. `commands/self_update/daemon_notice.rs:15-35` already resolves a workspace and names its daemon; `commands/clean/mod.rs:117-129` uses `stop::ensure_daemon_stopped`. If all-repository daemon restart is a real requirement, enumerate positively identified daemons and expose that scope explicitly rather than killing by process name. <br><br> **Constraints/consumers:** Preserve installation ordering: do not stop the intended daemon until both binary and hook assets are installed. A failed build/install must still avoid process termination. Do not signal test processes or the current installer invocation. <br><br> **Acceptance:** Extend installer stubs to assert repository-scoped shutdown only, no broad `pkill`, no shutdown on install failure, and explicit handling of shutdown failure. A test with two independently attributed daemon fixtures must leave the unrelated one alive. Add the Python installer tests to an invoked check if they remain outside CI; the inspected workflow has no invocation of `scripts/tests`.<br><br>
<a id="int-04"></a>**INT-04 — Public Rust hook validators are a disconnected and stale policy replica**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** DRY, redundant code, architecture, invalid comments. **Status:** established in-repository dead-policy surface; external library callers were not assessed. <br><br> **Locations:** `loom/src/hooks/validators/mod.rs:1-28`; `validators/bash.rs:28-155`; `validators/file_path.rs:28-108`; `hooks/mod.rs:31`; `hooks/config.rs:114-203`. Live owners: `loom-hooks/worktree-isolation.sh`, `worktree-file-guard.sh`, `fs/permissions/hooks/config.rs`, and `orchestrator/terminal/native/session_settings/contents.rs:179-210`. <br><br> **Evidence:** Graph impact for both `validate_bash_command` and `validate_file_path`, followed by repository literal searches, finds their own tests/doctests and exports, with no production invocation. Their stated purpose is “Rust implementations of the validation rules enforced by the `worktree-isolation.sh` hook.” The Bash replica remains raw regex plus a second heredoc/message stripper, while the installed shell path uses the shared command tokenizer and additional checks. The Rust file validator explicitly permits `../sibling/file.txt` and state heartbeat/signals files; the live file hook rejects every parent component at `:223-227` and rejects heartbeat/signals writes through the external shared-state root at `:358-374`. The handoffs exception at `:370-371` is deliberate and must remain distinguished. <br><br> **Related duplicate:** `HooksConfig::to_settings_hooks` is another emitter used by test fixtures, while production capsule generation independently emits the actual session hooks and interpreter wrapping. `HookEvent` itself remains live and is used by both capsule generation and settings cleanup. Do not delete that enum or the event-log reader: `commands/stage/skip_retry.rs` consumes `read_stage_events`. <br><br> **Consequence:** Passing Rust tests can be mistaken for validation of installed guard behavior. Maintaining tests, error types, exports, and comments for an uncalled policy doubles changes without improving enforcement. Fixtures built by a legacy emitter need not represent real capsule settings. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Remove/deprecate the unused validator surface and migrate any uniquely valuable cases to the installed shell-hook tests. Preserve actual OS confinement and live hooks. Migrate `HooksConfig` fixture construction to the production emitter or a deliberately named test fixture; retire its unused production struct/fields after checking library compatibility. Keep `HookEvent` as the live registration source. Do not wire the weaker replica into enforcement merely to make it used. <br><br> **Affected tests/exports:** `hooks/tests.rs`, `orchestrator/mod.rs` reexports, `loom/tests/worker_evidence/setup.rs`, `sandbox/config/preflight/tests.rs`, `commands/repair/tests_local_keys.rs`, `fs/permissions/tests/hooks_tests.rs`; correct stale `HooksConfig` construction comments in drift tests. <br><br> **Acceptance:** No production policy symbol remains supported only by self-tests. Shell regression cases cover the former Rust cases under genuine stage/worktree fixtures. Production capsule registration tests continue to pin hooks, matchers, and interpreter handling; absence of an unused public API is handled according to the crate's compatibility policy.<br><br>
<a id="int-05"></a>**INT-05 — Init and clean duplicate the same socket-reaping policy and types**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** DRY, architecture, excessive comments. <br><br> **Locations:** `commands/init/cleanup.rs:44-170` and `commands/clean/sessions.rs:17-117`. Each defines `SessionReapMode`, `SocketOutcome`, `handle_socket`, and the same attributed/dead/live sweep. The latter explicitly justifies duplication because the first module is private and widening visibility was “out of scope for this fix” (`:17-22`). <br><br> **Evidence/consequence:** Both implementations decide attribution, probe liveness, kill a socket server, warn on failure, remove the socket, and count the same outcomes. This is ownership-sensitive shared policy, not incidental formatting. A future safety correction must be made twice. The clean path already adds native terminal tombstone cleanup outside the common sweep, so sharing entire commands would be inappropriate. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Extract the socket policy, mode, outcome/statistics, and bounded sweep into the existing terminal/tmux ownership area. Return structured outcomes/warnings; keep CLI wording and clean-only tombstone work in command adapters. Migrate one caller, assert behavior parity, migrate the second, then remove duplicated types and historical explanation. This should coordinate with the runtime reviewer rather than make `commands::init` a shared service. <br><br> **Constraints:** Leave unattributed sockets untouched in both modes. `OrphansOnly` must preserve live sessions; destroying state requires attributable live-session cleanup first. Do not casually preserve the current “remove even if kill failed” behavior as desirable: separately decide whether a failed live kill should retain its last socket handle and stop destructive state removal. This review records duplication, not a validated replacement lifecycle. <br><br> **Acceptance:** Shared policy tests cover unattributed, dead attributed, live orphan-only, live destructive, kill failure, and remove failure outcomes. Both command tests exercise the shared policy and retain their distinct reporting/extra cleanup behavior.<br><br>
<a id="int-06"></a>**INT-06 — Daemon and browser protocols depend on a CLI-owned status collector**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lens:** architecture. **Status:** substantive boundary problem, not an observed wire defect. <br><br> **Locations:** `daemon/protocol.rs:241` stores `Box<commands::status::data::StatusData>`; `daemon/server/status.rs:13-19` calls that command-layer collector; `commands/status/data/mod.rs:94-113` defines the shared payload; `commands/status/data/collector.rs:321-379` loads stage/session/merge/quota/token/event state; `commands/status/web/model.rs:6-12,202-231` derives browser attention from `commands::status::render`. <br><br> **Evidence/consequence:** The long-lived daemon's wire DTO and filesystem read model are owned under a CLI command, while the dashboard's nonterminal attention model is obtained from a render module. Presentation and service ownership are entangled: changes to a command/render subtree can change shared daemon/web payload semantics, and reuse requires importing the CLI namespace. This is especially consequential because collection performs session correlation, freshness filtering, and sanitization, rather than simply rendering strings. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Move the existing status DTOs, collection helpers, merge read report, and presentation-independent attention facts to one shared status/read-model owner. Keep static text, terminal styling, TUI widgets, HTTP routing, and browser transport adapters where they are. First move types with temporary reexports and byte-for-byte wire parity, then collector/attention facts, then update consumers and remove reexports. Avoid introducing a generic repository/service framework. <br><br> **Affected consumers:** daemon protocol/server/broadcast consumers; `commands/status.rs`; CLI render/TUI; `commands/status/web/{model,broadcast}`; terminal resolver's session inventory import; existing web schema fixtures and status collector tests. Coordinate with the planner/frontend reviewer; this finding owns the shared data boundary rather than UI design. <br><br> **Constraints/acceptance:** Preserve serde names/defaults/optional fields, exact session ownership correlation, stale-token omission, terminal escape sanitization, merge/blocked guidance, and daemon-to-file fallback. Existing collector behavior tests plus daemon/web serialized fixture parity should pass without changing payloads. The daemon protocol should no longer import `commands::*`; the browser DTO should no longer depend on terminal rendering for semantic facts.<br><br>
<a id="int-07"></a>**INT-07 — `_common.sh` couples unrelated hook state through a large shared namespace**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** architecture, excessive coupling. **Status:** maintainability recommendation; no timing claim. <br><br> **Locations:** `loom-hooks/_common.sh` has 1,728 lines: message/heredoc stripping (`:34-82`), command parsing/wrapper classification (`:146-853`), diagnostics/dependency checks (`:873-926`), stage lookup (`:946-990`), heartbeat locking/ownership/writes (`:1008-1230`), process ancestry/agent identity (`:1247-1590`), containment helpers (`:1598-1667`), reviewer override and worker-brief assembly (`:1675-1728`). Many registered hooks source it, including both guards and lifecycle writers. <br><br> **Evidence/consequence:** `loom_reviewer_model_override` modifies the caller's `MODEL`, `EFFORT`, `MODEL_SOURCE`, `NEEDS_REWRITE`, `WARN_ESCALATION`, and `WARN_REVIEWER`; `loom_worker_brief` reads several spawn-guard globals. Those are spawn policy internals, yet they reside beside concurrency-sensitive heartbeat locking and a permissive shell grammar. This is a concrete responsibility/hidden-input problem, beyond exceeding a line limit. `_bounded.sh`, `_lifecycle.sh`, `_read_ledger.sh`, and `_codex_forward.sh` already demonstrate smaller library boundaries in the tree. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Move parser/command-inspection code into a parser library; move the heartbeat lock/owner primitives together into a heartbeat library; keep identity/stage resolution in an identity library; put spawn-specific overrides/brief assembly next to `spawn-guard.sh` with explicit inputs and prefixed outputs. Retain a temporary `_common.sh` compatibility loader while migrating callers in small groups. Keep the strict forwarding argv parser separate from the permissive guard scanner: their acceptance contracts intentionally differ. <br><br> **Constraints:** Preserve Bash 3.2, nounset/errexit behavior, source guards, nested `sh -c` recursion/length failure rules, fallback checks after parse failure, stdin/stdout protocol discipline, heartbeat lock acquisition ordering, stale-owner policy, and no-jq failure posture. Do not rewrite the parser simultaneously with the file movement. Embedded library registration and installed hook drift checks must include every new file. <br><br> **Acceptance:** Existing shell cases run against installed asset layouts on Linux and system Bash 3.2; heartbeat owner/lock tests and token-helper cases retain behavior. A caller audit shows spawn-only functions/globals no longer in the default shared library. Measure hook latency separately if performance is used to justify a later change.<br><br>
<a id="int-08"></a>**INT-08 — Repair dispatch treats human descriptions as executable identifiers**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** architecture, non-idiomatic code, coupling. <br><br> **Locations:** `commands/repair.rs:15-19` (`RepairIssue` has only severity and display strings), `:213-280` (`fix_issue` and helper dispatch); `commands/repair/settings_checks.rs:61-70,173-181` are representative producers. <br><br> **Evidence/consequence:** Fix selection uses `WorkspaceFix::classify(&issue.description)`, `fix_settings_issue(..., &issue.description)`, and literal `contains`/`starts_with` checks for old skills, old agents, `exclude_slash_tmp`, phantom merges, and incoherent stages. Some fix functions receive the description itself to recover the target. Thus wording is part of a hidden execution protocol. An editorial change can silently turn a fix into “Skipped” or send it through a broader settings arm; the comment at `:239-246` documents ordering dependencies between message fragments. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Add a typed action/kind carrying the necessary target data to `RepairIssue` (or a separate diagnostic/action pair). Construct it at detection time, dispatch exhaustively on it, and render descriptions independently. Keep a variant for diagnostic-only/manual issues rather than guessing actions from unknown strings. Migrate one detector family at a time; remove parsers only after all producers move. <br><br> **Consumers/constraints:** All detector modules listed by `check_all_issues` (`:157-170`), workspace classification, settings/drift fixes, home-asset migration, merge-state repair, coherence repair, output and tests. Preserve user-authored deny rules, manual-only failures, confined target paths, and recovery behavior; typed actions do not authorize additional repairs. <br><br> **Acceptance:** Changing a diagnostic's wording must not change its selected action. Table tests map each detected kind to exactly one action, with target data retained. Unknown/manual issues stay skipped. Tests still demonstrate that user-authored settings rules are untouched.<br><br>
<a id="int-09"></a>**INT-09 — Forwarder guidance and sandbox comments describe superseded behavior**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR for active agent guidance; associated stale comments are MINOR. **Lenses:** invalid comments/documentation, cross-language contract drift. Related to INT-02. <br><br> **Exact claims and contradictions:** <br><br> • `agents/loom-codex-forwarder.md:59-60`: “If the one snapshot is still running, the report has `state: active` and no `LOOM-FORWARD-END`; the job continues under daemon ownership.” `codex-forward.sh:363-374` instead calls cancel, emits an END with `outcome:"timed_out"`, prints evidence state `timed_out`, and exits 124 for queued/running/timed-out snapshots. The installed instructions tell the shim/reader to reason about an output shape this branch no longer emits. <br> • `loom/src/codex.rs:103-108,121-124`: comments call `~/.claude/plugins/data/codex-openai-codex` the companion's state directory. `codex-forward.sh:324-326` unconditionally exports `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA=$HOME/.codex/plugin-data`. The old grant is still code, so cleanup requires tracing all consumers before removal; the prose is already wrong for this wrapper. <br> • `codex-forward.sh:187` prints “nested Seatbelt refused” in every direct evidence trailer, although `:250-252` also selects direct mode after a Linux bubblewrap refusal. The preceding deferred note is accurate, but the final evidence trailer contradicts it. <br> • `loom/src/main.rs:30-31`: “`complete` runs at the tail of a stage.” `cli/dispatch.rs:189-192,381` dispatches `loom complete` to dynamic shell completion. Stage completion is `loom stage complete`. Keep its quiet update policy, correct the reason. <br> • `hooks/config.rs:143-146` points readers to `generate_hooks_settings` and settings carrying `LOOM_WORK_DIR`; the current capsule owner is `orchestrator/terminal/native/session_settings`. Retire the obsolete emitter with INT-04 rather than polishing misleading legacy construction documentation alone. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Rewrite the forwarder rule to describe bounded wait/cancel/timeout output and distinguish missing authoritative END from explicit timeout/unknown outcomes. Correct the state-root and direct-mode reason text; audit grant consumers separately before narrowing permissions. Correct command and hook-owner references. Keep prompt text concise; leave low-level ownership guarantees in code/tests rather than repeating incident history in agent instructions. <br><br> **Affected consumers/constraints:** Installed forwarder asset, wrapper output consumers/receipts, startup lane diagnostics, sandbox capability assembly, doctrine/asset contract tests. Preserve START/END identity correlation, exact unit/invocation behavior, timeout outcome interpretation, and the rule that unknown evidence never establishes success. <br><br> **Acceptance:** Existing completed/failed/cancelled/running/queued/timeout wrapper fixtures agree with documented behavior. Test Linux and macOS direct-mode evidence reasons without requiring a live provider. Agent guidance should name only emitted states and the actual mode transition, with no assertion that a canceled unit keeps running normally.<br><br>
<a id="int-10"></a>**INT-10 — Dispatch wrappers and comments encode line-count accounting rather than interfaces**<br><br>**Severity:** MINOR. **Lenses:** redundant wrappers, excessive/invalid comments. **Status:** optional simplification, not a wrong-behavior finding. <br><br> **Locations/evidence:** `cli/dispatch.rs:163-167` says extraction exists because “every new arm has to buy its line back”; `:189-193` wraps one call solely so `loom project` fits a “ledgered line count”; `:214-230` accepts the full `Commands` enum for only sessions/worktree and needs an `unreachable!`; `:232-236` repeats the line-ceiling explanation. `commands/clean/mod.rs:133-137` and `commands/status/data/collector.rs:89-90` also narrate line-budget-driven extraction. <br><br> **Consequence:** Historical accounting obscures the behavior contract. The broad-enum helper makes illegal calls representable and forces a runtime unreachable arm, although its inputs are already matched by the parent. These comments do not explain a behavioral invariant or reusable boundary. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Inline trivial wrappers when the containing function remains readable, or use narrowly typed subcommand dispatchers that own a coherent command family. Keep genuine seams such as `dispatch_completions`' multi-branch behavior. Replace line-count narratives with one-line responsibility descriptions; retain concurrency, protocol, sandbox, and platform rationale. Do not pursue blanket comment deletion or violate project size limits to remove a two-line helper. <br><br> **Acceptance:** Clap surface and dispatch destinations remain unchanged; existing CLI surface tests suffice. No production dispatcher takes a broad enum solely to immediately reject unrelated variants.<br><br>
<a id="int-e01"></a>**INT-E01 — Failed updater rollback deletes the recovery file named in its error**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** correctness, ownership architecture, invalid comments. **Locations:** `loom/src/commands/self_update/install.rs:34-55` (`install_binary`), `self_update/mod.rs:247-287` (live caller), `self_update/tests/install_binary.rs:4-13` (unsupported explanation for absent failure coverage). <br><br> **Evidence/trigger:** The old executable is renamed into a `TempPath`. If the staging rename fails and the rollback rename also fails, the function returns an error telling the operator to copy `backup_path` manually. Returning drops that `TempPath`, which attempts to unlink the old executable. A concurrent directory entry at `current_exe`, a filesystem fault, or changed permissions can produce asymmetric rename failure; the error path explicitly exists to handle it. When unlink remains permitted (for example a directory now occupies the destination while the parent remains writable), the only backup is deleted. The test comment claims that there is “no directory- or file-level state reachable from a test that fails one same-directory rename but not the other,” overlooking destination changes and fault injection, then uses that claim to omit the rollback test. <br><br> **Consequence:** Manual recovery instructions name a file the error return deletes. The install also temporarily removes the public executable path between the first and second rename despite being described as atomic. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Preserve ownership of the backup on rollback failure (`keep`/persistent recovery location) and report the actual retained path. Prefer the bootstrap installer's approach of retaining the active executable until an atomic replacement rename, with a separately preserved backup; do not conflate that improvement with silently dropping recovery evidence. Reuse `install.sh:239-246`'s ordering concept, not shell invocation from Rust. Add a small injected filesystem-operation seam if needed to make double failure deterministic. <br><br> **Consumers/constraints:** `loom update`; signed binary validation remains before writes, mode remains executable, stage and backup remain on the destination filesystem, normal success cleans temporaries, and failure before replacement leaves a runnable original. Do not alter signature verification or add unsafe downgrade fallback. <br><br> **Acceptance:** Deterministic tests for success, staging failure with successful rollback, and staging plus rollback failure. Last case asserts original bytes remain at the exact reported recovery path after the function has returned. A concurrent reader test or injected step assertion establishes that the installation path is never absent if the replacement algorithm is made atomic. Rewrite the test comment to describe the actual tested failure model.<br><br>
<a id="int-e02"></a>**INT-E02 — HTTPS-only regression test accepts the failure mode it claims to distinguish**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** misleading comments, ineffective regression coverage. **Location:** `loom/src/commands/self_update/tests.rs:76-89`, `self_update/client.rs:22-28`. <br><br> **Evidence:** The test comment says a connection error at loopback port 1 differs from “the scheme rejection asserted here.” The assertion only checks `result.is_err()`. Removing `.https_only(true)` still yields a connection error for the stated fixture and passes this assertion. Thus it does not protect the live security setting it names. <br><br> **Smallest sufficient repair:** Assert the precise error category/cause for a disallowed scheme, or run a local HTTP listener and assert that a plaintext request is never delivered. Keep redirect downgrade checks separate and test their actual custom policy. Do not introduce a live external network dependency. <br><br> **Consumers/constraints/acceptance:** Shared updater client and its HTTPS-only setting; the test must fail when that setting is removed or plaintext is accepted and must not pass merely because the destination is unreachable. Use a deterministic local fixture with bounded shutdown. No production HTTPS defect was established in this read; this is a test-quality finding.<br><br>
<a id="int-e03"></a>**INT-E03 — The terminal shutdown fixture releases its client before shutdown starts**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** ineffective lifecycle coverage, invalid comments. **Location:** `loom/src/commands/status/web/terminal/tests_upgrade/shutdown.rs:125-164,167-202`. <br><br> **Evidence:** `attach_live_terminal` binds the client socket to local `_bridge_client` at line 152 and returns only the bridge JoinHandle at line 164. Returning drops the socket. The caller connects another client, sleeps 200 ms, and only then sets `running=false` at line 181. The bridge can already exit on its peer disconnect and release its slot. The assertion at lines 159-163 establishes a nonzero count before the helper returns, not at shutdown. This contradicts the claim that it holds a “real, live terminal” through shutdown and can pass with terminal shutdown draining broken. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** Return an owning fixture containing the live client and bridge handle; keep that client alive until the server and bridge complete shutdown. Assert the slot is occupied immediately before flipping the shutdown flag. Preserve the late HTTP connection used to check refused upgrades and use bounded reads/joins. <br><br> **Consumers/acceptance:** Dashboard shutdown and terminal Slot lifetime tests. A mutation that removes the bridge's shutdown observation must fail this test while ordinary peer-disconnect tests remain independent. No production shutdown defect is established by this finding.<br><br>
<a id="int-e04"></a>**INT-E04 — Settings comments preserve a resolution rule the current tests explicitly reject**<br><br>**Severity:** MINOR. **Lenses:** invalid comments. **Locations:** `loom/src/commands/status/web/config_api.rs:13-15`; `config_api/tests/updates.rs:104-105`; `config_api/tests/resolution.rs:167-199`; `config_api/tests.rs:135-152,291-313`. <br><br> **Evidence:** The module describes only two project-scoped keys, while the implementation's tested registry scope also includes pressure, models, and plan keys. The unset test says “a keyless [context] would still win whole and resolve to the built-in”; the resolution test expressly proves a context section containing only prompt_cache_split falls through to the user ceiling. These comments give future edits the wrong fallback rule. <br><br> **Smallest repair/constraints:** Describe the shared workspace registry as the scope owner; describe removal of empty sections as normalization, without inventing an effective-value requirement. Keep key-level fallback, derived context-window behavior, and unknown sibling preservation as established by the existing tests. <br><br> **Acceptance:** Comment statements agree with the shared workspace resolver and the cited tests; no behavior change or new tests are needed.<br><br>
<a id="int-e05"></a>**INT-E05 — Direct Codex supervision does not retire the child on TERM/HUP**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** ownership architecture, duplicated lifecycle policy, correctness. **Locations:** `loom-hooks/_codex-direct.py:265-300`, `_retire:207-230`; `loom-hooks/codex-forward.sh:135-141,259-269`; lifecycle tests `loom-hooks/tests/codex-direct-lifecycle.py:29-55` and `codex-forward-direct.sh:111-163`. <br><br> **Evidence/trigger:** The Python supervisor starts Codex in a new session (`start_new_session=True`). It only invokes retirement on normal result collection or a Python BaseException. It installs no SIGTERM/SIGHUP handler; the default action for those signals terminates Python directly, bypassing its exception/finally cleanup. Sending TERM to the supervisor, or terminating its enclosing shell process group, leaves Codex in its separate session. The shell wrapper's sole EXIT trap deletes temporary logs and does not own or retire that new group. <br><br> **Consequence:** Cancellation can stop supervision and evidence reporting while the direct Codex process continues writing the stage worktree. Its normal 25-minute deadline disappears with the supervisor. The existing tests cover an internal timeout and a stubbed failure to signal children, not termination of the supervisor itself. <br><br> **Cross-boundary confirmation:** Runtime reviewer traced `orchestrator/terminal/native/mod.rs:330-361`, `native/pid_guard.rs:53-75`, and `tmux/backend.rs:127-146`: terminal teardown closes a window/server or signals the identity-checked stored PID, without a general escaped-descendant traversal guarantee. Rust OwnedProcess retirement belongs to bounded command invocations, not every terminal session. Do not assume it compensates for this gap. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** Make TERM/HUP request bounded retirement while Python still owns the child and stream handles, then report cancellation or unresolved ownership accurately. Establish signal handling before launch, cover the child-spawn/registration window, and restore prior handlers for an importable supervisor. Preserve PID/start-time checks and cleanup deadlines. Uncatchable SIGKILL remains an explicit limit unless an external ownership primitive is introduced. <br><br> **Consumers/acceptance:** Direct wrapper lane on Linux/macOS and PostToolUse lifecycle evidence. Deterministic fixture starts a long-running child (plus descendant), observes its identity, sends TERM and HUP separately to the supervisor, and verifies all owned children are gone within the cleanup budget. Exercise cancellation during startup and while draining output. No terminal completion may be emitted while ownership is unresolved.<br><br>
<a id="int-e06"></a>**INT-E06 — The settings projection duplicates section validation and disagrees with pressure execution**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** DRY, architecture, observable correctness. **Locations:** `loom/src/user_config/workspace.rs:189-250` (`resolve`, `section_key`, `section_readable`); runtime `loom/src/fs/work_dir/config_sections/pressure_config.rs:15-24,112-117`; consumers `commands/status/web/config_api/entries.rs`, `commands/config/tui/state/row.rs`, `commands/pressure/mod.rs:204`. <br><br> **Trigger/evidence:** Set project `[pressure]` to `claude_model = "sonnet"` and `rounds = -1` (or an integer above u32::MAX). The runtime deserializes rounds as Option<u32>, rejects the entire section, and falls back to user/default values for every pressure key. The settings resolver's imitation of deserialization checks only `value.is_integer()`. It therefore admits the section and reports sonnet as the effective Project model, even though execution will use the user/default model. It independently rejects rounds only when resolving that key, after having accepted its sibling model. <br><br> **Consequence:** Both settings editors can show a model/effort as effective that pressure will not use. Their claim of shared resolution is wrong at precisely the malformed-file recovery boundary. Existing parity tests cover a string field with numeric shape but not an integer outside the runtime's numeric domain. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** Have the shared workspace projection use the actual typed section parser and its validated accessors, or extract one typed resolution owner both execution and editors call. Do not fix only this range check and leave a second structural deserializer to drift when fields/types change. Preserve whole-section rejection for structural parse failure, per-key fallback for well-typed off-list enums and zero rounds, and untouched unrelated config fields. <br><br> **Acceptance:** For valid settings, invalid enum strings, unknown fields, negative/overflow rounds, and zero rounds, assert every projected pressure key and source agree with `read_pressure_config` plus the user fallback. Use scratch config paths; no external processes/network are required.<br><br>
<a id="int-e07"></a>**INT-E07 — Update refresh locks belong to the exiting launcher rather than the fetcher**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** ownership architecture, concurrency, invalid comments. **Location:** `loom/src/update_check/mod.rs:112-154,173-195,209-249,266-296,325-329`; tests `update_check/tests.rs:168-190,219-259`. <br><br> **Evidence/trigger:** `try_create_lock` stamps `std::process::id()`, the foreground invocation's PID. `spawn_refresh` launches a detached child without transferring lock ownership. The foreground command normally exits immediately. While the fetch remains in flight, another invocation sees the recorded parent PID is dead, unlinks the lock, and starts another child. This is the ordinary short-command lifetime described by the module itself, not merely its documented stale-lock TOCTOU race. Each completed child also calls unconditional `release_lock`, which can remove a successor's lock. <br><br> **Consequence:** The “Exactly one fetcher” and “at most once per stale interval” guarantees fail. A slow/unavailable network can cause many detached fetches from repeated CLI invocations before any writes last_checked, consuming resources and unauthenticated API quota. Existing eight-thread test keeps the stamped PID alive and cannot reveal the parent/child handoff bug. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** Give the actual fetch lifetime an ownership token/OS lock that survives launcher exit; let the child acquire/own it and re-check freshness under that ownership before fetching, or explicitly transfer ownership without exposing a stale-parent gap. Release only a lock still owned by this refresh. Keep foreground work free of network waits, the minimum refresh interval, silent failures, atomic state writes, and failed-attempt backoff. <br><br> **Acceptance:** Process-lifetime fixture uses a short-lived launcher and a gated fetch worker. After launcher exit, repeated new invocations cannot start another fetch until the worker finishes or its ownership is verifiably abandoned. A finishing older worker cannot unlink a newer owner's lock. No real GitHub request is needed.<br><br>
<a id="int-e08"></a>**INT-E08 — “Backup exists” is used as proof that later differing content belongs to Loom**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** ownership policy, invalid comments. **Locations:** `loom/src/assets/install.rs:163-183`; `assets/install/claude.rs:56-87,105-114`; `assets/tests/write_assets.rs:44-83`. <br><br> **Evidence:** The comment concludes “every later divergence is loom's own prior output” because an earlier .bak exists. Nothing records the last installed bytes. An operator can edit commands/address.md after the first backup, or a normal upgrade can create that first backup before any operator edit; the next install overwrites their new content without preserving it. The test's third string is labelled next-release output, but identical bytes could be a later user edit and the installer cannot distinguish them. <br><br> **Consequence:** The stated promise to preserve anything the installer did not put there is stronger than the implemented first-copy-only policy. This also affects shipped unnamespaced commands pressure/distill and agent files. Doctrine header policy is separate and deliberately treats marked documents as managed. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** Preserve the original backup while retaining any later unrecognized current content before replacement; if distinguishing unchanged prior shipped content is required, record installed content identity rather than inferring it from backup existence. Do not simply rotate away the operator's original backup. Define the retained-backup policy explicitly and report every displaced user copy. <br><br> **Acceptance:** Start with an original operator command, install, edit the installed command again, reinstall, and verify both operator versions remain recoverable while the installed asset updates. Also cover a first ordinary upgrade creating a backup followed by the first operator edit. Reinstalling identical bytes remains idempotent.<br><br>
<a id="int-e09"></a>**INT-E09 — Save-target labels contradict the editor's cross-scope writes**<br><br>**CRITICAL · reachable wrong behavior / invalid comments.** `commands/config/tui/render/inspector.rs:170–185,264–275` derives the displayed save destination only from `state.active_path()`: “s writes to <active file>” or “s writes nothing here”. `state/save.rs:15–31` intentionally writes every staged edit across both scopes. The consumer regression `tests/scope.rs:78–110` requires saving both files together. <br><br> Stage a user edit, then switch to Project. If a workspace exists the inspector says the project file is the save target while pressing `s` also writes the hidden user edit. Outside a workspace it says “s writes nothing here” while that same key still persists the user file. The renderer's comments claim these labels make pressing `s` unambiguous; the labels instead misdescribe a consequential global write. <br><br> Derive the save summary from staged target scopes, distinguishing the active editing destination from all files a save will touch. Preserve save-all, per-edit errors, successful-edit clearing, and the quit guard across both tabs. Add rendered assertions with a hidden staged user edit on a project tab, both with and without a workspace, followed by the existing headless save path. Do not change persistence to match the misleading label.<br><br>
<a id="int-e10"></a>**INT-E10 — Fresh-process terminal recovery cannot restore raw mode**<br><br>**CRITICAL · reachable wrong behavior / invalid recovery contract.** `utils.rs:72–76,90–130` promises recovery after a previous status TUI is killed. `main.rs:57–66` invokes that recovery in a fresh process; it calls `cleanup_terminal_crossterm` (`utils.rs:187–211`) and deletes the stale marker. Its only termios operation is crossterm's `disable_raw_mode()`. <br><br> The installed dependency is crossterm0.29 (`loom/Cargo.toml:32`). Inspection of its local primary source `crossterm-0.29.0/src/terminal/sys/unix.rs:30,145–170` confirms both libc and rustix implementations restore attributes only when the process-local `TERMINAL_MODE_PRIOR_RAW_MODE` is Some. A fresh loom process has None, so this call succeeds without changing terminal attributes. ANSI cleanup restores display/cursor state, while the promised canonical input/echo recovery remains undone and its retry marker is removed. The status TUI writes the marker at `commands/status/ui/tui/app.rs:108`; config TUI does not create a crash marker at all. <br><br> Separate same-process unwinding (where crossterm owns a real saved termios) from stale-process recovery. For the latter, use an explicitly defined terminal recovery operation on the relevant controlling terminal, or persist validated terminal identity and restore metadata; do not apply state from one terminal to another. Preserve stdout protocol gating and avoid claiming exact restoration without saved prior attributes. Acceptance: a subprocess owns a PTY, enters raw mode and writes the marker, is killed without cleanup, then a fresh invocation restores canonical/echo flags and clears the marker only after successful recovery. Existing tests only check ANSI output gating and formatting and cannot exercise this process boundary. No test was run during this audit.<br><br>
<a id="int-e11"></a>**INT-E11 — Completion installation repeats unsafe write policies**<br><br>**CRITICAL · reachable data loss / DRY / invalid confinement claim.** `completions/install.rs:79–87` tests FPATH writability by truncating the fixed name `.loom_write_probe` and then unlinking it. An existing file at that name loses its content; a symlink truncates its target. This happens while merely selecting the install directory (`zsh_install_path`,59–67). Use a uniquely named, exclusively created temporary file and remove only the file this probe created. <br><br> Separately, `completions/install/refresh.rs:4–18,34–50` states refresh is confined below home and checks only XDG candidates, while the unconditional default candidates can be symlinks outside home. `is_file()` follows them, and `fs::write` overwrites their targets. Lines64–70 explicitly acknowledge that hole yet conclude “Discarding is the safe direction in both cases, so neither is fixed here”; no discard happens for default paths. The asset install/update flow consumes `refresh_existing_in`, so ordinary updates can write outside the promised root. Existing tests exercise lexical XDG escape and regular default files, never default-file or parent-directory symlinks. <br><br> Apply the same confinement and atomic placement policy to every refresh candidate, including defaults, before writing. Preserve intentional skip-missing behavior and the distinction that explicit completion installation may target a selected FPATH outside home. Share already established placement primitives where their semantics match; do not copy another partial lexical check. Acceptance: existing probe bytes and symlink targets survive install-path selection; default and XDG candidates that resolve outside home remain untouched; legitimate in-home existing completion files refresh; missing paths stay absent. Related to INT01, but these are separate call sites and a separate destructive probe.<br><br>
<a id="int-e12"></a>**INT-E12 — Dynamic completion tests duplicate setup without observing completions**<br><br>**MAJOR · excessive/redundant code / invalid test claims.** `completions/dynamic/tests.rs:211–275,293–307`, `tests/tests_other.rs:6–35,76–105,134–235`, and `tests/tests_stage.rs:9–167` repeatedly construct the same workspace/context and assert only `complete_dynamic(...).is_ok()`. Replacing every routed candidate list with an empty list would still satisfy these purported routing/integration tests. `tests.rs:224–225` says stdout cannot easily be tested, although the same module already exposes the pure `route_completion` and subordinate result-returning functions to child tests; `tests_stage.rs:259–271` demonstrates asserting the routed value directly. <br><br> Replace the repetitive no-error cases with a table of command context and exact expected candidates through the existing pure seam, retaining one focused output-format test where useful. No new framework is needed. Keep separate fixtures for status filtering, prefixes, malformed data and missing workspaces where behavior differs. Acceptance: deleting a routing arm or routing a flag to the wrong candidate domain must fail a named case. This repairs test value and reduces ceremony without changing completion behavior.<br><br>
<a id="int-e13"></a>**INT-E13 — Completion value registries and shell fallback policies drift**<br><br>**MAJOR · DRY / architecture; concrete omitted suggestions.** `dynamic/mod.rs:196–237` hand-dispatches only selected value flags despite already having Clap's schema (`commands.rs:75–95`). Thus `--backend` and effort flags have registered finite choices but return no matching value candidates. `dynamic/memory.rs:5–6` separately freezes three entry kinds, omitting `change` and `receipt` supported by the CLI contract (`cli/types_memory.rs:319`) and memory readers. `tests_other.rs:110–117` pins that stale three-item list instead of its source. `scripts/fish.rs:5–12` suppresses filesystem fallback for every command except init, while `dynamic/mod.rs:248–250` explicitly returns no pressure candidates in order to use shell-native paths; pressure path completion works by fallback in bash/zsh but is disabled in fish. <br><br> First resolve finite option values from the same Clap Arg metadata already used for `--field`, retaining specialized dynamic providers only for stages/sessions/files. Source memory-kind candidates from its canonical vocabulary rather than another list. Make path-taking commands share a declared fallback contract across the three scripts (or explicitly enable pressure in fish as the smallest fix). Preserve hidden commands/flags, partial-prefix filtering and no network work on completion. Add value-routing assertions and shell fixtures that exercise actual pressure path fallback; do not treat helper-only filename tests as routed coverage.<br><br>
<a id="int-e14"></a>**INT-E14 — Executable guidance still points at old owners and model defaults**<br><br>**MINOR · invalid comments/guidance.** Newly full-read examples extend INT09: <br><br> • `agents/loom-code-reviewer.md:16` says “This agent defaults to sonnet” while its executable frontmatter sets `model: opus` at5; keep one default statement or derive the prose from the same source. Runtime override capability remains intentional. <br> • `commands/changelog.md:10` says `loom/src/main.rs` surfaces new Clap commands/flags directly; main now only does startup and dispatch. Point reviewers at `cli/types*.rs` and command Args owners, retaining README and commit-range review. <br> • `CONTRIBUTING.md:190` says release workflow runs flake checks; `.github/workflows/release.yml:93–95` explicitly leaves them to main CI. Lines201,211 say platform/key contracts live in self_update Rust and the workflow reads that source, while release.yml30,53,170,195 sources install.sh's canonical contract. Its note still advertises a public key in release notes, but generated notes link installation guidance without embedding it. Correct the documented owner and manual verification link; preserve the installer as the canonical release contract rather than restoring duplicated constants. <br> • `scripts/changelog-section.sh:9–12` promises exact-version preference over a minor group, but awk57 accepts whichever matching heading appears first. If both headings exist with the group first, exact release notes are ignored. Either implement explicit two-pass priority or document first matching heading; a fixture with both in reverse order pins the intended rule. <br><br> These are source/guidance findings, not stale sections found in doc/loom/knowledge. No knowledge CLI write is requested for them.<br><br>
<a id="int-e15"></a>**INT-E15 — Terminal smoke reader spins forever on early EOF**<br><br>**CRITICAL · reachable test/tooling failure.** `scripts/smoke-web-terminal.sh:101–120` loops on `sock.recv(4096)` until HTTP headers, frame headers and frame payload reach expected lengths. Every loop appends an empty byte string on orderly peer close and never exits. The socket's2-second timeout cannot bound EOF: subsequent reads return immediately forever. A server regression closing before complete headers or sending a truncated close frame turns a useful smoke failure into an unbounded CPU loop, holding CI Linux/macOS smoke steps (`ci.yml:146–148,192–194`). <br><br> Factor a tiny bounded exact-read/header-read helper that raises on EOF and enforces an overall monotonic deadline, retaining existing handshake/4004 assertions and token-through-stdin handling. Also bound the script's plain curl requests with a timeout so the intended smoke budget covers stalled HTTP routes. Acceptance: fixture servers closing after0bytes, partial headers, one frame-header byte, and a short payload all cause prompt nonzero exit; valid4004 completion still passes. Preserve EXIT cleanup of server and scratch files.<br><br>
<a id="int-e16"></a>**INT-E16 — MAJOR: shipped skill examples contradict the safeguards they teach**<br><br>**Evidence.** `skills/loom-auth/SKILL.md:141–150` promises reuse detection that revokes a refresh-token family, but deletes the old token and stores only a new token/user/expiry: a reused old token becomes an indistinguishable missing row, and no family is revoked. `skills/loom-c/SKILL.md:120–130` computes `*cap * 2` before its overflow check; unsigned wrap can produce a small allocation or zero, so the example does not preserve the capacity/ownership guarantee it teaches. `skills/loom-concurrency/SKILL.md:137–154` teaches propagation of the first child error but uses `let _ = res?`, discarding an inner `Result` returned by a fallible `fetch`. The same file's bounded/unbounded table puts Go's unbuffered rendezvous channel in the unbounded column (`:123–127`), contradicting its own parenthetical explanation. <br><br> These are installed instruction assets consumed by agents through the skill catalog, not demonstrated defects in Loom's own refresh-token service or C allocation implementation. Their concrete problem is that a copied reference implementation omits precisely the property its adjacent comment claims. They also spread generic, independently maintained safety recipes across a large guidance corpus without executable checks on their examples. <br><br> **Smallest repair.** Correct these examples in their existing owners: retain token-family/reuse state and make rotation transactional, check the capacity before multiplication (including zero-size semantics), propagate both join and operation errors, and describe Go rendezvous separately. Remove unsupported assurances rather than adding a second abstraction or mandatory infrastructure. Do not turn all illustrative snippets into a general-purpose test framework. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Focused example checks should demonstrate replay revokes the replacement family, a near-`SIZE_MAX` capacity is rejected before realloc, and a child operation error reaches the JoinSet caller. Verify the surrounding explanation agrees with the exact snippet and distinguishes illustrative omitted setup from a promised invariant.<br><br>
<a id="int-e17"></a>**INT-E17 — CRITICAL: concurrent stale-lock reclaimers can remove a live successor's lock**<br><br>**Evidence/trigger.** `loom-hooks/_common.sh:1017–1051` (`loom_heartbeat_lock_recover_if_abandoned`) reads dead-owner metadata, checks age/liveness, then removes `owner` and the directory by pathname. Two waiting hooks can both establish that the same old lock is abandoned. Reclaimer A removes it; writer C creates the replacement directory and publishes its live owner link; delayed reclaimer B then removes C's `owner` and directory using its stale decision. C is already inside its critical section, and another writer can now acquire the lock. The hard-link claim in `loom_heartbeat_lock_acquire` protects publication competition, not this recovery race. The adjacent assertion that no compliant writer can replace the directory while it exists overlooks another reclaimer removing it. <br><br> **Consequence/consumers.** This lock serializes PostToolUse, SessionStart, SubagentStop heartbeat read/modify/write, and lifecycle journal appends (`_post-tool-heartbeat.sh:148–164`, `_lifecycle.sh:278–308,337–388`). Losing mutual exclusion can overwrite newer heartbeat context with a value read earlier and permits concurrent journal writers. This is a cooperative race between legitimate hooks after a killed hook, not dependent on a hostile filesystem actor. <br><br> **Smallest repair.** Serialize abandonment reclamation with identity-aware ownership and ensure deletion applies only to the examined generation. A fresh metadata read alone still leaves a check/delete race; choose a protocol whose removal and acquisition cannot cross generations, retaining stock macOS/Bash3.2 support and conservative treatment of malformed metadata. Avoid inventing a separate lock implementation for each caller. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Extend `loom-hooks/tests/heartbeat-protocol.sh:131–156`: its abandoned-lock test has one reclaimer, and its successor test exercises release rather than recovery. Deterministically pause two reclaimers after observing the old owner, let one reclaim and a new writer enter, then resume the second; the new owner must remain protected and a third writer must stay outside until release. Preserve existing atomic-JSON and stale-session tests.<br><br>
<a id="int-e18"></a>**INT-E18 — CRITICAL: retrieval wrapper's timeout/failure contract is lost in duplicated dispatch**<br><br>**Evidence.** `loom-hooks/user-prompt-context.sh:77–86` promises a hard command timeout but its no-`timeout`/`gtimeout` branch directly invokes the delegate. Stock macOS is explicitly supported by the existing `_bounded.sh:24–60` watchdog and the hook already sources it at `:41`; a stuck delegate therefore leaves the wrapper unbounded on that platform. Even the GNU branches omit the shared helper's kill-after escalation. Additionally, `OUTPUT=$(... || true)` loses the delegate status: a delegate that emits a partial response then fails is still printed at `:106–107`, despite the failure-suppresses-output contract at `:12–14,88`. `codex-apply-patch.sh:97–103` independently repeats the same unbounded fallback for edit recording. <br><br> **Repair/consumers.** Route these calls through the already-sourced `loom_run_bounded`, retaining hook-specific budgets and fail-open behavior, and keep command success separate from captured stdout before publishing retrieval output. Preserve empty-success suppression, the byte ceiling, pinned binary/PATH, and read-only retrieval. This removes concrete duplicated portability policy rather than adding a wrapper layer. The external host may impose its own hook timeout; that does not make the script's promised bound true and should not replace its cleanup contract. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Extend wrapper tests with the existing no-timeout PATH fixture: a stalled direct delegate must finish within the wrapper budget, and output followed by nonzero exit must publish nothing. Existing `tests/user-prompt-context-malformed-json.sh` emits no stdout on failure; `single-json-line.sh` supplies already-correct output, so neither detects partial failure forwarding. Keep successful bounded output byte-identical.<br><br>
<a id="int-e19"></a>**INT-E19 — CRITICAL: SessionEnd mistakes task prose for completed lifecycle state**<br><br>**Evidence.** `loom-hooks/session-end.sh:54–77` selects the first glob match and greps the entire stage Markdown for `status: Completed` or `status: Verified`. An Executing stage whose description includes either literal (for example a reproduction or YAML example) is logged `completed:true` and skips the final handoff. This is ordinary task content, not a forged stage record. The shared `_common.sh:loom_find_stage_file` already handles canonical/legacy naming and ambiguous matches; the owner check also demonstrates stopping at frontmatter boundaries. SessionEnd maintains its own weaker interpretation. <br><br> **Repair/constraints.** Reuse canonical stage lookup and read only the authoritative frontmatter status through an existing parser or narrow validated helper. Preserve the best-effort end-of-session path and completion semantics agreed with the runtime, including whether Verified alone means no handoff is needed. Do not infer stage state by searching descriptions or silently choose among duplicate stage documents. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Extend `loom-hooks/tests/session-end-reason.sh` (its existing fake stage is only one `status: Executing` line and removes the Loom delegate): use a real frontmatter document with misleading completed/verified body text and a recording handoff stub; require `completed:false` and one handoff. Add true terminal state and ambiguous canonical/legacy match cases. This also makes the test exercise the actual persisted format.<br><br>
<a id="int-e20"></a>**INT-E20 — MAJOR: terminal/stop hooks copy workspace and stage parsers despite a shared helper layer**<br><br>`loom-hooks/stage-terminal-guard.sh:45–47` explicitly justifies copying helpers from `commit-guard.sh` to avoid sourcing an executable script. The copied groups cover worktree detection, root/state discovery, status parsing, and stage selection (`stage-terminal-guard.sh:52–233`; `commit-guard.sh:37–104,141–250`). This is a false choice: `_common.sh` is already a source-only helper layer and owns `loom_current_worktree`, `loom_find_stage_file`, and stage ownership parsing. `_lifecycle.sh:43–100` adds another stage-file/frontmatter implementation, and SessionEnd has the weaker fourth implementation described in INT-E19. <br><br> This has observable drift: the stop/terminal copies accept the first glob match and a branch-name fallback, while the common lookup rejects ambiguous/symlink matches and its worktree detector explicitly rejects branch-name evidence. A stage status/naming-policy change must therefore be repaired at several shell sites in addition to Rust. The stop guard further reparses the same stage status for its decision, message, and debug appendix; its large case table mostly performs different debug logging before the same allow outcome. The file length is a navigation symptom, not the basis of this finding. <br><br> **Repair/constraints.** Move only the shared workspace/stage lookup and frontmatter access into a source-only owner, reuse it from these hooks, and pass the parsed status forward. Keep hook-specific advisory vs denial decisions, merge/contract exemptions, canonical/legacy paths, nested-worktree behavior, and missing-state fallback explicit. Document intentional differences (if branch fallback is still required) in a small policy parameter or caller branch rather than a copied parser. Avoid moving all hook behavior into an even larger `_common.sh`. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Existing nested-worktree, terminal-guard, heartbeat ambiguity, and contract-session fixtures should exercise the same metadata helper. Add duplicate canonical/legacy, malformed frontmatter, and symlink-stage cases at that owner, while keeping per-hook tests for their distinct stop/deny outcomes. Update the copy-justification comments and the stop guard's remaining 'will block' diagnostics, which contradict its advisory-only exit0 behavior.<br><br>
<a id="int-e21"></a>**INT-E21 — MAJOR — Three meanings of “git commit” coexist in the same hook family**<br><br>**Lenses:** DRY, excessive code, invalid comments. `loom-hooks/commit-filter.sh:232–245` claims its subagent predicate detects actual `git commit` invocations, but checks any argument equal to `commit`; `is_git_commit_command` at325–332 repeats that broad predicate. The same file already contains `git_runs_commit` at334–365, which correctly locates the subcommand after Git global options. `post-tool-use.sh:281` independently uses the broad version. Therefore `git cat-file commit HEAD` and `git log --grep commit` satisfy the subagent gate and post-commit reminder despite being reads. The main-stage gate uses the precise version, and `tests/commit-filter-session-git-commit.sh:67–71` explicitly pins those same reads as allowed for the main role. The subagent test matrix never applies that distinction; `tests/post-tool-use-commit-reminder-tokenized.sh` covers quoted prose but no Git read whose argument is `commit`. <br><br> **Repair/constraints:** put Git-subcommand recognition in the command-analysis helper and reuse it in subagent detection, attribution scanning and post-tool reminders; keep the existing global-option skipping, wrapper resolution, malformed-input policy and session-role differences. Move definitions ahead of the first use. Do not broaden the subagent policy merely to make the predicates agree. This is a narrow consolidation within the existing tokenizer, not a new shell parser. <br><br> **Acceptance:** both roles classify `git -C repo commit` as a commit and `git cat-file commit HEAD`/`git log --grep commit` as reads; a read never produces a post-commit reminder. Existing quote, wrapper, attribution and stage-relay regressions retain their outcomes. Related INT-E20 concerns stage-file ownership rather than this command grammar.<br><br>
<a id="int-e22"></a>**INT-E22 — CRITICAL — Agent type names are incorrectly validated as filesystem identifiers**<br><br>**Lenses:** architecture, duplicated contracts. `loom-hooks/subagent-start.sh:63,114–119` records the harness agent type as a string; `codex-forward-guard.sh:239–246` and its `tests/codex-forward-guard-agent-type.sh:29,75–79` expressly support `codex:codex-rescue`. However, `subagent-stop.sh:108–110` passes agent type to `loom_lifecycle_safe_id`, whose `_lifecycle.sh:11–14` grammar forbids `:`. A legitimate plugin-qualified stop therefore returns before terminal evidence/heartbeat publication. Rust independently repeats the mistake: `subagent_lifecycle/claude.rs:125–126,344–350` validates `[parent_id, agent_id, agent_type]` with the same path-ID predicate, so a shell-only fix remains broken. <br><br> **Consequence:** plugin-qualified Claude workers can have valid start rows but no accepted terminal stop. `commands/subagents/wait/evidence.rs:56–91` turns missing Claude lifecycle evidence into `WorkerOutcome::Active`, keeping an owned wait active until another policy settles it or its deadline expires. The forwarding shim and its Codex child are distinct identities: `codex_outcome` at96–125 can independently obtain the companion's terminal outcome. This finding does **not** claim every qualified Codex child's result is lost. <br><br> **Repair/constraints:** define the agent-type grammar separately from stage/session/agent IDs; permit qualified type names that the harness supports, bounded and safely serialized, and keep exact equality with the registered start type. Retain strict filesystem component validation for identifiers actually used in paths. Apply the contract to both shell and Rust validation, or centralize stop validation at the Rust owner. Do not replace all safe-ID checks with a permissive string check. <br><br> **Acceptance:** a start/stop pair for a plugin-qualified Claude agent publishes terminal lifecycle evidence and settles its owned wait; the supported `codex:codex-rescue` shim's own stop is accepted without changing independent Codex authority. Wrong type, wrong parent/session, unsafe path identifiers and transcript-layout mismatches remain rejected. Existing stop fixtures only exercise unqualified worker/reviewer types; extend both shell and Rust matrices. Runtime reviewer independently confirmed the Rust consumer path.<br><br>
<a id="int-e23"></a>**INT-E23 — CRITICAL: cleanup discards its branch-inspection failure**<br><br>**Lenses:** correctness, boundary design, invalid comments. `commands/clean/worktrees.rs:77–104` deliberately returns `UnmergedBranches.listing_error` when `git branch --list` fails; the documented meaning is that branches may still hold unmerged work. `confirm_branch_deletion` at116–122 discards that field and treats the empty vector as proof that nothing would be lost. `clean/mod.rs:61` accepts that result before forced worktree cleanup (`worktrees.rs:211–213`) and root-directory removal (`269–276`). A transient inspection failure can therefore skip the required unmerged-work confirmation and continue deleting. The comment “Nothing committed-but-unmerged would be lost” is false on this branch. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** return an actionable inspection error before any destructive operation when `listing_error` is present; distinguish an empty successful listing from unknown branch state. Keep explicit `LOOM_CLEAN_YES` policy separate from an inferred safe listing. Preserve existing per-branch ahead-query conservatism and cleanup ordering. This is separate from the git cleanup owner’s scaffold `ls-files` failure finding. <br><br> **Acceptance:** inject a failed listing with an existing unmerged stage branch and assert no terminal/worktree/branch/state deletion occurs, both interactive and noninteractive; successful empty listing still proceeds, and a known unmerged listing retains confirmation behavior.<br><br>
<a id="int-e24"></a>**INT-E24 — CRITICAL: reconciler startup does not acquire or validate its lease**<br><br>**Lenses:** architecture, correctness, invalid comments. `hook/reconcile_graph.rs:118–126` calls `begin_run` then executes a graph pass unconditionally. `reconcile_graph/lock.rs:232–233` overwrites any current holder and discards the result of its locked write. A direct `loom hook reconcile-graph` invocation bypasses the live-holder/backoff policy; an unwritable lock also permits an uncoordinated rebuild. A delayed detached child can overwrite a newer claimant, despite the surrounding “at most one reconcile” contract. Parent-side `rebind_claim` checks ownership but child-side startup does not. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** make startup return an acquired lease; direct runs must use the normal claim policy, and spawned children must take over only their specific parent claim (nonce/generation, not merely a PID). Do not execute a pass after a failed lease write. Preserve pending coalescing, failure backoff, dead-holder recovery and test spawn suppression. Cancellation (`cancel.rs:36–41`) also releases a lease after an identity-query or signal failure: retain it on unknown liveness and release only after confirmed termination or confirmed stale identity. A confirmed unrelated PID may safely lose its stale lease. <br><br> **Acceptance:** injected live holder, failed write, delayed child/newer claim, and failed identity query invoke zero additional graph passes; ordinary parent-to-child handover and queued second pass still work. Existing tests cover parent rebind and end-pass takeover, not denied child startup. No claim of graph-file corruption is needed: overlapping full-tree rebuilds already violate the documented resource bound.<br><br>
<a id="int-e25"></a>**INT-E25 — CRITICAL: repeated reviewer stop certifies edits the reviewer never saw**<br><br>**Lenses:** architecture, correctness, test contract. `hook/review_harvest.rs:140–152` parses an existing transcript and computes the current worktree fingerprint at harvest time. Its duplicate check includes that new fingerprint. `review_harvest_dedupe_tests.rs:75–84` explicitly harvests a report, creates `changed.txt` with “after the review”, then harvests the unchanged transcript and requires a new round. The documented duplicate SubagentStop therefore can rebind an old clean report to new code. Completion’s fingerprint gate can then see current reviewed state despite no review of the new edit. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** bind each reviewer invocation to a trusted fingerprint when the review begins and preserve that identity across retries; reject or mark stale any report whose reviewed tree cannot be established. A repeated stop must not manufacture a fresh reviewed fingerprint. Coordinate reviewer launch, lifecycle/stop evidence, immutable round storage and completion review coverage; keep meaningful revised reports and malformed-round diagnostics. Do not rely on agent-authored claims alone for this binding. <br><br> **Acceptance:** review A, modify tree to B, replay the identical stop/transcript: no round may certify B and completion must require a new review.
