Concurrent harvests remain idempotent. Explicit new reviewer invocation over B is accepted. Distinct from VER-21’s overly permissive semantic fingerprint normalization.<br><br>
<a id="int-e26"></a>**INT-E26 — CRITICAL: review transcript FIFO blocks before regular-file validation**<br><br>**Lenses:** correctness, shared boundary ownership. `hook/review_transcript.rs:98–107` opens a path with `O_NOFOLLOW`, then checks `metadata.is_file()`. A FIFO with the accepted absolute `.../<session>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl` shape blocks inside `open` before the type check; tail byte caps do not bound opening. Direct hook CLI invocation hangs; a shell deadline limits that caller but loses the harvest. Existing harvest tests cover symlinks and large regular files, not FIFO. The same omission reaches the once-per-second daemon collector through `status/data/execution_models.rs:141–147` (raw File::open before a byte cap). `status/data/disputes.rs:40–48` first stats length, then uses unbounded read_to_string: a FIFO passes the zero-length test and blocks; replacement/growth also defeats its advertised 1MiB cap. The neighboring busy transcript reader already uses open_regular_no_follow correctly. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** use the existing shared nonblocking, no-follow regular-file opener before seeking/reading; retain identity/path/size validation. This belongs with VER-09’s and CTX-12’s shared safe-read boundary work, rather than another bespoke opener. Acceptance: a correctly shaped FIFO returns promptly without a writer and creates no round; regular tail, mismatch and symlink fixtures retain behavior.<br><br>
<a id="int-e27"></a>**INT-E27 — MAJOR: pack filtering duplicates bookkeeping and worker coverage drifts**<br><br>**Lenses:** DRY, ownership, correctness. `hook/worker_brief.rs:183–201` removes scoped-out plan/forwarder items and increments `omitted`, but leaves `omitted.coverage.included`, `included_tokens`, and weakest-included score describing the original pack. The parallel filtering owner in `user_prompt_compose.rs:255–320` recomputes these fields explicitly; `tests_user_prompt.rs:259–289` pins `included + omitted == candidates`. Worker fixtures initialize empty coverage and assert only content removal, so they miss this drift. A worker brief omitting one of two candidates can describe two included plus one omitted. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** put accounting for a narrowed item set on `ContextPack` (or a single context helper) and call it from prompt and worker filtering. Keep separate admission policies: prompt emit floor must not become a worker/stage floor. Preserve required-item unmet records, token recomputation, source fencing, and delivery of exactly the retained units. Acceptance: a populated two-item coverage fixture loses one scoped item and reports one included/one omitted with matching token sums; prompt dedupe/size-shrink tests still pass.<br><br>
<a id="int-e28"></a>**INT-E28 — MINOR: hook comments repeat implementation history and overstate contracts**<br><br>**Lenses:** excessive comments, invalid comments. `user_prompt_compose.rs:129–176` spends48lines explaining a five-line existential predicate; retain the exact/term rule and the important hook-only scope distinction, move incident narrative to knowledge. File-splitting rationales repeat at `user_prompt.rs:28–31`, `user_prompt_attachments.rs:13–15`, compose4–8, tests_user_prompt352–368, and each child test header; the module structure itself already expresses the split. `reconcile_graph.rs:297–338` repeats the spawn-guard incident and test-linkage explanation; retain one compact warning that integration tests link a non-cfg(test) crate and must explicitly disable spawning. <br><br> Specific corrections: `context_ceilings.rs:17` says malformed config gives “both built-in defaults”, while42–50 consults the user main ceiling; `forward_receipt.rs:285–288` says equality compares “every field but observed_at”, while289–297 omits model/effort as well (identity is indirectly in receipt_id). `init/plan_setup.rs:27–40` claims “every check that can FAIL” has run, although checked-out-branch validation is later at182 after workspace/hooks/settings creation. `repair/sandbox_settings.rs:99–104` says worktree settings.local.json “is the sandbox of a possibly LIVE stage session”, while129–131 correctly says sessions launch from capsules. Update these to current behavior without deleting safety rationale. <br><br> **Acceptance/constraints:** documentation-only review against referenced bodies; preserve timing, fail-open/fail-closed, scope and isolation contracts. No ceremonial tests required. Related to INT-E04 and INT-E14, but these are additional fully read call paths.<br><br>
<a id="int-e29"></a>**INT-E29 — CRITICAL: migration deletes unrelated user assets based on a prefixed namesake**<br><br>**Lenses:** ownership, data loss, non-idiomatic dispatch. `repair/home_assets.rs:94–121` declares any unprefixed skill obsolete when `loom-<name>` exists;127–155 does the same for agents. `fix_old_skill`185–194 recursively deletes that directory and `fix_old_agent`198–206 deletes the file. A user's independently authored `rust` skill alongside installed `loom-rust` is sufficient; no manifest, content identity, or legacy ownership evidence is checked and no backup is made. Generic `loom repair --fix` therefore destroys a valid user customization. This is distinct from asset installation backup preservation (INT-E08). <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** recognize only demonstrably Loom-owned legacy assets (known content/recorded ownership), otherwise report ambiguity and preserve them; retain a recoverable backup on migration. Carry the exact asset path in a typed issue instead of extracting it from prose: the apostrophe split at188/201 also truncates legitimate names. Update old-reference migration210–227 to match complete permission names rather than textual prefixes (`Skill(rustic)` currently matches `Skill(rust`). Share the typed repair action with INT08. <br><br> **Acceptance:** unrelated customized rust/loom-rust coexist unchanged; known legacy content migrates with backup; apostrophe-bearing asset names never target another asset; complete old permission references migrate while similarly prefixed user permissions remain byte-equivalent semantically. Explicit repair authorization is not evidence of legacy ownership.<br><br>
<a id="int-e30"></a>**INT-E30 — CRITICAL: a leftover relay ticket hides the authoritative applied result**<br><br>**Lenses:** state ownership, correctness. `request/status.rs:85–88` returns PendingRelay whenever the scratch ticket exists, before consulting the inbox or durable ledger. `hook/relay/ticket_check.rs:34–44` deliberately treats failed ticket removal as nonfatal because re-enqueue is idempotent. Thus a successfully relayed and applied request whose ticket cannot be removed is reported as never relayed; `request status --wait` immediately settles on that false pending state (112–118), then errors. The test named `a_pending_ticket_wins_over_the_inbox` does not populate a competing applied ledger record. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** let an authoritative matching inbox/ledger state take precedence; use the scratch ticket only when there is no accepted request evidence. Preserve genuinely unrelayed ticket handling and session/request identity validation. Keep enqueue idempotence and cleanup diagnostics. Acceptance: ticket plus Applied, Rejected or Applying evidence reports that evidence; ticket alone remains PendingRelay; missing evidence remains NotFound. Distinct from RT-18 ledger torn-tail repair.<br><br>
<a id="int-e32"></a>**INT-E32 — MAJOR: status retains obsolete render paths and a validation stub**<br><br>**Lenses:** redundant code, misleading comments. Repository-wide references show `render/completion.rs:51–191` has no production callers: both the screen renderer and line renderer are unused, while `ui/tui/app.rs:114` and `run/foreground.rs:149` call `render/summary.rs:20`. Their separate sorting, duration and table implementations still carry tests and public re-exports, and claim to be the completion screen for the TUI. `render_activity_status` and `render_context_bar` likewise have no production callers. `validation.rs:41–44` always returns zero, yet status validation calls it as reference validation; its comment only records removed runner/track history. `display/tests.rs` tests unrelated stage parsing rather than the sole production `count_files` function. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** establish whether any supported external Rust consumers rely on these public exports; remove unused internal render paths/re-exports/tests when none do, retaining the live shared completion summary. Remove the no-op validation call and obsolete historical comment, or implement a concrete promised reference check if one is part of the CLI contract. Keep parser tests with their parser owner. Consolidate the duplicate Markdown scans in diagnostics/validation around one result-producing reader while preserving different reporting policies and surfacing unreadable inputs. Acceptance: both live completion consumers still show final summaries; no active renderer or public compatibility commitment is removed merely because an internal grep is empty.<br><br>
<a id="int-e33"></a>**INT-E33 — CRITICAL: skipped cleanup failures recommend a command that refuses them**<br><br>**Lenses:** command-contract drift, correctness. `render/attention_model.rs:197–198,221–225` turns every cleanup warning into `loom worktree remove <id>`. `render/attention.rs:268–272` explicitly documents that warnings include Skipped stages. Yet `worktree_cmd.rs:208–220` requires Completed and a retained completed commit before safe removal. A skipped stage whose deferred cleanup failed gets an actionable-looking command that deterministically refuses; TUI/web share the same attention model. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** derive guidance from the same cleanup capability/policy used by the worktree command. Offer the safe retained-history cleanup path for skipped resources if supported, otherwise explain the refusal and require the existing exact destructive confirmation only after the operator chooses that action. Never bypass ancestry or change a skipped stage to Completed to make the hint work. Acceptance: Completed cleanup warning retains its verified removal command; Skipped warning provides an actually accepted safe recovery path or explicit inspection/confirmation guidance. Cover command execution, not only matching the rendered string. <br><br> INT-E31 is a duplicate consumer of **CTX-17**: both `commands/skill_index.rs:303–321` and `skills/index.rs:174–200` use transformed-string offsets. CTX-17 is canonical; preserve both marker/sentence/CSV contracts when sharing the repair.<br><br>
<a id="int-e34"></a>**INT-E34 — CRITICAL: plan titles and stage names bypass status terminal sanitization**<br><br>**Lenses:** boundary ownership, invalid contract comment. `data/sanitize.rs:38–78` promises to flatten every untrusted display string but does not touch `StageSummary.name`; the collector copies it directly at174 and `render/attention.rs:177–183,280–285` writes it to the operator terminal through colored text. `collect_status_data` also returns the raw `plan_name` from `load_plan_name:311–317`; `plan/parser/extraction.rs:29–46` only trims/prefix-strips the first H1. Static status202–203 and compact13–14 print that title directly. A valid quoted YAML stage name containing an escaped ESC, or a plan H1 containing ESC/OSC controls, reaches terminal output unchanged. Stage IDs are validated by list_all_stages, so the confirmed trigger does not require an invalid ID. History evidence already has its own sanitizer and is not part of this gap. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** apply the shared display-string sanitation at the aggregate boundary to names/titles, preserving identity keys and full text according to each presentation contract. Audit newly added string fields through one documented boundary rather than claiming all are covered by a partial list. Acceptance: collect then render a blocked stage with OSC52/CSI/bidi in its name and plan title; no original control survives in static/compact output, while ordinary Unicode names remain readable. Include the same sanitized payload in live TUI and web consumers.<br><br>
<a id="int-e35"></a>**INT-E35 — CRITICAL: TUI terminal acquisition has no rollback before construction succeeds**<br><br>**Lenses:** resource ownership, correctness. `ui/tui/app.rs:72–82` enables raw mode, then performs fallible alternate-screen entry and Terminal::new before a TuiApp exists. Either error returns through `?`, so its Drop cleanup is unavailable; the panic hook only handles panics and is installed after alternate-screen entry. This leaves the user's terminal raw or in the alternate screen on an initialization I/O failure. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** acquire terminal modes through a small guard immediately after each successful acquisition; transfer ownership to the app only after construction succeeds, and unwind partial setup on every error. Reuse this lifecycle for other crossterm UIs where applicable, without changing normal quit/signal behavior. Acceptance: injected failure after raw mode and after alternate-screen entry restores only acquired modes; successful startup/Drop and explicit cleanup still run exactly once. Distinct from INT-E10's fresh-process stale-marker restoration limitation.<br><br>
<a id="int-e37"></a>**INT-E37 — A/B cleanup recursively removes an unowned output directory (CRITICAL)**<br><br>`scripts/retrieval-ab:125–129,184–190` accepts any `--out` and immediately executes `rm -rf "$OUT"` under `--clean`; it does not canonicalize, reject the repository/home/ancestors, or establish ownership. `--clean --out "$PWD"` from the checkout removes tracked and untracked project data. The default temporary path does not establish the comment's claimed guarantee at93 that cleanup can never touch tracked files. Normal seeding also recursively removes fixed children under the supplied directory (294–300). <br><br> Make the harness own a marked child directory, validate the canonical containment boundary before either deletion path, and refuse foreign/preexisting unmarked directories. Preserve supported custom output roots and worktree deregistration. Acceptance: a clean of an owned harness tree succeeds; repository/home/ancestor roots, symlink redirections and foreign output content remain untouched and produce a clear refusal. No destructive reproduction was run.<br><br>
<a id="int-e38"></a>**INT-E38 — Executable skills repeat obsolete verification and liveness contracts (MAJOR)**<br><br>`skills/loom-dead-code-check/SKILL.md:53,64,80,85,164` repeatedly says analyzer exit codes are ignored and a missing tool can silently pass. The owner `verify/goal_backward/dead_code.rs:45–55` evaluates default assertions and records an unavailable-analysis gap on nonzero/timeout failures; `dead_code_execution_tests.rs` covers these outcomes. Its120s timeout and line-substring matching remain correct. Rewrite the shared explanation, comparison table, examples and checklist together; retain pattern-driven warning detection but require successful analyzer execution. Acceptance: examples and prose agree for successful warnings, nonzero analyzer exits, missing executables and timeouts. <br><br> `skills/loom-orchestration/SKILL.md:91–93` correctly labels exit6 hung/stalled, then calls an idle worker's silence positive evidence of death. No transcript growth is not PID death; the runtime uses separate process/worktree/host-job probes. Keep the prescribed cancellation-and-confirmation protocol, describe timeout as a stall signal, and do not teach callers that it proves termination. The300s stage default in plan-writer is current (`models/stage/mod.rs:31`), so that value needs no correction. <br><br> There is a concrete DRY owner problem behind this maintenance burden: orchestration140–192's allocation policy is repeated nearly verbatim in plan-writer151–201 despite the introductory ownership pointer; authoring-detail repeats exploration, model defaults, worker ownership, memory and dispute policy already in the main skill. Keep one authoritative executable policy per topic and let role-specific references carry only invocation/context differences. Preserve self-contained worker briefs by quoting applicable rules at dispatch, rather than maintaining independent source copies. Existing render/signal consumers and catalog paths must remain reachable. <br><br> Additional INT-E16 recipe evidence: `skills/loom-debugging/SKILL.md:123` advertises loop-until-failure but pipes `cargo test` through `tee` without enabling pipefail, so a failing test normally continues and its evidence is overwritten. `loom-dead-code-check:119` recommends `--features=all` for all feature gates (a feature named `all`, not Cargo's `--all-features`). Correct executable examples and add small recipe validation where already supported; do not create a new framework merely for illustrative prose. <br><br> **Supplement: INT-E38 additional confirmed consumers and limits.** <br><br> • `loom-usage/SKILL.md:680` says changing stage files has no effect and no reload exists, prescribing stop/reinitialize. Runtime owner confirms `orchestrator/monitor/core.rs:134–135,197–199` loads stages/sessions each poll, and recovery sync projects lifecycle state into the graph each tick. Distinguish plan-definition snapshot changes from live lifecycle reload; preserve CLI-only mutation and do not recommend direct state edits. Acceptance: operator guidance covers a CLI stage-state update without destructive reinitialization. <br> • `loom-plan-writer/SKILL.md:315` calls300s acceptance an absolute cap, although its own `references/sandbox.md` correctly documents extended timeout overrides up to3600. Verification owner confirms the override (VER-02). `references/stage-sizing.md:11` calls idle a death threshold while34 denies elapsed-time proof. Reconcile each pair at one owner. <br> • `loom-wiring-test/SKILL.md:160` offers `use crate::auth` as proof of a real consumer, despite its own rule and checklist rejecting import-only wiring. A removed production call leaves that check green. Replace the example with the actual invocation and behavioral proof; preserve the distinction between syntactic presence and runtime reachability. <br> • `references/sandbox.md:118–122` and main plan-writer369 describe pre-commit as executing inside every session sandbox. Runtime owner confirms commits now relay to a daemon host path. Align this prose with the existing commit execution/confinement owner; do not infer that every session needs extra network grants solely because it commits. <br> • The reachable-only statements conflict between orchestration250 and v2-contracts99, but the exact current runtime guard was not rechecked under the quota closure. This is an unresolved documentation consistency observation, not a separately confirmed runtime defect. Language adapter sections likewise contain differing escaping descriptions; VER-01 owns proven unanchored-selector behavior, and no additional adapter defect is claimed here.<br><br>
<a id="int-e39"></a>**INT-E39 — A/B harness maintains a second, stale evaluation contract (MAJOR)**<br><br>`scripts/retrieval-ab:512–563` calculates the proportion of cases with any expected top-five hit, then names it `precision_at_5` and renders it as precision@5 (681–688). The case file now explicitly distinguishes hit@5 from per-pack precision and prompt delivered-set judging (`loom/eval/retrieval-cases.yaml:21–37`). The shell scorer only reads expect/forbid/pass_floor: it ignores relevant, abstain, precision_floor and the hook-filtered prompt pack. Thus a top-five list containing one relevant item and four irrelevant items gets100% under this label, and a conversational abstain regression is never scored. This is duplicated evaluation logic that has drifted from the Rust owner, not a ranking-quality conclusion. <br><br> Use a versioned shared evaluation output where both binaries support it, or explicitly label the historical comparison hit@5 and implement only the agreed common metrics. Retain raw failures, distinguish unavailable metrics from zero, and preserve fair identical-data comparisons. Acceptance: one-of-five relevance reports0.2 precision and1.0 hit rate; forbidden/abstain cases and unavailable legacy metrics remain distinguishable. <br><br> The same harness's comments overclaim measurement guarantees:713–714 calls the first hook invocation page-cache-cold even though main815 runs all knowledge-context cases against the same roots first;231 says the no-timeout fallback never wedges although it runs the binary unbounded (shared INT-E18 mechanism). Its mtime fingerprint754 is second-resolution metadata over three paths, so unchanged values cannot prove no content mutation; concurrent unrelated mutation likewise cannot prove this invocation caused it. Describe the measured conditions precisely or introduce actual controlled isolation/evidence at the existing harness owner. Avoid expanding the823-line shell/Python/jq mixture with another parallel scorer.<br><br>

## Existing dashboard and shared web

<a id="dash-01"></a>**DASH-01 — Enter on a graph terminal button opens stage details**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** correctness, ownership of interaction behavior. **Confidence:** direct event-path trace. <br><br> **Evidence:** `web/src/components/graph/stage-graph.tsx:123-130`, `Canvas.onKeyDown`, handles Enter from any descendant of `.react-flow__node-stage`, calls `preventDefault()` and opens the stage. `stage-node.tsx:100-115` nests the terminal control inside that node. `web/src/components/terminal/terminal-glyph.tsx:72-81` stops propagation only in `onClick`. A keyboard user tabs to the enabled terminal button and presses Enter: its keydown reaches Canvas, which opens details and cancels native button activation. Pointer activation works, so existing click tests miss the fault. The descendant details button also overlaps two activation owners. <br><br> **Repair:** restrict the Canvas Enter shortcut to the actual focusable node wrapper, or explicitly ignore interactive descendants and already-handled events. Keep native button/link semantics. Do not add broad keyboard propagation stops across every card or remove wrapper keyboard access. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** graph stage wrappers, details button, TerminalGlyph, React Flow keyboard navigation, state key, `useOpenStage` and `useOpenTerminal` query routing. Preserve Escape focus clearing, native Space behavior and pointer pin/double-click behavior. <br><br> **Acceptance:** real keyboard activation of the terminal button changes the route to the terminal view exactly once; focused wrapper Enter opens details; focused details button activates once; state-key controls and Escape still work. Use a keyboard helper that generates native activation rather than a bare keydown assertion. Existing terminal glyph and overview tests are the natural homes.<br><br>
<a id="dash-02"></a>**DASH-02 — The status watchdog does not bound WebSocket handshakes**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** correctness, state-machine architecture. <br><br> **Evidence:** `web/src/api/ws.ts:114-123`, `checkLiveness`, checks only `live` and `error`. `openSocket` at 133-148 has no connection-attempt deadline; its progress timestamp is first set in `onopen`. A socket that emits neither open nor error/close leaves initial `connecting`, or a later `reconnecting`/`offline` attempt, outside the watchdog. The application waits for the browser/network implementation's own eventual failure instead of its advertised recovery timing. `web/src/components/header-lines.tsx:38-45` additionally treats a bootstrap HTTP snapshot as current while phase is `connecting`, displaying a frozen daemon/tick value during the initial stalled handshake. <br><br> **Repair:** track a deadline for each current connection attempt, independently of whether it has opened. Expire a stalled attempt through the existing generation-safe close/backoff path. Make the bootstrap display become stale when the live connection cannot establish. Preserve the distinction between HTTP bootstrap data and a live WebSocket; do not mark HTTP arrival as socket liveness. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** bootstrap fetch generation guard, `connectionAtom`, `DaemonLine`, visibility-change checks, injected clocks/timers and `dispose`. Preserve outage `since` across retries, stale-socket event suppression, monotonic snapshot application and no concurrent reconnect loops. Browser timeouts may eventually recover today; this finding is the missing application bound, not a claim every browser hangs forever. <br><br> **Acceptance:** initial and retry sockets that never fire any event time out and retry; an HTTP snapshot does not remain apparently live beyond the deadline; a late old-socket open/message is ignored; disposal cancels pending work; open sockets still use the existing message watchdog. Extend socket/bootstrap/watchdog and shell tests.<br><br>
<a id="dash-03"></a>**DASH-03 — Geometry memoization is invalidated by every new stage array**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** excessive work, architecture, invalid comment. <br><br> **Evidence:** `web/src/components/graph/stage-graph.tsx:77-87` says “dagre must not re-run each second” and memoizes the set of stages with token rows, but `layoutStages` also depends on the entire `stages` array. `web/src/state/apply.ts:9-20` installs each accepted snapshot; parsed later snapshots carry fresh stage objects/arrays. `web/src/lib/graph.ts:130-176` rebuilds the graph and unconditionally invokes `dagre.layout` at 155. Telemetry-only changes therefore redo layout despite unchanged node dimensions and topology. This is demonstrated invalidation, not a measured latency claim. <br><br> **Repair:** give geometry a stable projection keyed by ordered stage IDs, dependency edges and actual node dimensions/token-row presence. Cache coordinates/routes against that projection. Bind the current StageSummary and token data by ID when building React Flow nodes, rather than retaining old stage objects from a cached layout. Do not merely remove `stages` from dependencies: that freezes geometry or card data. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** `layoutStages`, node height calculation, `buildNodes`, `buildEdges`, rank captions, React Flow measurements and fit behavior. Preserve first-duplicate handling, dangling/self-dependency rules, dimension changes from status/content, edge clearance, and first-paint/refit semantics. Keep domain data in snapshot state; the geometry cache owns geometry only. <br><br> **Acceptance:** spy on layout calls: heartbeat/token-number-only updates retain coordinates without invoking Dagre, while displayed stage values refresh. Stage insertion/removal, dependency changes, node-height changes and token-row appearance invalidate geometry. Retain existing geometry/edge-routing tests. Rewrite the comment to describe the actual cache key.<br><br>
<a id="dash-04"></a>**DASH-04 — Remote binary startup regressions silently pass as environment skips**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** test reliability, excessive error erasure, invalid comment. <br><br> **Evidence:** `loom/tests/web_host.rs:143-160`, `spawn_remote_dashboard`, converts spawn errors to `None` and treats any missing expected startup line the same way. At 184-186 the real-binary contract test prints `SKIP ... dashboard did not start (no bindable wildcard address?)` and returns success. A binary startup panic, changed CLI argument, missing log line or timeout can therefore remove every remote-viewing/auth assertion while leaving the test green. The helper comment equates failure to print with “no bindable wildcard address”; that is not established. Child stderr is piped at 71 but not consumed for diagnosis. <br><br> **Repair:** detect the specific unsupported bind capability separately if an environment skip is necessary. Once that precondition passes, return a diagnostic Result from spawn/startup and fail on spawn failure, premature child exit or missing expected line. Retain bounded waits, capture bounded stdout/stderr and keep ChildGuard cleanup. Do not replace bounded startup with an indefinite wait. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** only the real-binary remote wildcard test and its process helpers. Preserve isolated temporary home/workspace, ephemeral port and token handling. This is distinct from integration's shutdown test losing its bridge client before shutdown. <br><br> **Acceptance:** a fixture child that exits early or never prints the startup line causes failure with useful diagnostics; an explicitly demonstrated bind restriction is the only skip path; valid startup executes every viewing/bootstrap/unauthenticated-write assertion.<br><br>
<a id="dash-05"></a>**DASH-05 — Human-readable strings carry typed domain meaning across the wire**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** architecture, duplication. **Kind:** maintainability repair, not an asserted current producer mismatch. <br><br> **Evidence:** `web/src/components/attention-status.ts:5-29` duplicates Rust attention labels into StageStatus; its own comment admits “A label rename on the Rust side desyncs this map with no compile-time signal”. Unknown labels become blocked. `web/src/components/stage-history-model.ts:73-113` independently parses Rust Display transition names, verdict text and command prefixes into history outcomes; unrecognized endings become resolved. Display copy consequently controls tone/glyph/outcome semantics. Current spelling matches tested fixtures, but neither boundary expresses this contract structurally. <br><br> **Repair sequence:** (1) add explicit serialized attention status and issue/round disposition at the backend projection owner; preserve the human label/reason. (2) Extend dashboard schemas/fixtures and render those fields. (3) Normalize older persisted string-only history at one backend compatibility boundary, using an explicit unknown outcome when meaning cannot be established. (4) Remove frontend literal parsing after all producers supply the fields. Avoid rewriting persisted historical explanations merely to fit a new enum. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** attention panel/now rows/history row marks, notification classification where it relies on labels, status guidance/projection producers, issue-history retirement and adjudication-round projection. Integration owns backend enum/schema-parity test weakness; coordinate that repair rather than adding another global-string search. Preserve current human text, automatic-attention semantics and older history readability. <br><br> **Acceptance:** changing a human label or ended-by explanation leaves typed tone/outcome unchanged; every producer disposition is represented in its owning schema; unknown legacy endings display unknown rather than successful resolution; existing fixture history retains its current meaning. This is a small boundary migration, not a frontend/domain rewrite.<br><br>
<a id="dash-06"></a>**DASH-06 — Admitted body reads can outlive the server's shutdown drain**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** lifecycle architecture, misleading contract. **Kind:** established lifecycle behavior; policy must be made explicit. <br><br> **Evidence:** `loom/src/web_server/serve.rs:97-101,128-133` stops accepting and waits at most three seconds for slot count to fall, then returns without joining or cancelling remaining workers. Its handler comment at 135 promises completion “or until running clears”. `http.rs:150-174`, `read_body`, has only the inherited five-second per-read timeout and never checks running or a total deadline. A valid admitted config POST with an incomplete body can survive the drain and complete later. Integration confirmed `commands/status/web/config_api/request.rs:74-105` then calls update without a shutdown check; `config_api.rs:108-126` writes the config. A client delivering bytes within each read timeout can prolong that lifetime. <br><br> **Repair:** choose and encode the lifecycle contract at shared request handling: cancelled incomplete mutating requests must observe stop/deadline, or admitted mutations must drain to a documented completed state before ownership is released. Thread a cancellation/deadline into body reads and check the mutation gate immediately before applying an update. Use a synchronization boundary if the guarantee is strictly no mutation after serve returns; a lone atomic check has a race. Keep a bounded shutdown path and correct the handler comment. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** shared HTTP body reader, connection slot accounting, dashboard config handler and shutdown tests. The request was authorized before shutdown: this is not an auth bypass. A CLI process exiting may terminate the worker; the lifecycle discrepancy matters for callers retaining the process/server resources. Preserve already-committed updates and legitimate in-flight completion according to the chosen policy. <br><br> **Acceptance:** open an authorized partial POST, stop the server, complete the body after the drain boundary and assert the selected cancellation/completion contract and disk state. Cover trickled bytes and timeout, slot release and normal complete POST. Do not weaken Host/Origin/cookie/CSRF checks. Integration's vacuous bridge shutdown test remains a separate finding.<br><br>
<a id="dash-07"></a>**DASH-07 — Palette and settings contracts have parallel hand-maintained copies**<br><br>**Severity:** MINOR. **Lenses:** duplication, excessive maintenance. **Kind:** targeted simplification. <br><br> **Evidence:** `web/src/components/theme-picker.tsx:36-115` embeds named theme palettes separately from `web/src/aurora-ui/shared/styles/tokens.css`; `theme-picker.test.tsx:47-100,257-270` adds another literal palette copy and checks picker values against it, rather than the active CSS owner. A CSS palette edit can leave a convincing but wrong preview while tests stay green. Separately `settings-cards.tsx:22-29` and `settings-lanes.tsx:22-29` redeclare the same exported SettingsTableProps/onWrite contract for two presentations. <br><br> **Repair:** give theme preview colors one source: scoped theme CSS variables on preview elements, or a small shared palette source used to generate both CSS and picker data. Choose the approach fitting the vendored Aurora ownership policy; do not create a global styling framework. Put shared settings presentation props in the existing settings model/shared owner and import them in both presentations. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** named-theme picker, theme adapters, CSS theme selectors, theme-picker tests and custom swatch API; settings cards/lanes/dialog. Shared APIs also serve excluded planner code: preserve them without expanding this review into planner. Preserve all named light/dark variants, active selection semantics and write callback/pending/error behavior. <br><br> **Acceptance:** a palette source change changes the applied theme and preview together; compare against the actual owner rather than a second literal test table. Both settings layouts compile against one contract and retain their existing write interaction tests. No additional runtime indirection is needed for the prop-type consolidation.<br><br>
<a id="dash-08"></a>**DASH-08 — Stale guide and source comments describe behavior the tree no longer has**<br><br>**Severity:** MINOR. **Lenses:** invalid/verbose comments and documentation. <br><br> **Concrete corrections:** <br><br> • `web/src/components/help-topics.ts:254`: “Recent activity starts empty after a reload. It lists only what this page has seen.” `web/src/lib/activity.ts:54-70` renders server-recorded history, and activity-panel tests cover history in the first snapshot. Replace with: “Recent activity comes from recorded stage transitions and survives a page reload.” Avoid implying unbounded retention. <br> • `help-topics.ts:255` and `help-legend.tsx:13`: “no heartbeat for five minutes” / “no heartbeat for 5 min” hard-code the default budget and omit the busy-tool distinction already explained by stage activity help. Describe exceeding the stage heartbeat budget with no tool call open; include the current quiet/busy terms consistently. <br> • `web/src/components/terminal/terminal-view.test.tsx:305-311` claims setup's ResizeObserver lacks configurable:true and cannot be replaced, ending “see the accompanying report for the one-line fix”. `web/src/test/setup.ts:11-15` already makes it configurable and writable; the test immediately replaces it. Delete this historical report narrative; retain only why a controllable observer is needed. <br> • `web/src/api/zod.ts:3` names nonexistent `loom/src/commands/status/web/http.rs`; CSP is owned by `loom/src/web_server/http.rs`. `web/src/api/ws.ts:179` names nonexistent `connection-badge.tsx`; the current consumer is `components/header-lines.tsx`, DaemonLine. Update references without changing behavior. <br> • `web/src/api/terminal.ts:154-156` says reconnecting “sends the same oversized frame again”. This transport chunks input and has no replay queue. Retain the chosen refusal behavior for close 1009, but explain the actual server rejection/user-retry policy instead of nonexistent replay. <br> • `web/src/aurora-ui/feedback/BusyRoundel.tsx:40` says “single GLOBAL busy instrument: mount one per app”; existing WorkRoundel/ActivityRoundel consumers include header and per-stage/pending feedback. Document supported local uses or narrow the claim to its intended consumer. Related visual CSS/component prose repeatedly narrates declarations in `terminal-controls.css` and `mode-key.tsx`; shorten only descriptions that repeat immediately visible animation properties. ## Git, terminal, subagents and runtime tests

<a id="rbr-01"></a>**RBR-01 — Wait evidence and forwarding views repeatedly reread the same sources**<br><br>**Severity:** MAJOR. **Lenses:** architecture, duplication, excessive work. <br><br> **Evidence:** `commands/subagents/wait/engine.rs:62-75` asks EvidenceSource for each worker separately on every poll. Both `LifecycleEvidence::claude_outcome` (`wait/evidence.rs:56-59`) and `codex_outcome` (97-104) call `subagent_lifecycle::replay` independently. That function (`subagent_lifecycle/store.rs:46-68`, cross-boundary trace) enumerates all stage journals. Unknown/companion cases then reread the stage journal through `has_exact_record`/`missing_or_idle_claude_evidence`. N workers therefore cause N whole-workspace lifecycle replays per two-second poll, plus secondary reads. A mutable journal can supply different observations for outcome and unknown-settled within the same poll. <br><br> Separately `classify.rs:248` reads an entire transcript, `forward_jobs_status.rs:36-38` calls an overlay which rereads it (`forward_jobs.rs:114`), then reads it again for metadata. Companion receipt state is read for overlay and again for metadata. The classification, overlay and reported metadata can reflect different moments, and ordinary diagnostic work unnecessarily scans growing transcripts repeatedly. `metrics.rs` already accepts parsed rows precisely to avoid a second concurrent read; forwarding does not share that boundary. <br><br> **Smallest repair:** make one poll own a fresh validated lifecycle snapshot and stage records, then derive every worker outcome/settled flag from it. Keep live companion/progress checks explicit, with at most one observation per execution in a poll. For list/harvest, construct one validated transcript/forwarding view per agent and pass it to overlay and metadata construction; share existing parsed rows where the bounded/validated reader contracts permit. Do not introduce a persistent cache or reuse a previous poll's success. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** wait engine, EvidenceSource fakes, lifecycle evidence adapter, direct/companion unknown semantics, classifier, receipt metadata, missing-forwarder views and render tests. Preserve strict identity validation, corrupted-journal refusal, forwarder lifecycle precedence, debounce, torn-tail policy and “fresh correlated success” semantics. The shared transcript reader's stricter validation cannot be silently weakened to reuse unvalidated rows. Coordinate any shared journal API with runtime, which owns that module. <br><br> **Acceptance:** instrument reader calls: one lifecycle replay per multiworker poll; one transcript acquisition per agent view; one companion observation feeds state and metadata. Evidence appended between polls is visible next poll; corrupt/unknown and failure precedence remain unchanged. Test concurrent append between acquisition and derivation without producing inconsistent outcome/settled pairs. Performance claim is repeated work established by source, not measured latency.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-02"></a>**RBR-02 — Parent discovery silently treats a partial directory scan as complete**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** correctness, boundary architecture. <br><br> **Evidence:** `commands/subagents/wait/identity.rs:210-244`, `claude_parent_candidates`, iterates `entries.take(MAX_PARENT_DIRS)` with MAX_PARENT_DIRS=512. It neither detects a 513th entry nor tells `common_parent` the search was incomplete. `common_parent` (165-197) then requires exactly one candidate in that partial result. The cap counts files and non-parent entries as well as valid UUID directories. A large Claude project history can hide the matching parent and fail a valid wait; if a second matching parent lies outside the prefix, an ambiguous request can be accepted. Filesystem enumeration order determines the result. The Codex scan in `codex_binding.rs:87-113` correctly reads cap+1 and rejects overflow, showing a usable local pattern. <br><br> **Repair:** at minimum detect overflow and return a clear incomplete-scan error instead of claiming uniqueness. Prefer deriving candidate parent IDs from the already-loaded exact start index and validating only their directories, if that API can preserve the same scope and ambiguity rules. Keep explicit `--session` as an intentional disambiguation path, not an implicit arbitrary fallback. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** worker-set binding, mixed Claude/Codex common-parent intersection, exact stage/Loom-session start rows and exit-5 diagnostics. Preserve canonical UUID/path checks and refuse genuinely ambiguous ownership. Do not simply raise/remove the cap while leaving directory scans unbounded. <br><br> **Acceptance:** >512 entries with unrelated files cannot silently omit a valid parent or accept a partial uniqueness proof. Cover a second candidate beyond the cap, explicit parent selection, mixed workers and ordinary small history. A test seam with controlled enumeration order is preferable to assuming filesystem order.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-03"></a>**RBR-03 — A failed tracked-file query authorizes scaffold deletion**<br><br>**Severity:** CRITICAL. **Lenses:** data preservation, ownership of destructive policy, invalid rationale. <br><br> **Evidence:** `git/cleanup/worktree.rs:89-100`, `tracked_scaffold_paths`, converts every `git ls-files -z` error into an empty set. `remove_worktree_scaffold` (120-144) then treats known settings, spool and instruction-symlink paths as untracked; `remove_claude_entry` (266-283) removes settings files immediately. `cleanup_worktree` invokes this before asking Git to remove the worktree (22-40). A damaged/unreadable index can therefore make a normal cleanup delete a tracked settings file containing local edits before Git later refuses the removal. The comment at 85-88 explicitly justifies swallowing errors because unit tests use nongit temporary directories. A test convenience has weakened the production ownership check. <br><br> **Repair:** return Result from the tracked-path query and fail before any scaffold mutation when tracked ownership is unknown. Keep a separately established no-Git/half-removed recovery path if required, using positive classification of allowed leftovers; tests for pure scaffold policy can pass an explicit known tracked set or create a repository. Do not infer “no tracked paths” from a command failure. Retain the existing protections for tracked symlinks, settings, nonempty sandbox names and unknown files. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints:** direct `cleanup_worktree`, batch cleanup, MergeLifecycle cleanup and manual verified/destructive removal. Forced cleanup is separately authorized and must remain explicit; this finding concerns the non-forced path. `tests.rs`, `tests_spool.rs`, `tests_tracked_scaffold.rs` and half-removal fixtures currently exercise the helper directly. Preserve genuine half-removal recovery without opening an escape to arbitrary deletion. <br><br> **Acceptance:** corrupt the worktree index or inject a failing tracked-file query while a tracked settings file has uncommitted edits: cleanup must fail with the file bytes, symlink targets and spool intact. Existing valid-repository scaffold cleanup still succeeds. A plain nongit directory is not automatically trusted merely to retain old unit-test setup. <br><br> **Related uncertainty to close during follow-up:** `worktree_failure.rs:107-136` shares a best-effort walker between diagnostics and deletion proof, swallowing read-dir/entry/stat errors. `recover_half_removed` uses an empty result to permit remove_dir_all. Separate a fallible exhaustive ownership walk from best-effort error reporting; do not describe an unreadable subtree as verified scaffold. Whether the same I/O error also prevents deletion is platform/timing dependent, so this is an unsafe proof boundary rather than an asserted guaranteed deletion on every permission failure.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-04"></a>**RBR-04 — Subagent comments describe removed watch behavior and overstate empirical limits**<br><br>**Severity:** MINOR. **Lenses:** invalid/verbose comments. <br><br> **Evidence and precise corrections:** <br><br> • `commands/subagents/classify.rs:17-24` says tool-wait never times out and forbids a “tool-wait for too long” escalation. Classification itself remains tool-wait, but `wait/stall.rs:156-176` does return Stalled for Bash over max(budget,1800) and ordinary tools over budget. Scope the claim to diagnostic classification; describe watch's distinct heuristic and preserve Agent/Task exemption. <br> • `classify.rs:104` advertises `--debounce` on `list`/`harvest`/`watch`. `commands/subagents/mod.rs` exposes debounce only for list/harvest; watch has stall-secs. Correct the supported flags. <br> • `render.rs:41,49` says “every watch poll” / “list/watch gather cycle”. Watch now uses the owned-worker engine and never calls this gather. Say list/harvest invocation. <br> • `wait/stall.rs:9,68` refers to `engine::codex_outcome`; its owner is `evidence.rs`, `LifecycleEvidence::codex_outcome`. <br> • `wait/codex_binding.rs:253-255` says an Unknown receipt “never reaches a terminal lifecycle record”. `wait/direct_tests.rs:96-140` expressly verifies reconcile appends Unknown and an already-bound watch exits immediately. Keep refusal of a new unknown binding; explain that the identity cannot establish a valid new execution, without claiming Unknown is never journaled. <br> • `wait/stall.rs:170` prints “past the {threshold}s ceiling on a real one”. The preceding comment derives a heuristic from observed durations and explicitly notes permission prompts are not bounded by the tool timeout. Replace the claimed real-call ceiling with “past the configured stall threshold”; preserve the separate Stalled outcome rather than suggesting proven death. <br><br> The long historical census and split-for-line-limit narratives in classifier/test headers can be condensed to the measured reason for a conservative debounce and a pointer to evidence. Keep distinctions between exact lifecycle termination, legacy text inference, forwarding ownership and stalled-versus-dead. These are correctness constraints, not removable verbosity. <br><br> **Acceptance:** CLI flags and symbol references resolve; guide/comments distinguish diagnostic classification from wait policy; exact-unknown reconciliation and existing stall tests retain behavior. Comment-only edits require no new ceremonial tests.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-05"></a>**RBR-05 — MAJOR: Hook installation equates matching text with an executable, reachable hook**<br><br>**Evidence and trigger.** `loom/src/git/hooks.rs:42` installs the pre-commit section. `updated_hook_content` at 80–106 returns `None` for matching text; the caller returns at 66 before `make_executable`. Consequently an installed hook whose executable permission is removed remains disabled after another installation. `is_pre_commit_hook_installed` checks markers, so it also reports this disabled installation as present. The reference-transaction installer in `git/hooks/reference_transaction.rs` already treats executable permission as part of idempotency and tests repairing it. <br><br> The same pre-commit update routine appends shell text to any existing hook. For a valid shell hook ending in `exit 0`, the protection is unreachable. For a hook using another interpreter, the appended shell section is invalid in that language. The current tests establish text preservation, not effective chaining. This is an ownership issue at the installer boundary: callers cannot infer that protection runs from a marker being present. <br><br> **Repair.** Make installation success mean the hook will be executable and its Loom entry point reachable. Repair mode even when content is current, using the existing reference-transaction policy where appropriate. Preserve foreign hooks through an explicit, documented chaining mechanism; when safe composition cannot be established, return an actionable conflict instead of claiming successful installation. Do not attempt to parse arbitrary shell or other interpreter programs. Keep user hook exit semantics and arguments intact. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Exercise a current section with mode 0644, a foreign shell hook ending in `exit 0`, a non-shell shebang, and an existing hook that fails. Assert actual invocation and exit behavior, plus unchanged foreign content. Consolidate the small shared permission/idempotency policy without creating a general hook framework.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-06"></a>**RBR-06 — CRITICAL: Merge rollback can overwrite a newly created file or follow its symlink**<br><br>**Evidence and flow.** `loom/src/git/merge/checkout_files.rs:114` restores removed identical untracked files with unconditional `std::fs::write`. It neither reserves a missing destination nor refuses an existing file or symlink. Callers include the partial removal rollback at 102–113, the stash failure path in `checkout_apply.rs:128`, and clean fast-forward refusal in `fast_forward.rs:59`. After Loom removes an identical untracked file, an external writer can recreate it before a Git failure triggers restoration; rollback then truncates those new bytes. If the recreated entry is a symlink, the write follows it. The `after_errored` branch at `fast_forward.rs:76` filters destinations using `symlink_metadata`, but other callers do not; its precheck also cannot make the later write exclusive. <br><br> **Scope and constraints.** Concurrent external writers are an accepted broader merge window, and this finding does not promise atomicity across arbitrary editor activity. The narrower defect is that the restoration primitive itself destroys an occupied destination, despite rollback being intended to preserve operator work. Existing error-path tests protecting a recreated regular file do not cover the unconditional callers. Restoration also silently skips failed blob reads and writes, so callers cannot distinguish complete recovery from missing files. <br><br> **Repair.** At the shared restoration owner, create each destination exclusively and refuse symlink traversal; use an anchored parent-directory strategy where parent replacement matters. Return a structured restoration result, preserving evidence/backups and surfacing occupied destinations or failed recovery rather than silently succeeding. Do not solve this with another `exists` check. Route every rollback caller through the same policy and carry recovery information into its merge result. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Directly test restoration with a different regular file and with a symlink already occupying the destination, then exercise each caller's failure route. Existing content and the symlink target must remain unchanged; the result must identify incomplete restoration. A missing destination should still recover exact original bytes. Inject a blob-read/write failure and require an actionable recovery result.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-07"></a>**RBR-07 — CRITICAL: Hook control-path matching uses configuration spelling rather than resolved location**<br><br>**Evidence and trigger.** `loom/src/git/merge/control_paths.rs:228` converts `core.hooksPath` to a string prefix without resolving lexical path components. `is_control_path` at 196–212 strips only one leading `./` before comparing this prefix with Git's repository-relative paths. A configured `x/../.githooks` therefore fails to match a changed `.githooks/pre-commit`, even though Git resolves both to the same location. `core.hooksPath=.` reduces to an empty matching prefix after the later stripping step and likewise fails to protect hooks in the repository root. Absolute paths under the repository containing `..` have the same mismatch. <br><br> **Consumers and consequence.** Both stage control-path admission and target guard evaluation consume this shared classifier. A change to executable hooks can pass the control-path gate because its path is spelled differently in configuration. This is separate from intentional outside-repository hook paths, which a tracked repository diff cannot modify directly. <br><br> **Repair.** Resolve the configured directory into a normalized repository-relative location with an explicit root-directory case. Handle existing symlink ancestors according to the gate's ownership policy, while supporting a directory that does not exist yet. Keep outside-repository paths distinct from query failures. Share this canonical classifier across stage and target checks; do not patch individual string comparisons in consumers. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Use actual repository configuration for `.`, `././.githooks`, `x/../.githooks`, and absolute equivalent paths. Changes in the effective hook directory must be classified consistently and held at the stage/target boundaries. Preserve existing behavior for ordinary `.githooks`, paths outside the repository, and unrelated files. Include a missing-yet-to-be-created directory and a symlink ancestor to establish the intended policy.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-08"></a>**RBR-08 — MAJOR: Resolver cleanliness accepts failed Git inspection as empty output**<br><br>**Evidence.** `loom/src/git/merge/resolved.rs:77` checks branch and index state. It discards the status returned by `query` for `ls-files -u` and `status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=no`; only stdout is inspected. `query` at 146 returns nonzero command status as a boolean, not an error. A corrupt or unreadable index can make both commands fail without stdout, while symbolic-ref and merge-base still succeed because they do not need that index. The resolver readiness check can therefore return success without establishing either an unmerged-free index or a clean tracked worktree. <br><br> **Repair and constraints.** Require successful status for every inspection whose empty output proves cleanliness, retaining stderr in the error. Distinguish expected absence from execution failure for `MERGE_HEAD` and detached HEAD checks. Reuse the pinned runner; its ownership protection must remain intact. A later merge may still fail, so the demonstrated defect is false resolver readiness, not proof of a bad merge landing. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Corrupt an otherwise valid stage worktree index after recording a surviving completed commit. Readiness must refuse with the failed command's reason. Keep existing clean, untracked-only, conflict, wrong-branch, rewritten-history and target-moved cases. This also removes the ambiguous `(bool, String)` convention from mandatory successful queries.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-09"></a>**RBR-09 — CRITICAL: Worktree recovery deletes an unregistered directory without establishing that it is disposable**<br><br>**Evidence and consumer.** `git/worktree/operations.rs:get_or_create_worktree` checks whether `.worktrees/<stage>` has a `.git` entry and appears in Git's listing. A missing entry or a successful listing that reports the directory unregistered reaches prune followed by `remove_dir_all` on the whole directory before recreation; prune errors are ignored. The listing helper's own error propagates through `?` and does not authorize deletion. An invalid pointer that still exists alongside valid registration is reused, so invalid contents alone are not this deletion trigger. `orchestrator/core/spawn_setup.rs:91` invokes this during ordinary stage startup, without a force or discard-work decision. Missing pointer/registration can therefore turn existing uncommitted work into disposable data. Registration proves a usable worktree, not ownership of every file in an unusable one. <br><br> **Repair.** Fail with a recoverable infrastructure error when an existing directory cannot be identified safely; preserve its bytes and report the failed proof. Reuse the cleanup module's explicit scaffold/owned-remnant policy for a positively identified empty remnant, rather than duplicating a broader removal route here. Keep a deliberate operator force path separate. A registration failure should not become implicit consent to discard content. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Create an existing stage directory containing a unique untracked file, with its `.git` absent or with a successful Git listing confirming no registration. Normal startup must preserve it and report recovery instructions. Also preserve the existing listing-error propagation, and cover a genuinely disposable empty remnant and prune failure. The spawn-failure call to `remove_worktree(..., true)` is separately authorized forced cleanup; do not generalize this finding to all cleanup calls.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-10"></a>**RBR-10 — MAJOR: Missing dependency branch fallback drops the configured target**<br><br>**Evidence.** `git/worktree/base.rs:resolve_base_branch` uses `init_base_branch` for no dependencies and for all merged dependencies. Its single completed/unmerged dependency fallback, after finding the dependency branch absent, calls `resolve_target_branch(&None, repo_root)`. A run configured for a feature branch can consequently select the repository default branch. The existing missing-branch test supplies `None`, so it cannot expose this asymmetry. `orchestrator/core/spawn_setup.rs:35` supplies `self.config.base_branch`; `resolve_worktree` subsequently uses the returned branch for both accepted-tip lookup and worktree creation. <br><br> **Repair and constraints.** Resolve the configured target once and consistently reuse it for every `ResolvedBase::Main` route. Separately make the missing-branch assumption explicit: absence alone does not prove dependency work merged. If this fallback remains supported, it must not silently choose another target. The normal scheduler may avoid this state; the defect is in the documented recovery/fallback contract and becomes reachable when dependency records and Git state diverge. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Use divergent default and configured target branches with a completed dependency whose stage branch is absent. Assert the result and created worktree use the configured target, or that the new explicit missing-work policy refuses safely. Retain tests for the existing dependency branch and transient incomplete dependency.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-11"></a>**RBR-11 — CRITICAL: Required session confinement flags are treated as optional compatibility features**<br><br>**Evidence and flow.** `native/capsule.rs:probed_capsule_support` returns all-false support when `claude --help` fails. `capsule_from` then discards an already generated settings path when `--settings` is unsupported and omits source restriction independently when `--setting-sources` is unsupported. `native/launch.rs:prepare_session_launch_with` always writes the per-session settings, calls this fallible-by-omission assembly, and proceeds to create the launch wrapper. `native/mod.rs:build_claude_command` simply omits absent flags. Both native and tmux launches share this path. <br><br> The capsule is now the owner of session sandbox settings, guards, relay hooks, tool restrictions and location-specific denies. It lives under the state directory, outside ambient project settings discovery. Omitting `--settings` therefore removes the explicit attachment of that confinement policy, while the command still requests the resolved permission mode. Omitting source restriction reintroduces the local-settings inheritance the capsule's own documentation says it closes. The actual remaining restrictions depend on ambient user/project policy; this finding does not assert every such launch is entirely unsandboxed. <br><br> **Tests and architecture.** `tests_capsule.rs` explicitly requires empty legacy argv for unsupported/unprobed capsules and tests that no supported flags yields `SessionCapsule::default()`. These are now insufficient safety contracts. The confinement integration fixtures hard-code support for every flag, read the generated file directly, and feed it to srt; they prove policy contents when applied, but cannot catch a launch that fails to apply the file. <br><br> **Repair.** Separate mandatory launch capabilities from optional prompt-cache optimization. Return an actionable spawn refusal if required settings/source/MCP isolation cannot be established, preserving probe failure diagnostics. Bound the help subprocess with the existing process owner and deadline; its current bare `.output()` can also hang launch indefinitely, and its failed result is cached for the process lifetime. Keep unsupported optional prompt-cache splitting as a graceful fallback. Validate the final invocation contract before spawning either backend. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Drive launch preparation with failed, partial and complete capability probes. No session wrapper should be launched without the required capsule attachment and isolation flags. A fake executable that hangs or fails on help must produce a bounded actionable refusal, while a supported executable receives the exact generated capsule path. Keep the existing sandbox content probes, and add an invocation-boundary assertion rather than replacing them.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-12"></a>**RBR-12 — MINOR: Terminal and worktree comments preserve obsolete ownership and mechanical history**<br><br>**Examples.** `native/launch/host.rs` says only its entry points read process environment, but downstream wrapper/build-cache and `session_settings/host_sockets.rs` also read it. `native/capsule.rs` and several test modules explain that they were split to satisfy a line limit; that history does not explain behavior. `terminal/mod.rs` says three session types while the dispatch handles six. `native/mod.rs` describes four public spawn methods after consolidation into two, calls adjudication main-repository-only despite worktree-capable dispatch, and retains removed fallback history. `pid_tracking.rs` has an unfinished merge-session doc comment attached to `test_read_pid_entry_pid_only`. `spawner.rs` keeps “Increased from” timing notes without a present constraint. <br><br> `git/worktree/settings.rs:create_worktree_settings` contains a long history of removed broad state grants, repeated current grant descriptions, a dangling “finding #5 (option 2)” reference, and claims token denies are written into settings.local.json despite session capsules owning them. `tests_settings.rs` repeats the old defaultMode writer. These comments obscure the current boundary that matters: inherited project settings, capsule-owned session policy, and exact narrow state grants. <br><br> **Repair and acceptance.** Replace mechanical history with concise present contracts, keep the substantive permissions and quoting rationale, and link exact current owners. Extract the small named settings transformations where needed to bring long functions under the repository's limit; do not split merely to hide line counts. Consolidate duplicate test fixture builders for base settings and capsule inputs where their semantics really match, preserving separate positive controls and emitted-policy assertions. Readers should be able to identify the policy owner and launch prerequisites without following removed file writers or historical finding numbers.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-13"></a>**RBR-13 — CRITICAL: Native window ownership is stage-scoped rather than session-scoped**<br><br>**Evidence and trigger.** `native/launch.rs:338–339` derives the window title from `session.tracking_key`, then adds `session.id` only to the PID-file key. `native/mod.rs:233–240` repeats that derivation; `kill_session` at lines 336–343 attempts title-based closure before the guarded process-identity path. `native/tests.rs:192–196` explicitly preserves equal titles for successive sessions. Two live sessions in repositories using the same stage ID also produce equal titles; terminating one can close the other repository's window. Exact title matching fixes prefix collisions but cannot distinguish equal titles. The macOS helpers close every window matching the name; Linux wmctrl/xdotool resolve a matching window without session attribution. <br><br> **Caller qualification.** The ordinary single-session command (`commands/sessions.rs:133`) first checks liveness; a definitively dead PID is rejected by `native/mod.rs:371` before title fallback. Thus a known-dead tombstone through that command is not the trigger. The live cross-repository collision is sufficient. Any delayed-teardown/replacement scenario must establish a caller that actually reaches `kill_session` without that precheck; do not infer it merely from an old persisted record. This narrows the earlier report's overly broad dead-PID example. <br><br> Confirmed direct consumers are `core/event_handler/stage_takedown.rs:155–168`, which kills captured sessions before checking whether they remain alive, and `core/merge_handler/resolver_stop.rs:111–119`, which assigns the stage tracking key and kills during failed-spawn settlement before proving death. These show why the backend itself needs attribution even when some command callers precheck liveness. <br><br> **Repair and owner.** Give native launch a persistent per-session window identity, including project ownership where necessary, and use that identity consistently for existence and teardown. Prefer a stable window handle when the emulator supports one; otherwise use a session-unique title and preserve the friendly stage label separately. Legacy records lacking attributable window identity should use verified PID teardown or conservatively report uncertainty. Do not change tmux naming mechanically: its session-unique socket already supplies the missing namespace, and `viewer::tmux_session_name` shares the current helper. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints.** Native launch, all emulator argv/AppleScript builders, liveness checks, teardown, PID fallback, old persisted sessions, tmux viewer naming, and native tests. CON-A-04 owns iTerm's missing title/app-name mismatch; its repair should consume this ownership contract rather than add another stage-only title. <br><br> **Acceptance.** With old/new sessions for one stage and identical stage IDs in two repositories, terminating any one closes only its own window. A stale/dead PID must not permit closure of another session's window. Exercise Linux and macOS command fixtures and preserve legacy conservative behavior. <br><br> <a id="con-a-04"></a>**Reconciled evidence from CON-A-04: CON-A-04 → RBR-13 — duplicate evidence: iTerm spawn and teardown do not share window identity.** <br><br> Lenses: architecture, DRY, correctness. Spawn creates an iTerm window without setting its supplied title; teardown/liveness query that title. Spawn names application `iTerm`, while teardown names `iTerm2`. The command-generation test never asserts the title. Evidence: `loom/src/orchestrator/terminal/emulator.rs:228-237,417-434`; `native/window_ops/macos.rs:45-62,190-199`. Canonical finding: RBR-13. Use one application identity and one session-owned window identity across spawn, lookup and close; simply using the stage id can close the wrong generation. Preserve AppleScript/shell escaping. Future validation: generated identity tests and live macOS spawn/liveness/complete for iTerm and Terminal.app. Only literal mismatch is proved statically; alias resolution and Terminal.app title drift remain unmeasured.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-14"></a>**RBR-14 — MAJOR: xdotool command failure suppresses verified PID teardown**<br><br>**Evidence.** `native/window_ops.rs:close_window_by_title` checks the search exit status, but treats `bounded_window_command(windowclose).is_some()` as success. `Some(Output)` also represents a nonzero exit. `NativeBackend::kill_session` then returns `Ok(())` immediately, skipping PID termination. An xdotool search that finds an ID followed by a failed close consequently reports successful teardown with the process still alive. The wmctrl and AppleScript paths correctly inspect command success. <br><br> **Repair.** Interpret window-close output status explicitly, continue suitable candidates/fallbacks on failure, and retain PID identity until termination is established. Keep the existing bounded process runner. Separate command acceptance from observed process death where the higher-level handoff needs the stronger guarantee. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Script search success followed by windowclose exit 1, timeout and success; assert that only success claims the window operation and failures reach guarded PID teardown. Existing Linux tests cover parsing/escaping, not this exit-status branch.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-15"></a>**RBR-15 — MINOR: Terminal documentation repeatedly describes removed fallback behavior and implementation history**<br><br>**Invalid claims.** `tmux/backend.rs` and `tmux/tests_spawn.rs` explain cleanup in terms of automatic native retry after tmux failure; `terminal/backend.rs:143–161` explicitly returns the configured tmux lane's failure without retry. The dispatcher comment additionally promises nothing is written on spawn failure, although preparation writes wrappers/settings before a later tmux error. `native/wrapper/tests.rs` says merge sessions run in the main repository, while `spawn_merge_session_in_worktree` and the wrapper's own `kind_env` documentation correctly place them in the stage worktree. `tmux/history.rs:bound_history` promises a whole-line boundary but retains a partial tail when an over-limit capture contains no newline. Its save-history comment promises capture-failure logging, whereas an absent/failed capture returns silently. <br><br> **Excess and duplication.** `native/wrapper.rs` repeats a ten-argument positional API through creation/rendering layers and argues that tests must remain untouched as justification. A small launch-input value can express the existing contract without changing rendered bytes. `tmux/reconcile/steps.rs` spends much of its body restating rules already expressed in focused tests; retain the non-obvious survivor-floor and add-before-kill constraints, trim repeated rhetoric and historical verification narratives. `tmux/backend.rs`, several test module headers and `wrapper/script_text.rs` explain file splitting by line ceilings rather than ownership. `wrapper/tests.rs` duplicates an executable-file ETXTBSY retry loop while `tests_exec_env.rs` demonstrates direct `bash <path>` execution for environment behavior; keep an executable invocation only where testing execution permissions is the point. `tmux/tests_discovery.rs` duplicates session fixture construction for alternate filenames; one fixture writer with an explicit filename can serve both. <br><br> **Repair and acceptance.** Rewrite comments against the actual configured-lane dispatch, preserve cleanup rationale without a nonexistent retry, state bounded-tail behavior accurately or enforce the whole-line contract, and make logging promises match implementation. Consolidate fixture setup and wrapper inputs without weakening behavior assertions. Review final comments against their functions; existing wrapper byte assertions and behavioral tests should remain semantically equivalent after a future implementation change. These are maintainability findings, not claims that comment edits repair runtime teardown.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-16"></a>**RBR-16 — CRITICAL: A numbered handoff can block the daemon before its byte limit applies**<br><br>**Evidence.** `handoff/generator/lookup.rs:numbered_handoff_paths` accepts any directory entry matching the filename grammar. `read_handoff_artifact` uses `File::open(path)` and then `take(MAX_HANDOFF_BYTES + 1)`. Opening a FIFO with no writer blocks before the 1 MiB cap can help; a symlink is followed, and an endless or slow device is not a regular bounded artifact. The lookup runs in completion monitoring, recovery and handoff merge, including under the handoff directory lock. `generator/mod.rs:handoff_has_context` separately performs an unbounded `read_to_string` after lookup, reintroducing an unrestricted read/race for red-band reuse. <br><br> **Repair and constraints.** Use the existing `fs::safe_read::read_bounded` owner, anchored under the canonical state root, for all handoff reads. It opens nonblocking and refuses symlink/special-file traversal; remove the separate unbounded reread or carry the validated parsed document into reuse. Preserve the distinction between absent artifacts, oversized/invalid documents and I/O uncertainty. A regular-file size cap alone or metadata check followed by ordinary open leaves the blocking/replacement race. <br><br> **Consumers.** `monitor/completion_blockers.rs`, `core/loop_recovery/park.rs`, `core/event_handler/recover_hung.rs`, status completion view, generator merge and continuation selection. Current confinement denies sessions direct handoff writes, so this is a malformed/persisted-state robustness failure, not a claim that a confined agent can freely plant files there. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Named FIFO with no writer, symlink to an outside file, directory, oversized regular file, disappearing file and valid handoff must all finish within a bounded test deadline; rejected entries cannot block the single daemon poll thread or hold the directory lock indefinitely. Retain explicit error behavior where current callers park on uncertainty.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-17"></a>**RBR-17 — MAJOR: Trusted checkpoint loading merges unauthenticated state before verification**<br><br>**Evidence.** `generator/lookup.rs:load_trusted_session_checkpoint` first calls `load_session_checkpoint`, which folds every syntactically valid checkpoint, and only then calls `trusted`. `completion/checkpoint.rs:merge_latest` discards the older latest evidence based on the incoming timestamp; `merge_observation` similarly overwrites phase/attestation and can set conflicts. `completion/trusted.rs` cannot recover discarded signed evidence and copies the unverified `conflict` bit. Thus an unsigned newer checkpoint beside an authentic one can erase the trusted latest/blocker, or an unsigned conflict flag can make authentic evidence non-actionable. No forged MAC is required to suppress recovery. Existing tests cover a forged checkpoint alone and tampering within one checkpoint, not authentic and forged artifacts folded together. <br><br> **Repair and owner.** Authenticate each artifact's components before any lossy trusted merge. Recompute conflict/capacity from authenticated observations and receipts, and define timestamp ordering using signed evidence rather than unsigned observation bounds. Retain an explicitly untrusted loader for diagnostics. Separate trusted aggregate types or APIs would make it harder for a new caller to accidentally fold then filter. Do not promote unsigned state by re-signing its aggregate. <br><br> **Constraints and acceptance.** This is evidence suppression, not acceptance of forged completion. Normal session confinement restricts direct file writes; persisted corruption, legacy unsigned artifacts or operator-created files can still exercise the contract. In both filename orders, signed actionable evidence and accepted receipts survive unrelated unsigned newer evidence, unsigned conflict flags and unsigned nonce-capacity fillers. Genuine signed identity conflicts must remain fail-closed. Test the public trusted loader, not only `CompletionCheckpoint::trusted` in isolation.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-18"></a>**RBR-18 — MAJOR: The handoff parser treats three hyphens inside YAML as its closing delimiter**<br><br>**Evidence and consequence.** `handoff/schema/parsing.rs:extract_yaml_from_handoff` strips the initial `---`, then uses `after_first.find("---")` without requiring a delimiter line. Valid serialized values such as a completed task `compare before---after` or a command containing three hyphens truncate the YAML. Depending on position, parsing either silently downgrades the generated V2 document to prose or accepts only an earlier valid prefix, losing structured fields. Checkpoint lookup ignores V1 fallback entirely, so a document the production formatter just wrote can lose durable completion evidence on reload. Existing round-trip tests use only punctuation-free values. <br><br> **Repair.** Reuse the repository's line-aware frontmatter boundary parser where its contract fits, or centralize a small exact delimiter-line reader. Preserve pure-YAML and legacy prose support deliberately; content within quoted scalars and indented block scalars must not end frontmatter. Distinguish malformed declared V2 data from ordinary legacy prose if callers need to report loss of machine-readable evidence. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Round-trip actual formatter output with triple hyphens in stage descriptions, completed tasks, decisions, commit messages and checkpoint commands; preserve every V2 field and attestation. Include an indented scalar line containing `---`, a genuine delimiter line, CRLF and missing closing delimiter.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-19"></a>**RBR-19 — MAJOR: Semantic handoff upsert drops fields outside its reduced projection**<br><br>**Evidence.** `generator/merge.rs:fold_handoffs` reads prior `HandoffV2` values but reconstructs `HandoffContent`, which has no fields for discovered facts, open questions or files read. `fold_collections` also reduces each completed task to description, dropping its associated files. `semantic_eq` ignores branch and every body-only value (goals, plan, test status, memory). `commands/handoff/create.rs:build_handoff_content` supplies a fresh memory journal and branch, then invokes this upsert: when only memory changes, the call returns the old path as `Unchanged`. When another field forces creation, prior structured fields absent from HandoffContent disappear from the newly preferred document. Existing tests protect completed descriptions and checkpoint counts, but do not cover the schema's full semantic payload. <br><br> **Repair and architecture.** Choose one canonical handoff document model for parse, merge, equality and render.
