Preserve full typed task/file records and all supported navigation/context fields; carry body-only memory explicitly or persist it structurally before comparing. Define which observations are snapshots (current uncommitted files/branch), which are accumulating facts, and which should intentionally be excluded from idempotence. Keep incidental render timestamps out of equality. Avoid a parallel list of fields in a second lossy DTO and another handwritten equality predicate. <br><br> **Acceptance.** A memory-only or branch-only update must be represented; prior discovered facts, open questions, files read and task-associated files survive a subsequent completion-checkpoint merge. Identical semantic inputs remain idempotent. A newly clean worktree must not resurrect an older uncommitted-file snapshot merely because the newest list is empty.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-20"></a>**RBR-20 — MINOR: Numbered artifact enumeration is duplicated and has an unchecked terminal value**<br><br>`generator/numbering.rs` repeats filename parsing/directory walking in next-number and latest lookup; `generator/lookup.rs` implements it a third time with different absent/error handling and ordering. `max_number + 1` overflows for a filename ending in `4294967295.md` (panic with checked overflow, wrap otherwise), rather than returning a controlled allocation refusal. Consolidate a single fallible enumeration of valid numbered artifacts and use checked allocation. Preserve semantic lookup's stage/session validation and legacy latest-file fallback separately. Add maximum-number and malformed-number fixtures; a high filename must never overwrite a lower numbered artifact or panic the host. <br><br> The handoff formatter also has repetitive comments narrating each immediately following section, while `HandoffOrigin::AgentCeiling` says sessions may write the handoff directory and `session_content.rs` correctly describes relay-only writes. Replace that stale ownership claim and retain comments about the actual trust boundary. `git_handoff` renders commit messages directly in table cells while the main formatter escapes pipes in decisions; share a small table-cell renderer if these formats remain, covering pipes and embedded newlines consistently.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-21"></a>**RBR-21 — MAJOR: Recovery tests discard their expected route or never execute the advertised recovery**<br><br>**Lenses: correctness of verification, invalid comments.** `loom/tests/integration/merge_conflict_recovery.rs:132,166,199,318` calls `matches!(route, expected_variant)` as a standalone statement. The resulting boolean is discarded. The tests still fail on an unexpected `Err`, but any successful wrong route passes these purported refusal, verified-ancestry and daemon-ownership assertions. The neighboring tests correctly put `matches!` inside `assert!`, so this is a localized omission rather than a special assertion convention. <br><br> `loom/tests/phantom_merge.rs:190–248` has two `sync_path_reverts_merged_true_*` tests that do not invoke the daemon sync owner or assert the reverted persisted state. The first inspects ancestry and calls the separate MERGE_HEAD reconciliation helper, then asserts the pre-call `stage.merged` is still true. The second only checks that branch lookup fails. Their comments acknowledge the missing operation but still describe the tests as sync regression coverage. `integration/dependency_conflict.rs:93–181` similarly names a resolver retry, accepts any first resolver error, manually constructs a merge branch, then directly creates a worktree without retrying `resolve_base_branch`. Its initial failure is the unmerged-dependency scheduling refusal, not proof that the resolver encountered a merge conflict. <br><br> **Smallest repair.** Wrap the four route checks in explicit assertions with the actual route in failure diagnostics. For phantom recovery, call the production sync transition through the existing daemon recovery harness and reload the stage; if equivalent coverage already exists there, remove or rename these prerequisite-only tests and link the actual regression case. For dependency conflict, either test a supported resolution-and-retry entry point or accurately name the narrower manually prepared base/worktree contract. Do not reproduce the production state mutation inside the test as the supposed operation under test. <br><br> **Consumers and constraints.** These tests protect complete/merge conflict routing, daemon sync, and dependency-base recovery. Keep the real repository ancestry setup and the distinction between final merged stages and unmerged completed stages. The module introduction at `merge_conflict_recovery.rs:14` says reconciliation reverts `Completed + merged=true`, while its actual `daemon_reconcile_moves_only_an_unmerged_stage_out_of_completed` coverage preserves finalized merged stages; update that claim to the current rule. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Changing each route to a different successful variant must fail its test. Disabling the production phantom-merge reset must fail a test that reloads the persisted stage and verifies both preserved commit evidence and `merged=false`. A wrong error category on initial dependency resolution cannot satisfy a test claiming a conflict. This finding concerns missing proof, not a claim that all corresponding production routes are currently incorrect.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-22"></a>**RBR-22 — MAJOR: Test process setup has multiple owners with observably different failure and isolation rules**<br><br>**Lenses: architecture, duplication, nonidiomatic error handling.** `integration/helpers.rs:13–86,121–143`, `e2e/helpers/git.rs:11–47`, and the near-copy `e2e/uncommitted_changes.rs:17–56` launch Git and only check `Command::output`'s I/O result. A nonzero `init`, `commit`, `checkout`, merge or branch deletion is accepted as successful fixture preparation. This hides the first failure and lets later assertions operate on a different repository history. `e2e/helpers/tests.rs:11–19` checks only that `.git` and README exist, so even its repository-helper test does not verify the promised initial commit. Newer fixtures such as `merge_off_checkout.rs:17–43`, `map_cli.rs:22–38` and `target_ref_hook_support/mod.rs:17–66` already show checked status and isolated global/system configuration. <br><br> The hook suites separately implement installation, permissions, JSON stdin, process environment and output capture. `hooks_commit_filter.rs:48–78` clears only two session variables and waits with piped output undrained; `hooks_git_add_guard.rs:48–69` also waits without draining and does not clear the relay environment. `hooks_no_preexisting_failures.rs:42–57` drains output but inherits that environment. `hooks_poll_guard.rs:175–197` uses the shared scrub and `wait_with_output`. The undrained pipes can stall if a regression emits enough output; this is not evidence that present small-output cases deadlock. <br><br> `integration/binary_spawn_guard.rs:23–29` recognizes only two textual spawn spellings. The already-present `confinement_status.rs:148` directly uses `Command::new(loom_bin_path())` outside the sanctioned list. That caller does scrub at line 150, so this is a false enforcement claim, not an observed identity leak in that test. The guard and helper comments promise every `LOOM_*` variable is removed, but `helpers.rs:200–212` is a hand-maintained list that omits the wrapper's `LOOM_BOOT_ID` (`native/wrapper/host_env.rs:55`). Other fixture families instead enumerate the prefix. The divergence makes future additions easy to miss. <br><br> **Repair and migration.** Introduce a small shared test-support module for checked Git execution and a child-command environment policy; migrate the duplicate Git initializers first. Make normal fixture mutations fail immediately with arguments, cwd, status, stdout and stderr. Provide a separate explicit unchecked result for tests intentionally producing Git conflicts. Isolate global/system config and inherited repository selectors for fixture commands; tests of config inheritance or hooks must explicitly opt back into the specific behavior under test. This complements CON-B-04's production Git-runner finding rather than replacing it. <br><br> Give hook harnesses one reusable process runner that closes stdin, drains both outputs, enforces an outer deadline, and kills/reaps owned children on failure. Keep scenario payloads, hook dependencies and expected behavior visible in each suite. Use prefix-based relay-environment scrubbing followed by explicit scenario additions, or document a narrower list with a real parity check. Keep HOME/update-check isolation and avoid process-global environment mutation in concurrently running tests. Reduce the spawn guard's claim to its actual syntactic coverage or test/enforce the common command factory rather than pretending a substring search resolves aliases. <br><br> **Acceptance.** A fake Git exiting nonzero on fixture commit/merge fails at setup with diagnostics; successful fixture setup proves HEAD exists. Hook tests behave identically under an inherited Loom session and on a clean shell, except explicit session scenarios. A child emitting more than pipe capacity and a child that never exits both produce bounded, diagnosed test failures without orphan processes. Preserve the real hook and CLI invocations in worker-evidence/Codex suites, including their independent wire fixtures and identity assertions.<br><br>
<a id="rbr-23"></a>**RBR-23 — MINOR: Legacy end-to-end suites duplicate unit behavior and describe stronger coverage than they exercise**<br><br>**Lenses: excessive code, duplication, invalid/verbose comments.** `e2e/daemon_config/intervals.rs:7–43`, `parallel_sessions.rs:35–47`, and `daemon_config/tests.rs:113–146` assign a struct field and immediately assert that field equals the assigned value. They exercise no parser, scheduler, clock or command consumer. `sessions/status.rs:54–69` checks enum variants equal themselves; `sessions/creation.rs:44–56` checks selected fields of derived clone. These are lower-value than the actual transition/persistence tests beside them. <br><br> `e2e/merge.rs:117–152` already checks all 18 combinations of the two optional merge flags and the daemon boolean, while earlier tests and `daemon_config/tests.rs:155–195` repeat subsets. The latter meaningful precedence checks are bundled into an ignored test with unrelated orchestrator construction. `stage_transitions.rs`, `e2e/sequential.rs`, `e2e/parallel.rs`, `completion_flow.rs` and `failure_resume.rs` repeat large stage-construction sequences; several call Completed→Completed after `complete_stage` has already completed and marked the stage, naming that no-op a separate verification transition. <br><br> **Misleading contracts to correct.** `e2e/manual_mode.rs:133–164` tests that a waiting stage has no signal by never asking the production scheduler to process it; `test_manual_mode_creates_signal_without_session_spawn` only invokes the signal formatter. These are formatter/listing tests, not manual-mode orchestration proofs. `e2e/handoff/session_integration.rs:28–49` manually changes both statuses, so its name does not establish that context exhaustion automatically triggers a stage transition. `adjudication_e2e.rs:295–331` is named `accept_verdict_amends_plan_and_clears_feedback` but deliberately verifies failed amendment persistence; rename it to that failure contract. `e2e/uncommitted_changes.rs:191–194` calls a newly created untracked file an unstaged modification; the mixed case does not modify an existing tracked file. <br><br> Other concrete stale comments: `daemon_config/tests.rs:61–62` says defaults are exhaustively spelled out with no default spread while line 69 uses one; `stale_project_execution.rs:41` says construction eagerly builds NativeBackend, although `terminal/backend.rs:61–81,113` defers it in a OnceLock. `adjudication_e2e/{amendment,sessions}.rs` justify splits by claiming the parent is under the line limit, but the parent is 525 lines. `e2e/merge.rs:118` calls 18 optional-flag combinations eight; sequential/parallel/failure tests repeatedly call current Queued/WaitingForDeps/Completed states Ready/Pending/Verified. Repeated narrative comments describing each immediately following assignment/assertion obscure the few real setup constraints. <br><br> **Repair and consumers.** Delete field-assignment/self-equality checks, keep default and serialization contracts, and consolidate precedence into the existing complete table. Extract a focused fixture builder for dependency metadata scenarios without hiding transition steps or assertions. Name metadata-only tests accordingly; retain separate real-Git ancestry tests and actual orchestrator/manual-mode integration tests. Enable pure precedence checks independently of terminal-dependent tests. Replace redundant narration with short reasons for knowledge-stage bypass, identity isolation, Git configuration and process cleanup. Trim the repeated tmux platform-history essays into a concise constraint plus knowledge link, preserving the bind-probe versus writability distinction and the real PID/server-death assertions. <br><br> **Acceptance.** Each retained test names an operation it actually invokes and can fail for a production behavior change. The consolidated suite still covers all 18 merge-precedence combinations, dependency blocking/fan-in, persisted metadata preservation, forbidden transitions, real Git retention and actual tmux lifecycle. A scheduler that produces signals for waiting stages must be caught by a scheduler test, not credited to a formatter test that never passes that stage. Consolidation must not broaden skips or replace strong behavioral assertions with source-text checks.<br><br>

## Additional runtime concerns

<a id="con-a-01"></a>**CON-A-01 — MAJOR: heartbeat-only retirement kills healthy foreground work**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, correctness. A stage whose first call is a long Codex forward has no post-tool progress; `never_worked` parks it at one stage budget. A later foreground call is retired at the longer stall threshold even while its transcript has an open tool. Evidence: `loom/src/orchestrator/monitor/heartbeat.rs:310-334`, `never_worked.rs:29-80`, `core/event_handler/recover_hung.rs:123-172,262-299`; heartbeat writers `loom-hooks/post-tool-use.sh:242` and `subagent-stop.sh:210`; no heartbeat producer in `_codex-direct.py`. Smallest fix: make current-attempt worker evidence part of the shared stall decision, including bounded observed transcript/progress and actual process identity. Preserve timeout overrides and genuine dead-worker escalation; an open tool alone must not grant infinite life. Consumers: monitor, status activity badge, forwarder lifecycle. Future validation: first-call and after-progress long work versus dead/no-growth worker at identical clock points. Static proof only.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-02"></a>**CON-A-02 — MAJOR: dispute publication is not transactional with stage admission**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, correctness. A concurrent stage transition can occur after the filing snapshot is accepted but before its stage update. `request.md` already exists when `try_request_adjudication` rejects, leaving an open request for a filing reported failed. Evidence: `loom/src/stage_service/dispute.rs:80-106`, `dispute_kinds.rs:192-219`. The dispute lock serializes filings but not independent stage writers. Smallest fix: one service-owned operation validates and publishes under the required stage snapshot, or rolls back only its own newly allocated request on refusal. Preserve dispute-before-stage lock order, idempotency receipts and budget accounting. Consumers: daemon and operator filing adapters. Future validation: barrier-controlled status mutation between snapshot and update, asserting neither orphan request nor spent budget. No concurrency test run.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-03"></a>**CON-A-03 — MAJOR: completion marker hides finalization and scheduler failures**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, correctness, invalid comments. `finish_orchestration` warns on failed plan archival/DONE transition, then publishes completion anyway. The supervisor also publishes that marker on initialization error or panic. The summary silently omits unreadable stages and ignores in-flight buckets while claiming they cannot occur. Evidence: `loom/src/daemon/server/orchestrator.rs:53-77,169-180,231-238`; `status.rs:57-108`; `broadcast.rs:194-196,235-236`. Scheduler failure has a separate file, but this completion projection does not incorporate it. Smallest fix: persist a typed terminal outcome containing scheduler/finalization failure, and derive protocol status from it; make finalization retryable without duplicate destructive work. Do not count unreadable/in-flight records as successful completion. Consumers: CLI/TUI/web subscribers and plan lifecycle. Future validation: archive error, panic, malformed stage and executing stage all produce an explicit non-success summary. Runtime owner separately covers timestamp projection.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-05"></a>**CON-A-05 — MAJOR: human-readable branch output is parsed as a machine protocol**<br><br>Lenses: DRY, nonidiomatic code, correctness. Git's linked-worktree `+` marker survives listing and merged-branch parsing. `parse_branch_list` also splits padded `branch -v` lines at literal spaces, giving an empty hash for its own `* main       abc1234` fixture. Evidence: `loom/src/git/branch/operations.rs:91-99`, `info.rs:23-37,53-66`, `ancestry.rs:166-172`, `cleanup.rs:12-27`. Terminal/Git reviewer independently traced the same consumers. Smallest fix: share structured `for-each-ref` field output and compare fully qualified refs for ancestry; avoid display markers/padding entirely. Preserve branch validation and checked Git exit handling. Future validation: current and linked branches, varied name widths, empty subjects and exact hashes; assertions must inspect fields, not just names.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-07"></a>**CON-A-07 — MAJOR: confined criteria ignore the warm caches exposed to them**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, correctness, unnecessary repeated work. Every criterion gets new `UV_CACHE_DIR`, `BUN_INSTALL_CACHE_DIR` and `XDG_CACHE_HOME` under fresh scratch, while warm host uv/Bun caches are mounted at their old locations. `populate` seeds only Cargo. Offline dependency resolution can fail despite a populated host cache, and XDG builds repeatedly start cold. Evidence: `loom/src/verify/criteria/confine_scratch.rs:81-105`; `confine_host.rs:149-155`. Verification reviewer confirmed cache tests read a sentinel through `HOME`, and the real uv fixture has no dependencies: neither tests the configured lookup. Smallest fix: define tool-specific cache lookup/seeding ownership with stable isolated per-tree/plan state or safe read-only host reuse. Preserve denied criterion network, namespace isolation and operator-cache protection; do not expose every host cache writable. Consumers: freeze, check, completion, provision. Future validation: offline uv/Bun dependency present only in warm host cache, repeated command hits, cache miss and concurrent stages.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-08"></a>**CON-A-08 — MAJOR: public sandbox validators are a disconnected policy replica**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, dead/excessive code. `detect_path_escape`, `validate_paths` and `is_legitimate_work_access` are publicly reexported but production never calls them; their tests read like live boundary guarantees. Evidence: `loom/src/sandbox/config.rs:242-358,715-811`, `sandbox/mod.rs:16-17`; repository caller search finds only self/tests. Smallest fix: delete the unused policy surface and misleading tests, or deliberately connect a single fail-fast validator to the existing point-of-use filter if rejecting currently accepted plans is required. Preserve mandatory deny/path filtering. Future validation must exercise actual plan emission, not only helper output.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-11"></a>**CON-A-11 — MAJOR: native file guards do not use the session's effective credential policy**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, DRY, correctness. Credential-path matching reads only project `settings.local.json`, while Loom supplies runtime policy in a capsule. Checkout-rooted sessions have no worktree-root restriction. Token checks additionally match only textual `.work`/`.loom/work` parents after canonicalization, so a state-root alias to a differently named directory loses that protection. Evidence: `loom-hooks/credential-guard.sh:285-309,325-338,530-565`; `worktree-file-guard.sh:344-379`; capsule policy in `native/session_settings/contents.rs`. Smallest fix: load trusted effective mandatory/session denies in the hook and compare secret paths against canonical `LOOM_WORK_DIR`, sharing the existing history-root logic. Preserve operator exemptions and never reintroduce `Read(...)` permission rules: they have a separate runtime failure mode. Future validation: knowledge/adjudication checkout sessions, aliased root, absent local settings, permitted neighboring file and all file-tool shapes. Hook/open races remain an accepted OS-boundary limitation.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-12"></a>**CON-A-12 — CRITICAL: host Git readers still discover agent-controlled repository configuration**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, DRY, correctness. Handoff and cleanup run host `git status` using a worktree's mutable `.git` pointer. A foreign repository's clean-filter config can execute in the host process. Disabling hooks/fsmonitor does not pin repository identity. Evidence: `loom/src/handoff/session_content.rs:72-75`; `git/cleanup/removal.rs:115-125`; hardened/pinned alternative `git/worktree/pinned.rs:107-112,156-162`. Existing concern's scratch-repository reproduction was not rerun here. Smallest fix: route every registered-worktree host reader through `WorktreeGit::pinned` (including `GIT_COMMON_DIR`), propagate registration/refusal failures, and separately define a pinned main-checkout identity. Consumers: monitor/handoff/cleanup/in-progress merge and verification observers. Preserve isolated Git environment and replace/graft/commitGraph protections. Future validation: planted foreign gitdir with executable filter positive control, registered normal worktree, and bad config refusal. Gitlink recursion remains unproven.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-13"></a>**CON-A-13 — MAJOR: knowledge protection guards a tool route instead of the persisted invariants**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, excessive enforcement code, invalid guidance. Every sandbox config grants knowledge writes, but the file hook refuses named edit tools and suggests only append-only `update`. Shell/interpreter writes remain equivalent capabilities, and no completion-time knowledge/index invariant check was found. Evidence: `loom/src/sandbox/config.rs:134-151`; `loom-hooks/worktree-file-guard.sh:336-348`; targeted `verify/` and `commands/stage/` search; `plan/schema/validation.rs:1155-1189`. Smallest fix: validate knowledge/index freshness at the trusted completion owner whenever the diff touches it, then permit legitimate owner editing with sync; narrow other stage grants only after tracing actual write consumers. Name replace/delete/update in guidance and remove the misleading inside-worktree remedy. No new Bash-text matcher. Future validation: direct write with stale index/doubled heading fails; valid CLI/edit+sync succeeds.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-14"></a>**CON-A-14 — MAJOR: generated work instructions lose criterion semantics and contradict exit policy**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, invalid/verbose guidance. Acceptance prints only `criterion.command()`, so expected exit 1 appears as an ordinary success check. The same stage prefix says commits occur only after all work and separately says commit before blocking/disputing. Integration override cites a nonexistent complete-suite Rule 5, and fix-round wording suggests one check per criterion. Evidence: `loom/src/orchestrator/signals/format/sections.rs:484-489`; `helpers.rs:143,221`; `cache.rs:115-123`; `cache/blocks.rs:90,137`. The stale cross-reference and reviewer-handoff wording are evidence contributions to INT-E14/E16, not additional CON-A-14 defects; its distinct root is the generated criterion/exit semantics. Smallest fix: one semantic formatter emits expected exit behavior; one exit/commit policy states the filing exception; use repeated criterion flags in one run and pass review summaries/log pointers explicitly. Consumers: standard/integration/knowledge stage templates and pinned hook doctrine. Future validation: negative criterion rendering and byte-identical shared doctrine with explicit exception, not broad snapshots that repeat wrong text.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-15"></a>**CON-A-15 — MINOR: context telemetry reports unreadable deliveries as an ordinary miss**<br><br>Lenses: correctness, observability. `load_deliveries(...).ok()` collapses storage/parsing failure into “no delivery record for this session,” hiding the condition the telemetry is meant to expose. Evidence: `loom/src/orchestrator/core/stage_telemetry.rs:23-43`. Smallest fix: match error/missing/delivered separately with bounded safe diagnostic text. Consumers: context availability measurements. Future validation: unreadable/corrupt ledger versus absent ledger produce distinct reasons; telemetry remains best effort.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-16"></a>**CON-A-16 — MINOR: stage identifiers and model display normalization have competing owners**<br><br>Lenses: DRY, correctness. Memory's validator accepts a lone `.` and arbitrary non-separator characters, whereas status rejects `.`, emptiness and non-ASCII-safe components. Model display independently strips the same prefix/date in status and subagent tables. Evidence: `loom/src/commands/memory/handlers/work_dir.rs:82-87`; `status/data/sanitize.rs:146-153`; `status/data/execution_models.rs:157-166`; `subagents/table.rs:110-124`. Smallest fix: share domain ID validation at persistence boundaries and a small model-display helper in a neutral owner; retain tighter validation instead of weakening status. No path-escape claim follows from accepting dot alone. Future validation: a common ID table and prefix/date/Unicode display cases shared by consumers.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-17"></a>**CON-A-17 — MINOR: failed request output leaves a ticket that cannot be relayed**<br><br>Lenses: correctness, lifecycle ownership. Request state is written before fallible `requested` output; on closed stdout the caller never reaches `TicketSink::print_line`. A retry follows that saved request until the 120-second grace expires. Evidence: `loom/src/host_verify/wait.rs:31,122-168,192-223`; `commands/verify_relay.rs:127`; `commands/stage/complete_relay.rs:109`. Smallest fix: make ticket publication/state ownership explicit and retire only this request's unsubmitted state on output failure; preserve a successfully submitted request and concurrent ownership safeguards in VER-08. Future validation: failing writer before ticket emission, successful emission followed by unrelated failure, concurrent new owner.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-18"></a>**CON-A-18 — MAJOR: merge commit path authorization trusts any ancestor's tree**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, correctness. A Merge scope accepts `MERGE_HEAD` anywhere in target ancestry, then exempts built-in protected/gitlink paths equal to that historical tree. Example: the stage forks at B; target commit T1 adds a gitlink and T2 removes it; the resolver merges T1 and stages its gitlink. T1 is an ancestor of current target T2, so the commit exemption accepts the removed gitlink when no plan `deny_write` glob covers it. This is commit authorization only; later landing gates still apply. Evidence: `loom/src/git/stage_commit/checks.rs:95-138,187-230`. The production caller always supplies the target (`core/inbox_drain/commit.rs:165-195,85-87`), so omitting `merging_into` is not a demonstrated production route. Plan-denied paths have a separate real-merge-result guard (`checks/guarded.rs:33-67`); the example excludes them. Smallest fix: record the authorized target snapshot and tie merge-side exemptions to that snapshot, while allowing a genuinely authorized snapshot to remain valid if the target advances. Do not reject every older target tip or weaken the guarded-path merge-result checks. Future validation: target adds then removes an uncovered gitlink, resolver selects the historical addition, fresh legitimate merge, and authorized target movement. No execution check was run.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-19"></a>**CON-A-19 — MINOR: pressure record placement and preview repeat incompatible document rules**<br><br>Lenses: DRY, invalid output. Record insertion chooses the last unfenced END marker and only column-zero spaced headings, while plan parsing uses a different metadata/ATX model. Preview prints the record after deleting the final report; actual completion writes it first. Evidence: `loom/src/pressure/record.rs:98-125`; `plan/parser/extraction.rs`; `commands/pressure/mod.rs:81-86,104`; `pressure/mod.rs:136-161`; preview test `commands/pressure/tests.rs:129`. Smallest fix: reuse one parsed document boundary/heading model and one ordered pipeline description for execution and preview. Preserve fence exclusion and record-write failure priority; coordinate VER-16's replacement transaction. Future validation: multiple marker-like prose lines, indented ATX, existing record section, and exact write-before-delete ordering.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-20"></a>**CON-A-20 — MINOR: the overview viewer has no cleanup owner**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, lifecycle. Viewer tmux servers deliberately keep `exit-empty off`; detach leaves them alive and generic socket cleanup deliberately excludes their deterministic repo-owned name. Evidence: `loom/src/commands/attach/overview.rs:122-150,254-280`; `orchestrator/terminal/tmux/socket.rs:68-74`; `viewer.rs:44-67`. Smallest fix: a repository-specific viewer retirement path used by explicit clean/init, with verified ownership, instead of expanding generic same-user socket deletion. Preserve overview reconnection while a run is active. Future validation: detach then clean retires only this repo viewer, leaving another repo and unrelated user server intact.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-22"></a>**CON-A-22 — MAJOR: memory test helpers mutate cwd visible to unrelated tests**<br><br>Lenses: nonidiomatic testing, correctness. Memory tests change process-global cwd under `#[serial]`, which does not exclude unannotated tests. Common fixtures declare the temporary repository after the restoration guard, so it can be deleted before cwd is restored. Evidence: `loom/src/commands/memory/handlers/tests.rs:25-61,78-80`; `tests_resolve_pending.rs:26-71`; `tests_record_shape.rs:14-16`. Smallest fix: pass explicit roots to command logic or run cwd-dependent CLI tests in subprocesses; do not mark every unrelated test serial. If a small temporary guard remains, restore cwd before deleting its tree. Future validation: parallel relative-path consumer cannot observe the fixture cwd; failure paths restore ownership. No tests run.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-23"></a>**CON-A-23 — MINOR: concurrent daemon-absence readers exclude each other**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, nonidiomatic locking. `inspect_lock` proves absence by acquiring `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB` and returns the still-locked descriptor. Another reader can observe that transient probe as a held daemon lock and report unreachable. Evidence: `loom/src/fs/daemon_state.rs:23-52`; the daemon takes exclusive ownership in `acquire_lock`. Smallest fix: use shared nonblocking probe locks while retaining the descriptor for the proof interval; verify every caller and daemon-start interleaving. Preserve regular-file/no-follow checks and exclusive real daemon ownership. Future validation: overlapping absence probes both succeed, actual daemon excludes both, daemon startup cannot overlap the guarded proof.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-24"></a>**CON-A-24 — MAJOR: printed manual launch commands do not escape paths**<br><br>Lenses: correctness, invalid operational guidance. A checkout/worktree/signal path containing spaces breaks the printed command; shell metacharacters in those filesystem names execute when pasted. Evidence: `loom/src/orchestrator/core/manual_start.rs:10-53` interpolates `display()` into `cd`, env assignments and a double-quoted prompt. Smallest fix: shell-escape every argument and quote the entire prompt as one argument using the existing escaping owner. Preserve the explicit no-confinement notice and validated identifiers. Future validation: spaces, quotes, dollars, backticks and newlines survive as literal argv without executing marker commands.<br><br>

## Additional data and verification concerns

<a id="con-b-01"></a>**CON-B-01 — CRITICAL: restart recovery can kill an unrelated recycled process group**<br><br>Trigger: a daemon restarts after a recorded running host job has disappeared and one of its recorded criterion PGIDs has been reused by an unrelated process-group leader. `host_verify/signals.rs:72–94` filters cancelled records by `live_child`, but includes all interrupted records. `kill_led_groups` at 143–159 checks only that the recorded PID currently leads a group, then sends SIGKILL. The check proves group leadership, not job identity. This can terminate unrelated operator services. <br><br> Lens: lifecycle ownership and invalid safety comments. Smallest sufficient repair: persist and verify process start identity for every criterion group, with boot identity where appropriate; use an identity-bound signalling handle on supported platforms. Do not merely remove group cleanup: surviving criterion grandchildren must still be retired. Update restart tests that currently allow a stranger host-child PID. Future validation: stale job with recycled group survives; genuine orphan criterion dies. Static proof; no signals sent. <br><br> Provenance: `verification-v2-followups#Dispute Accept and Host-Job Recovery Gaps`; `source-graph-review-backlog#kill_led_groups On Restart-Interrupted Records`. Verification/runtime reviewers confirm no existing finding owns this root cause.<br><br>
<a id="con-b-02"></a>**CON-B-02 — MAJOR: transient refusal I/O becomes permanent non-retryable rejection**<br><br>`stage_service/dispute_kinds.rs:197–200` converts every `confirm` error to `Response::Error`. The confirmation path calls `load_session_exact` and `load_freeze` (`333–369`), which can fail on I/O. `file_freeze_refusal:100–120` maps the response to `Declined`; `orchestrator/core/event_handler/freeze_refusal.rs:299–315` persists that decline so it is never filed again. A temporary read error therefore strands an otherwise admissible freeze refusal until another lifecycle intervention. <br><br> Lens: typed boundaries and error classification. Return a typed admission outcome separately from infrastructure `Result` errors, preserving retry on I/O and permanent rejection on genuine admission failures. Keep request-key idempotency and budget checks. Future validation: fail the confirmation read once, retry successfully with one dispute/budget spend; permanent admission rejection remains declined. <br><br> Provenance: `contract-autonomy-followups#Freeze-Refusal Filing Gaps`. Static trace; no fault injection executed.<br><br>
<a id="con-b-03"></a>**CON-B-03 — MAJOR: approval does not replenish the exhausted freeze-refusal budget**<br><br>`commands/stage/human_review.rs:101–122` closes old disputes and resets fix, stall, merge-handback and contract-attempt state, but `skip_retry.rs:347–356` resets only the separate contract-attempt store. `models/stage/dispute_budgets.rs:41,65,100` retains `stage.tally.freeze_refusal_disputes`. After refusal-budget escalation, human approval queues a writer whose first refusal encounters the same exhausted tally and escalates again. <br><br> Lens: split ownership of retry policy. Reset the freeze-refusal tally in the locked approval update when granting a fresh contract phase; preserve unrelated dispute tallies unless the approval policy expressly resets them. Future validation: exhaust refusal budget, approve, then file one refusal successfully with the intended new budget. Static proof; no state mutation performed.<br><br>
<a id="con-b-04"></a>**CON-B-04 — MAJOR: Git calls inherit repository-redirection variables**<br><br>`git/runner.rs:71–92` configures argv, locale, replacement/graft suppression and cwd, but retains inherited `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE` and related repository selectors. A Loom process launched with one of these variables can inspect or modify a different repository/index despite an explicit `repo_root`. Callers include context evidence, merge and status. <br><br> Lens: shared boundary ownership. Remove repository-selection variables centrally before applying explicitly authorized per-call overrides; retain supported configuration/auth variables deliberately. Future validation: point ambient selectors at a second scratch repository and prove ordinary reads/writes target the explicit root; explicit overrides still work. Static configuration trace; no Git mutation executed.<br><br>
<a id="con-b-05"></a>**CON-B-05 — MAJOR: the bounded process runner has unbounded output allocation**<br><br>`process/mod.rs:188–199` spawns readers using `read_to_end` into growable vectors. `collect_output:204–224` bounds waiting time only. A child emitting large output within its timeout can exhaust daemon memory before downstream callers reject the result's size. The existing shared runner is the correct owner of the bound. <br><br> Lens: boundary contracts and DRY. Add explicit output byte limits per stream or a caller-specified bounded capture policy; keep draining or terminate the owned child on overflow so a full pipe cannot deadlock it. Preserve deadlines and process-group retirement. Future validation: sustained output is bounded, overflow is distinguishable from timeout, and descendants/pipes close. Static proof; no stress process run.<br><br>
<a id="con-b-06"></a>**CON-B-06 — MAJOR: timeout retries lose daemon frame position**<br><br>`commands/status/web/broadcast.rs:199–236` sets a 50 ms socket timeout and ignores timeout errors before calling `read_message` again on the same stream. `daemon/wire.rs:182–210` uses fresh local buffers with `read_exact` for each header and body. A timeout after consuming a partial frame discards the consumed position; the next read treats remaining JSON bytes as a new length, causing protocol errors and reconnects. The TUI shares the framing implementation and needs the same repair. <br><br> Lens: shared stream-state ownership. Preserve partial header/body state across timeouts in one framed reader, or reconnect on any timeout that consumed data. Keep maximum frame validation and shutdown responsiveness. Future validation: drip a header and body across successive timeout boundaries, then send a second frame; both decode once in order. Static trace, no socket reproduction. <br><br> <a id="int-e36"></a>**Reconciled evidence from INT-E36: INT-E36 — CRITICAL: live TUI discards partially received frames on a read timeout.** <br><br> **Lenses:** protocol ownership, invalid comments/tests. `ui/tui/app.rs:194–216` treats every WouldBlock/TimedOut as idle even though `daemon/wire.rs:189–213` uses stateless read_exact for prefix and body. If a valid frame arrives in pieces separated by more than50ms, consumed bytes are lost and the next call interprets remaining body bytes as a new length. An OrchestrationComplete frame split this way followed by daemon EOF is never handled; the TUI exits as “Daemon exited” without its completion summary. Sustained slow status delivery repeatedly discards/reconnects. The comment promises recovery on the next read, but a timeout can also split the binary prefix, and `app_tests.rs:24–32` only classifies a manually constructed error; it never drives a fragmented stream. <br><br> **Repair/consumers:** give the client an incremental frame decoder retaining prefix/body offsets across idle polls, or move whole-frame decoding to a bounded reader task that forwards complete messages. Keep event responsiveness, max response size and whole-frame ownership. Distinguish zero-byte idle from incomplete-frame lifetime and impose an overall frame deadline. Coordinate the shared wire contract with RT-27 concurrent writers and RT-28 deadlines. Acceptance: split both prefix and body across multiple idle intervals, then complete them; exactly one response arrives, including completion immediately before EOF. Incomplete malicious frames still time out without unbounded allocation. <br><br> Deduplication: INT-E36 is retained as consumer evidence for canonical CON-B-06 (shared frame decoder); the canonical finding includes split completion/EOF and test-contract evidence here.<br><br>
<a id="con-b-07"></a>**CON-B-07 — MAJOR: merge-conflict stages disappear from the conflict count**<br><br>`git/merge/status.rs:163–168` skips every non-Completed stage before `check_merge_state`; therefore a stage currently in `MergeConflict` never enters `report.conflicts`. Status collection calls this report (`commands/status/data/collector.rs:345`); TUI/compact headers and browser notification policy consume its conflicts array. The UI can show a conflict stage beside zero counted conflicts. <br><br> Lens: model projection ownership. Classify explicit conflict status before the Completed-only Git checks, preserving the current completed-stage merge verification. Future validation: mixed Completed/MergeConflict/Executing inputs yield the conflict id once and retain completed-stage warnings. Static source trace.<br><br>
<a id="con-b-09"></a>**CON-B-09 — MAJOR: host-job retry ownership is lost when current job advances**<br><br>`host_verify/submit.rs:64–68` discards terminal current records and queues a replacement. `host_verify/apply.rs:101–123` recovers only the current record after a restart. An earlier terminal job whose application failed is retained solely in process-wide `FAILED_APPLIES` (`orchestrator/core/host_jobs.rs:28–40,62–65`); replacing current and then crashing loses its retry. The comment claiming startup recovers the list is stronger than the scan. <br><br> Lens: lifecycle ownership and process-global state. Keep unapplied terminal job identifiers in durable per-runner state, or scan retained terminal job records rather than only current. Ensure pruning does not delete unapplied jobs. Move pending-apply ownership next to the runner; a line-count cap does not justify a hidden static registry. Preserve idempotent markers, stage/session binding and capped retries. Future validation: fail apply once, submit another job, restart, recover exactly the unapplied job and reject obsolete bindings safely. <br><br> Related residual: `host_jobs.rs:212–234` gives up by writing only an applied-error marker. For a Freeze job it does not publish a refusal record, so automatic refusal adjudication has nothing to file. On exhausted retries, persist a terminal infrastructure outcome consumable by the contract writer/adjudicator, with an explicit fallback when storage itself is unavailable. Future validation should check the refusal channel, not just the marker. <br><br> Provenance: `source-graph-review-backlog#FAILED_APPLIES Is Process-Wide State`, `#Startup Scan Covers Current Records Only`, `#Give-Up Leaves Freeze Refusals Unfiled`.<br><br>
<a id="con-b-10"></a>**CON-B-10 — MAJOR: forwarding transcript classification uses a narrower argv grammar than receipt formation**<br><br>`models/forward_receipt/transcript/argv.rs:19–39` accepts exactly eight words, while `forward_argv_in:50–85` accepts eight, ten and twelve, including authorized unit/invocation identities. `transcript.rs:164` still calls the narrow parser; `commands/subagents/forward_jobs_transcript.rs:28–34,58–75` builds classification only from those parsed invocations. A valid unit-bound forward therefore forms a receipt but disappears from the transcript-derived forwarding-use index. <br><br> Lens: duplicated policy and DRY. Share the wrapper argv parser and represent optional identifiers structurally. Let callers apply documented restrictions explicitly, instead of maintaining incompatible recognition grammars. Keep exact wrapper validation and shell-syntax refusal; widening must not admit chained or forged commands. Future validation: identical eight/ten/twelve-word calls are recognized by both receipt and transcript consumers; reordered, extra, invalid identity and compound commands remain rejected. <br><br> Provenance: `token-accounting-and-proof-defects#Delivery-Proof and Quota-Continuity Defects`.<br><br>
<a id="con-b-11"></a>**CON-B-11 — MAJOR: knowledge excerpts bypass the shared terminal sanitizer**<br><br>`context/render.rs:14–20,107–110` fences the excerpt but writes its bytes unchanged. Human-readable knowledge context renders these items (`commands/knowledge/context.rs`, shared item renderer). Markdown backtick fences do not neutralize ANSI OSC/CSI sequences or bidi controls in terminal output. Source windows already use `untrusted::terminal_safe`; knowledge excerpts do not. <br><br> Lens: shared boundary ownership and inaccurate “escape-proof” comment. Apply the existing layout-preserving sanitizer before choosing the excerpt fence, and preserve original content identity separately from rendered text. Future validation: ESC/OSC and bidi controls in a knowledge body cannot reach human stdout; normal tabs/newlines, source references and fence sizing remain correct. Static proof, no terminal escape executed. <br><br> Provenance: `source-graph-review-backlog#Lease, Census, Window and Hardening`. The same bullet's inline-code/source-window claims are fixed and have separate knowledge corrections.<br><br>
<a id="con-b-12"></a>**CON-B-12 — MAJOR: subcommand lints interpret stage paths in the wrong coordinate system**<br><br>`plan/schema/validation/v2_lints/mod.rs:181–187` compares `files` and `artifacts` strings directly with a repository-relative directory; it never joins `stage.working_dir`. The unknown-Loom-subcommand lint uses that result to decide whether a stage can introduce the subcommand. With `working_dir: loom` and `files: [src/cli/**]`, a legitimate new CLI command receives a hard error because the comparison expects `loom/src/cli`. <br><br> Lens: shared path semantics. Normalize each owned path against working_dir before checking its literal glob prefix; preserve glob support and repository containment. Update every consumer of `stage_touches_dir`, rather than patching one lint. Future validation: equivalent repository-relative/root-stage and working-dir-relative/nested-stage plans produce the same result; sibling-directory ownership still fails. <br><br> Provenance: `verification-v2-followups#The Subcommand Lint Ignores working_dir`.<br><br>
<a id="con-b-13"></a>**CON-B-13 — MAJOR: prompt truncation parses headings inside quoted evidence**<br><br>`orchestrator/adjudication/prompt/truncate.rs:57–95` finds a heading by unrestricted substring, then treats the next two occurrences of three backticks as the fence. `freeze_refusal_evidence.rs:323–333` deliberately produces a fence longer than the longest embedded backtick run. When evidence exceeds the prompt budget, a quoted copy of a heading in an earlier section can select the wrong body; an embedded three-backtick run inside a valid longer fence can stop trimming early. The final hard cap then discards tail evidence contrary to the documented priority. Contract prompts also use fixed fences (`prompt/contract.rs:203–205,272–281`). <br><br> Lens: shared parsing and typed evidence ownership. Preserve evidence sections as structured bounded fields until final rendering, or parse line-anchored headings outside fences and match complete fence delimiters. Share safe fence generation while keeping instructions immutable. Future validation: an over-budget prompt with a quoted candidate heading, embedded three-backtick line, and four-backtick outer fence trims only its intended section and preserves the remaining evidence order. Static proof; no execution. Distinct from RT-24 knowledge section scanners; verification reviewer confirmed no existing owner.<br><br>
<a id="con-b-14"></a>**CON-B-14 — MAJOR: session tracking keys alias different stage/type pairs**<br><br>`models/session/methods.rs:88–97` maps `(auth, Contract)` and `(contract-auth, Stage)` to the same `loom-contract-auth` key; analogous collisions affect merge, knowledge, adjudication and base-conflict prefixes. `validation.rs:19–22,72` reserves device names but permits those stage IDs. `orchestrator/orphan_candidates.rs:29–35` identifies an orphan by tracking-key prefix and a session-shaped suffix. Its `session-` check rejects ordinary stage-prefix mis-splits, but cannot distinguish these exactly equal keys. `session_registry.rs:205–243,252–270` iterates all session kinds and chooses the first stage's candidate, so a missing session record after restart can be adopted under the wrong stage/type, despite a valid process identity. <br><br> Lens: identity representation and lifecycle ownership. Use an unambiguous encoded tuple in new tracking keys and pid metadata, retaining explicit migration for existing pid files; fail closed on ambiguous legacy ownership. Do not simply change prefix matching or weaken process checks. Future validation: two executing stages `auth` and `contract-auth`, one orphan contract pid file, and reversed stage ordering always preserve the actual owner or refuse ambiguous adoption. Static source trace; no process or state mutation.<br><br>
<a id="con-b-15"></a>**CON-B-15 — MAJOR: nested JavaScript functions inherit the outer class receiver**<br><br>`context/extract/treesitter/binding.rs:134–150` walks from the caller through function definitions until it encounters a type, returning that type's scope. It does not stop at an ordinary nested JavaScript/TypeScript function, which has its own `this`. Thus `class C { m() { function f() { this.x(); } } x() {} }` can bind `f`'s call to `C::x` at Receiver confidence even though calling `f()` has no such receiver. The binding at `binding.rs:118–127` bypasses later ambiguous resolution once exactly one member matches. <br><br> Lens: language policy at shared binding boundary. Preserve a dialect-aware distinction between lexical receiver inheritance (arrow functions) and functions that establish a new receiver; stop receiver ownership at the latter. Avoid weakening Rust/C++ implementation ownership. Future validation: normal nested function is unbound, an arrow remains bound to the enclosing class, and explicit inner classes retain their own receiver. Source reviewer context confirms this gap and no earlier CTX ownership. Static proof; no extractor test executed.<br><br>

## Additional scoped notes

**Open specialist trace — provision option overrides**<br><br>`schema/validation/v2_fields/provision_installs.rs:154-160` checks only whether a required flag/value occurs anywhere in argv. It does not reject conflicting repeated options, a later override, or option-looking operands after `--`. `install_rule:139-149` also chooses the first non-dash word without consuming global-option values. Runtime review confirmed `orchestrator/provision.rs:111-121` executes the supplied command verbatim through `run_provision_with_timeout`, with no option normalization. The schema can therefore certify a command containing both `--backend=copyfile` and `--backend=hardlink`, or both `--config=/dev/null` and another config path. Whether a particular installed runner accepts the conflict and which value wins requires runner-specific confirmation; this audit has not executed package managers. Before treating hardening validation as a guarantee, reject contradictory/ambiguous forms or parse their effective options using a bounded explicit subset. Preserve accepted hardened forms and cover each supported package manager's option terminator/override behavior. This remains a flagged uncertainty, not a demonstrated host-code-execution claim.<br><br>
**Additional comment corrections**<br><br>• `git/merge/in_progress.rs:187`: `unmerged_paths` claims Git failure yields an empty vector, but its checked command propagates failure. Describe the actual `Result` contract. <br> • `git/branch/ancestry.rs:87`: the commits-ahead comment says either missing ref yields zero, while a missing base with an existing branch is an error, including in the tests. Preserve that distinction. <br> • `git/repository.rs:1`: the module says bootstrap does not stage or commit user files, but `create_bootstrap_commit` explicitly stages and commits an existing user `README.md`; the inline bootstrap test requires this. State the precise README-only contract instead of the broader false guarantee. <br> • `git/merge/tree.rs:33`: the blanket `MergeBlock` claim that nothing in the checkout changed contradicts its `StashNotRestored` variant. Likewise, pending-commit commentary should distinguish preflight blocks from a raced `update-ref` refusal after the commit has been written. Preserve the substantive recovery constraints and remove unconditional claims. <br><br> These corrections cover invalid comments and excessive explanatory surface alongside the ownership and duplicated-policy findings above. Test readability and useful boundary comments should be retained.<br><br>
**Additional traced limitations**<br><br>• `native/launch/host.rs:writable_roots` silently defaults failed plan/stage reads. The resulting host facts may omit writable roots used to reject executable paths. This merits a follow-up that carries read errors and resolves one coherent plan snapshot; actual exposure depends on the later capsule denies, so this report does not claim a demonstrated escape from this omission alone. <br> • The raw worktree listing parser uses newline records without decoding quoted paths; it should use Git's NUL-delimited listing for arbitrary filesystem paths. Its output feeds registration and checked-out-target detection. This reinforces RBR-09's requirement to preserve directories when registration is uncertain; exact unusual-path behavior needs a focused real-Git regression, not another hand-written ordinary-path fixture.<br><br>

## Accepted architectural risks and policy decisions

<a id="con-a-09"></a>**CON-A-09 — optional operator-launch policy and notice decision**<br><br>Lenses: architecture, policy clarity. Bootstrap explicitly refuses execution inside a stage and starts an operator-owned interactive Claude session. Its auto-mode argv has no Loom capsule or manual-mode-style confinement notice. Ambient operator settings may still sandbox it; this is not a demonstrated stage-confinement escape. Evidence: `loom/src/commands/knowledge/bootstrap/mod.rs:69-70,133-139,277-304,330-333`; `prompt.rs:13-19,54-68` disables named edit tools and supplies permission/model/tool/prompt arguments without a capsule. Optional decision: share an appropriate knowledge-launch confinement policy or explicitly describe inheritance of operator settings. Do not count this as an established MAJOR defect absent an explicit requirement that operator bootstrap carry stage confinement. Preserve operator authorization and the distinct bootstrap scope.<br><br>
<a id="con-a-10"></a>**CON-A-10 — accepted architectural risk requiring an owner decision**<br><br>Lenses: architecture. Stage sessions can write shared extracted package sources and, for Codex lanes, non-Loom rules/skills/plugins/packages under the operator's Codex directory. Later operator tools/builds trust that state outside the original stage lifetime. Evidence: `loom/src/sandbox/package_caches.rs:40-82`; `codex.rs:119-124`; `sandbox/control_surfaces.rs:61-96`. Current denies DO protect Codex AGENTS, prompts, catalog and Loom-installed skills; the old broader allegation is corrected. This is an explicitly accepted trust/performance tradeoff, not a newly reproduced escape. Owner decision required before implementation: namespace mutable cache/control state per plan/session and mount only safe host seeds read-only. Preserve package installation and credential refresh requirements. Future validation: poisoning canary must stay inside isolated state while operator source/cache remains unchanged; test all supported ecosystems. Do not weaken isolation to solve CON-A-07.<br><br>

## Reconciliation and implementation use

Concern ledgers preserve the original file/heading, claim, disposition, source evidence and related finding ID. Confirmed duplicates strengthen the existing finding rather than create another repair. Fixed/stale claims are corrected in curated knowledge; unresolved claims retain the missing proof or experiment and must not be treated as established defects. Planner concerns remain excluded. Read the relevant ledger and source before turning a concern into implementation work.

For each repair, use the finding's affected-consumer list as its scope, retain the stated safeguards, and add the focused regression that distinguishes the wrong behavior. Keep unrelated cleanups separate. Cross-package changes above require coordination at the named shared owner. Review completeness describes inspected source and reconciled records; it does not assert the absence of undiscovered defects or substitute for implementation testing.
